To better quantify this observation, we compare how sparse the input images can be reconstructed from (1) random features, (2) clean features, and (3) robust features. For each of the tasks, we use Lasso to reconstruct the 100 images, and sweep over all possible weights of the  $\ell_1$  regularizer (which controls how sparse the reconstruction is). The results are presented in the first row of Figure 12. As one can see, using clean features one can also sparsely reconstruct

the input, but using robust features the reconstruction can be even sparser. This, to some extent, supports our theory that robust features are more "pure" than clean features.

Perhaps more importantly, our theory suggests that for clean-trained models, adversarial perturbations (we refer to as clean deltas) have "dense mixtures"; while for robust-trained models, adversarial perturbations (we refer to as robust deltas) are "more pure." This was visually illustrated in Figure 7. Now, to better quantify this observation, we compare how sparse clean deltas and robust deltas can be reconstructed from robust features. See the second row of Figure 12. $^{19}$  From this experiment, we confirm that in practice, adversarial perturbations on robust models are more "pure" and closer to real input images.

Remark 8.2. We point out when comparing how sparse clean deltas and robust deltas can be reconstructed from robust features, we did not cheat. For instance, in principle clean deltas may not lie in the span of robust features and if so, it cannot be (sparsely) reconstructed from them. In our experiments (namely, the second row of Figure 12), we noticed that clean deltas almost lies in the span of robust features (with regression error  $< 0.00005$  for AlexNet and  $< 10^{-9}$  for ResNet).

# 8.4 Comparing Different Attackers

We also demonstrate in Figure 13 that feature purification occurs against several different attackers.

Figure 13: Visualization of the 27-th convolutional layer of ResNet-34 against different attackers.  
![](images/e9f83b435d0255546d92ed6948cec7c3d53c12f38df3a961a663d861763ba37e.jpg)  
Take-away message: feature purification happens against different attackers, and stronger attacker gives stronger effect of feature purification.

# 8.5 Feature Purification is a Low-Rank Update

Recall from Theorem 5.2 and illustrated in Section 6.2 that the non-robustness of neurons in a clean-trained model, only comes from a common dense mixture direction  $\mathbf{M}w^{\star}$ . This suggests, during the robust (e.g., adversarial) training, we do not need to re-train all the parameters; it suffices to search only for a hidden mixture direction. We use experiments to support this finding.

Before we do so, please note we derived this "common dense direction" theory using a two-layered, binary classification setting. When there are multiple classes, one would expect there to be more dense mixture directions. In such a case, "low-rank update" is a more suitable choice.[20]

![](images/726a4b5611f3c12b65d2d6e550c73d7c741dce13065476d7d1cefaf5bffcb29b.jpg)

![](images/4da1abab98d1ec5ef2a9cc53c73ee18401f1e819c2ec52f551d5f9b19f0c4c4b.jpg)

![](images/be7a29a4b2bdc70f86384e04076424f1918e0700b3a0c2f8bc0e25dc687d6547.jpg)  
Figure 14: Perform low-rank update on a clean-trained model, and only (adversarily) train the low-rank part of the parameters, can effectively recover the robustness of a fully adversarily-trained model.

![](images/e944d11abcea4780d60ae29d23d257e877b9bbc6025a522ef8c55f2e5094d770.jpg)

Now on CIFAR-10, we compare clean training, (traditional) adversarial training, as well as first conducting clean training and then performing an adversarially trained, low-rank update on all the convolutional parameters. We use the (pre-activation) ResNet-28 architecture as well as its widened versions ResNet-28-3/5/10 [110]. Recall ResNet-28- $x$  has three groups of basic convolutional layers, each of  $16x$ ,  $32x$  and  $64x$  channels respectively.

- We use "rank  $r$ " to denote a rank- $r$  update to all of the convolutional parameters.  
- We use "rank  $r^*$ " to denote rank- $r$ ,  $2r$ ,  $4r$  updates respectively to the convolutional parameters in the three groups.

We present our findings in Figure 14. For example, for the ResNet-28-10 model:

- Using the clean-trained model weights alone, the robust accuracies are zeros.  
- Now, on top of such clean-trained weights, barely training a rank-1 (or rank  $1^*$ ) update for each convolutional matrix, or equivalently only about  $\frac{1}{160}$  (less than 1 percent) of the parameters, one can already recover more than  $80\%$  of the maximum robust accuracy.

- Similarly, in the "rank  $8^{*}$ " case we train only about  $5\% = \frac{8}{160}$  of the parameters, one can recover more than  $90\%$  of the maximum robust accuracy.

We believe this preliminary experiment can be useful in supporting our theory, and may be of independent interests for other applications. (Indeed, in a follow-up work we found an application of such low-rank update to language model fine-tuning [48].)

# APPENDIX: COMPLETE PROOFS

We give a quick overview of the structure of our appendix sections.

In Section A, we warm up the readers by calculating the gradient of the objective, and demonstrating that polynomially many samples are sufficient for the training.

In Section B, we formally introduce  $\mathcal{S}_{j,pot}^{(t)}$ , the set of "potentially lucky neurons" and  $\mathcal{S}_{j,sure}^{(t)}$ , the set of "surely lucky neurons" at iteration  $t$ . In particular, we shall emphasize on how those notions evolve as  $t$  increases.

In Section C, we formally prove how "lucky neurons" continue to be lucky, and more importantly, for every neuron  $i$  that is lucky in direction  $j$ , why it grows faster than other unlucky directions  $j'$ , and how much faster. Specifically, Theorem C.1 corresponds to the initial "lotterywinning" phase where the accuracy remains around  $50\%$ ; and Theorem C.2 corresponds to the later phase where large signals become even larger and eventually most neurons become "pure + dense mix" of the form (6.1). This is the most difficult section of this paper.

In Section D, we prove that why clean training gives good clean (testing) accuracy. It is based on the structural theorem given by Theorem C.2, and requires some non-trivial manipulations of probability theory results (such as introducing a high-probability, Bernstein form of the McDiarmid's inequality).

In Section E, we prove that why the model obtained from clean training is non-robust. It formally shows how the "dense mixtures" become accumulated step by step during clean training.

In Section F, we prove our theorems for both  $\ell_2$  and  $\ell_{\infty}$  adversarial training. In particular, in this section we demonstrate why practical perturbation algorithms, such as the fast gradient method (FGM), can help the (adversarial) training process "kill" those "dense mixtures."

In Section G, we prove lower bounds for the neural tangent kernel model given by two-layer networks.

In Section H, we give missing details of some probability theory lemmas.

# A Notations and Warmups

We find it perhaps a good exercise to do some simple calculations to warm up the readers with our notations, before going into the proofs.

Global Assumptions. Throughout the proof,

- We choose  $m = d^{1 + c_0}$  for a very small constant  $c_0 \in (0, 1)$ .

(One should think of  $c_0 = 0.0001$  for a simple reading. Our proof generalizers to larger  $m = \mathsf{poly}(d)$  since having more neurons does not hurt performance, but we ignore the analysis so as to provide the simplest notations.)

- We assume  $k < d^{(1 - c_0) / 2}$ .  
- We choose  $\lambda = \frac{\log\log\log d}{d}$  for simplicity.

(The purpose of  $\log \log \log d$  factor is to simplify notations, and it can be tightened to constant.)

- Whenever we write "for random  $x$ ", "for random  $z$ " or "for random  $\xi$ ", we mean that the come from the distributions introduced in Section 2 with  $x = \mathbf{M}z + \xi$ .

Network Gradient. In every iteration  $t$ , the weights of the neurons are  $w_1^{(t)},\ldots ,w_m^{(t)}\in \mathbb{R}^d$ . Recall the output of the neural network on input  $x\in \mathbb{R}^d$  is

$$
f _ {t} (w ^ {(t)}; x, \rho) = \sum_ {i = 1} ^ {m} \mathsf {R e L U} (\langle w _ {i} ^ {(t)}, x \rangle + \rho_ {i} - b ^ {(t)}) - \mathsf {R e L U} (- \langle w _ {i} ^ {(t)}, x \rangle + \rho_ {i} - b ^ {(t)}).
$$

Fact A.1. If we denote by  $\ell_t^\prime (w^{(t)};x,y,\rho)\stackrel {\mathrm{def}}{=}\frac{d}{ds} [\log (1 + e^s)]\mid_{s = -yf_t(w^{(t)};x,\rho)} = \frac{e^{-yf_t(w^{(t)};x,\rho)}}{1 + e^{-yf_t(w^{(t)};x,\rho)}}$  then

$$
\begin{array}{l} \nabla_ {w _ {i}} \mathbf {L o s s} _ {t} (w ^ {(t)}; x, y, \rho) = - y \ell_ {t} ^ {\prime} (w ^ {(t)}; x, y, \rho) \nabla_ {w _ {i}} f _ {t} (w ^ {(t)}; x, \rho) \\ = - y \ell_ {t} ^ {\prime} (w ^ {(t)}; x, y, \rho) \left(\mathbb {1} _ {\langle w _ {i} ^ {(t)}, x \rangle + \rho_ {i} \geq b ^ {(t)}} + \mathbb {1} _ {- \langle w _ {i} ^ {(t)}, x \rangle + \rho_ {i} \geq b ^ {(t)}}\right) \cdot x \\ \end{array}
$$

Let us also note that

$$
\nabla \mathbf {R e g} (w _ {i}) = (\| w _ {i} \| _ {2} + 1) \cdot w _ {i}
$$

Lemma A.2. Suppose  $\mathcal{Z} = \{x^{(1)},\ldots ,x^{(N)}\}$  are i.i.d. samples from  $\mathcal{D}$  and  $y^{(i)} = y(x^{(i)})$  , and suppose  $N\geq \mathsf{poly}(d)$  for some sufficiently large polynomial. Let  $f = f_{t}$  and suppose  $b^{(t)}\leq \mathsf{poly}(d)$  and  $\sigma_{\rho}\geq \frac{1}{\mathsf{poly}(d)}$  . Then, for every  $w_{1},\dots ,w_{N}$  that may depend on the randomness of  $\mathcal{Z}$  and satisfies  $\| w_i\| \leq \mathsf{poly}(d)$  , it satisfies

$$
\begin{array}{l} \left| \frac {1}{N} \sum_ {i \in [ N ]} \mathbb {E} _ {\rho} \left[ \mathbf {L o s s} \left(w; x ^ {(i)}, y ^ {(i)}, \rho\right) \right] - \underset {x \sim \mathcal {D}, y = y (x), \rho} {\mathbb {E}} \left[ \mathbf {L o s s} \left(w; x, y, \rho\right) \right] \right| \leq \frac {1}{\operatorname {p o l y} (d)} \\ \left\| \frac {1}{N} \sum_ {i \in [ N ]} \mathbb {E} _ {\rho} \left[ \nabla_ {w} \mathbf {L o s s} (w; x ^ {(i)}, y ^ {(i)}, \rho) \right] - \underset {x \sim \mathcal {D}, y = y (x), \rho} {\mathbb {E}} \left[ \nabla_ {w} \mathbf {L o s s} (w; x, y, \rho) \right] \right\| _ {F} \leq \frac {1}{\mathrm {p o l y} (d)} \\ \end{array}
$$

In addition, suppose for every  $i \in [N]$ , we have an i.i.d. random sample  $\rho^{(i)} \sim \mathcal{N}(0, \sigma_{\rho}^2 \mathbf{I})$  that is independent of  $\mathcal{Z}$  and  $w$ . Then, with probability at least  $1 - e^{-\Omega (\log^2 d)}$  over  $\rho$ , we have

$$
\begin{array}{l} \left| \frac {1}{N} \sum_ {i \in [ N ]} \mathbb {E} _ {\rho} \left[ \mathbf {L o s s} (w; x ^ {(i)}, y ^ {(i)}, \rho) \right] - \frac {1}{N} \sum_ {i \in [ N ]} \mathbf {L o s s} (w; x ^ {(i)}, y ^ {(i)}, \rho^ {(i)}) \right] \leq \frac {1}{\text {p o l y} (d)} \\ \left\| \right. \frac {1}{N} \sum_ {i \in [ N ]} \mathbb {E} _ {\rho} \left[ \nabla_ {w} \mathbf {L o s s} (w; x ^ {(i)}, y ^ {(i)}, \rho) \right] - \frac {1}{N} \sum_ {i \in [ N ]} \nabla_ {w} \mathbf {L o s s} (w; x ^ {(i)}, y ^ {(i)}, \rho^ {(i)}) \left. \right]\left. \right\| _ {F} \leq \frac {1}{\mathrm {p o l y} (d)} \\ \end{array}
$$

Proof. The proof of the first part can be done by trivial VC dimension or Rademacher complexity arguments. For instance, the function  $\mathbb{E}_{\rho}[\nabla \mathbf{Loss}(w; x, y, \rho)]$  is Lipschitz continuous in  $w$  with Lipschitz parameter at most  $\mathsf{poly}(d)$  (note that this relies on the fact that we take an expectation in  $\rho$ ), and thus one can take an epsilon-net over all possible choices of  $w$ , and then apply a union bound over them.

The proof of the second part can be done by trivial Hoeffding bounds.

# B Neuron Structure and Initialization Properties

We consider  $m = d^{1 + c_0}$  for a very small constant  $c_0 \in (0,1)$ , and consider constants  $c_1 > c_2$  to be chosen shortly. Let us define a few notations to characterize each neuron's behavior.

Definition B.1 (neuron characterization). Recall  $w_{i}^{(t)}$  is the weight for the  $i$ -th neuron at iteration  $t$ . We shall choose a parameter  $\sigma_w^{(t)}$  at each iteration  $t \geq 0$  and define the following notions. Consider any dimension  $j \in [d]$ .

1. Let  $\mathcal{S}_{j, sure}^{(t)} \subseteq [m]$  be those neurons  $i \in [m]$  satisfying

$\langle w_i^{(t)},\mathbf{M}_j\rangle^2\geq (c_1 + c_2)(\sigma_w^{(t)})^2\log d,$  
$\langle w_i^{(t)},\mathbf{M}_{j'}\rangle^2 < (c_1 - c_2)(\sigma_w^{(t)})^2\log d$  
-  $\operatorname{sign}(\langle w_i^{(t)}, \mathbf{M}_j \rangle) = \operatorname{sign}(w_j^\star)$ .

for every  $j^{\prime}\neq j$

2. Let  $\mathcal{S}_{j, pot}^{(t)} \subseteq [m]$  be those neurons  $i \in [m]$  satisfying

$\langle w_i^{(t)},\mathbf{M}_j\rangle^2\geq (c_1 - c_2)(\sigma_w^{(t)})^2\log d$

3. Let  $S_{\text{opt}}^{(t)} \subseteq [m]$  be the set of neurons  $i \in [m]$  satisfying

$\| w_i^{(t)}\| _2^2\leq 2(\sigma_w^{(t)})^2 d$  
$\langle w_i^{(t)},\mathbf{M}_j\rangle^2\geq (c_1 - c_2)(\sigma_w^{(t)})^2\log d$  
$\langle w_i^{(t)},\mathbf{M}_j\rangle^2\geq 2(\sigma_w^{(t)})^2\sqrt{\log d}$  
$|\langle w_i^{(t)}, \mathbf{M}_j \rangle| \leq \frac{\sigma_w^{(t)}}{\log d}$

for at most  $O(1)$  many  $j \in [d]$ .

for at most  $2^{-\sqrt{\log d}}$  many  $j \in [d]$ .

for at least  $\Omega\left(\frac{d}{\log d}\right)$  many  $j \in [d]$ .

Lemma B.2 (geometry at initialization). Suppose each  $w_i^{(0)} \sim \mathcal{N}(0, \sigma_0^2\mathbf{I})$  and suppose  $\sigma_w^{(0)} = \sigma_0$ . For every constants  $c_0 \in (0,1)$  and  $\gamma \in (0,0.1)$ , by choosing  $c_1 = 2 + 2(1 - \gamma)c_0$  and  $c_2 = \gamma c_0$ , we have with probability  $\geq 1 - o(1 / d^3)$  over the random initialization, for all  $j \in [d]$ :

$$
| \mathcal {S} _ {j, s u r e} ^ {(0)} | = \Omega \left(d ^ {\frac {\gamma}{4} c _ {0}}\right) =: \Xi_ {1} \qquad \qquad | \mathcal {S} _ {j, p o t} ^ {(0)} | \leq O \left(d ^ {2 \gamma c _ {0}}\right) =: \Xi_ {2} \qquad \qquad \mathcal {S} _ {e p t} ^ {(0)} = [ m ]
$$

Remark. In the rest of the paper, we shall assume Lemma B.2 holds in all upper-bound related theorems/lemmas, and for simplicity, we assume  $\gamma > 0$  is some small constant so that  $(\Xi_2)^{100} \leq d^{c_0}$ . The notations  $\Xi_1$  and  $\Xi_2$  shall be used throughout the paper.

Definition B.3 (neuron characterization, continued). Recall  $b^{(t)}$  is the bias at iteration  $t$ , and let us introduce more notions.

1. Let  $\mathcal{S}_{\text{opt+}}^{(t)} \subseteq [m]$  be the set of neurons  $i \in [m]$  satisfying

$\| w_i^{(t)}\| _2^2\leq \frac{(\sigma_w^{(t)})^2d}{\log^2d},$  
$\left|\langle w_i^{(t)},\mathbf{M}_j\rangle \right|\geq \frac{\sigma_w^{(t)}}{\log d}$

for at most  $O(1)$  many  $j \in [d]$ .

2. Let  $S_{\text{opt}++}^{(t)} \subseteq [m]$  be the set of neurons  $i \in [m]$  satisfying

$\| w_i^{(t)}\| _2^2\leq \frac{(\sigma_w^{(t)})^2}{\beta^2}$  for  $\beta \stackrel {\mathrm{def}}{=}\frac{1}{\sqrt{k}\Xi_{2}^{10}}$

3. Let  $S_{j, pot+}^{(t)} \subseteq [m]$  be the set of neurons  $i \in [m]$  satisfying

$|\langle w_i^{(t)},\mathbf{M}_j\rangle |\geq \frac{k}{d\beta} b^{(t)}$

4. Let  $S_{j, sure+}^{(t)} \subseteq [m]$  be the set of neurons  $i \in [m]$  satisfying

$\langle w_i^{(t)},\mathbf{M}_j\rangle^2\geq 4k(b^{(t)})^2,$  
-  $\operatorname{sign}(\langle w_i^{(t)}, \mathbf{M}_j \rangle) = \operatorname{sign}(w_j^\star)$ .

Note that we do not have good properties on  $S_{ept+}^{(t)}$ ,  $S_{ept++}^{(t)}$ ,  $S_{j,pot+}^{(t)}$  or  $S_{j,sure+}^{(t)}$  at initialization  $t = 0$ ; however, they will gradually begin to satisfy certain properties as the training process goes. See Section C for details.

# B.1 Proof of Lemma B.2

Proof of Lemma B.2. Recall if  $g$  is standard Gaussian, then for every  $t > 0$ ,

$$
\frac {1}{\sqrt {2 \pi}} \frac {t}{t ^ {2} + 1} e ^ {- t ^ {2} / 2} <   \mathbf {P r} _ {g \sim \mathcal {N} (0, 1)} [ g > t ] <   \frac {1}{\sqrt {2 \pi}} \frac {1}{t} e ^ {- t ^ {2} / 2}
$$

Therefore, for every  $i\in [m]$  and  $j\in [d]$

-  $p_1 = \mathbf{Pr}[\langle w_i^{(0)},\mathbf{M}_j\rangle^2\geq (c_1 + c_2)\sigma_0^2\log d] = \Theta (\frac{1}{\log d})\cdot \frac{1}{d^{(c_1 + c_2) / 2}} = \Theta (\frac{1}{\sqrt{\log d}})\cdot \frac{1}{d\cdot d^{(1 - \gamma / 2)c_0}}$  
$p_2 = \mathbf{Pr}[\langle w_i^{(0)},\mathbf{M}_j\rangle^2\geq (c_1 - c_2)\sigma_0^2\log d] = \Theta (\frac{1}{\log d})\cdot \frac{1}{d^{(c_1 - c_2) / 2}} = \Theta (\frac{1}{\sqrt{\log d}})\cdot \frac{1}{d\cdot d^{(1 - 3\gamma / 2)c_0}}$

1. We first lower bound  $|\mathcal{S}_{j,sure}^{(0)}|$ . For every  $i \in [m]$ , with probability at least  $p_1 / 2 \cdot (1 - p_2)^{d-1} \geq \Omega\left(\frac{1}{\sqrt{\log d}}\right) \cdot \frac{d^{\frac{\gamma}{2}c_0}}{m}$  it satisfies

$$
\begin{array}{l} \langle w _ {i} ^ {(0)}, \mathbf {M} _ {j} \rangle^ {2} \geq (c _ {1} + c _ {2}) \sigma_ {0} ^ {2} \log d, \quad \operatorname {s i g n} (\langle w _ {i} ^ {(0)}, \mathbf {M} _ {j} \rangle) \operatorname {s i g n} (w _ {j} ^ {\star}) \geq 0 \\ \forall j ^ {\prime} \neq j, \langle w _ {i} ^ {(0)}, \mathbf {M} _ {j ^ {\prime}} \rangle^ {2} \leq (c _ {1} - c _ {2}) \sigma_ {0} ^ {2} \log d \\ \end{array}
$$

By concentration with respect to all  $m$  choices of  $i \in [m]$ , we know with probability at least  $1 - o\left(\frac{1}{d^3}\right)$  it satisfies  $|\mathcal{S}_{j, sure}^{(0)}| = \Omega\left(d^{\frac{\gamma}{4}c_0}\right)$ .

2. We next upper bound  $|\mathcal{S}_{j,pot}^{(0)}|$ . For every  $i \in [m]$ , with probability at most  $p_2 < O\left(\frac{1}{\sqrt{\log d}}\right) \cdot \frac{d^{\frac{3\gamma}{2}c_0}}{m}$  it satisfies

$$
\langle w _ {i} ^ {(0)}, \mathbf {M} _ {j} \rangle^ {2} \geq (c _ {1} - c _ {2}) \sigma_ {0} ^ {2} \log d
$$

By concentration with respect to all  $m$  choices of  $i$ , we know with probability at least  $1 - o\left(\frac{1}{d^3}\right)$  it satisfies  $|\mathcal{S}_{j, pot}^{(0)}| = O(d^{2\gamma c_0})$ .

3. As for  $S_{ept}^{(0)}$ , we first note that for every  $i \in [m]$ , by chi-square distribution's tail bound, with probability at least  $1 - o(1 / d^3)$  it satisfies  $\| w_i^{(0)}\|_2^2 \in \left[\frac{\sigma_0^2d}{2}, 2\sigma_0^2d\right]$ .

For every  $i \in [m]$ , the probability of existing  $q = 20 / c_0$  different

$$
j _ {1}, \dots , j _ {q} \in [ d ]: s. t. \forall r \in [ q ]: \left\langle w _ {i} ^ {(0)}, \mathbf {M} _ {j _ {r}} \right\rangle^ {2} \geq (c _ {1} - c _ {2}) \sigma_ {0} ^ {2} \log d
$$

is at most  $d^q \cdot (p_2)^q \leq d^{-q \cdot \frac{c_0}{2}} \leq \frac{1}{d^4m}$ . Union bounding over all possible  $i \in [m]$  gives the proof that, with probability at least  $1 - 1/d^4$ , for all but at most  $q = O(1)$  values of  $j \in [d]$ , it satisfies  $\langle w_i^{(0)}, \mathbf{M}_j \rangle^2 < (c_1 - c_2)(\sigma_w^{(t)})^2 \log d$ .

For every  $i\in [m]$  and  $j\in [d]$ , with probability at least  $1 - e^{-\sqrt{\log d}}$  it satisfies  $\langle w_i^{(0)},\mathbf{M}_j\rangle^2\leq 2\sigma_0^2\sqrt{\log d}$ . Therefore, with probability at least  $1 - o(1 / d^3)$ , there are  $\geq d(1 - 2^{-\sqrt{\log d}})$  indices  $j\in [d]$  satisfying  $\langle w_i^{(0)},\mathbf{M}_j\rangle^2\leq 2\sigma_0^2\sqrt{\log d}$ .

For every  $i\in [m]$  and  $j\in [d]$ , with probability at least  $\frac{1}{40000\sqrt{\log d}}$  it satisfies  $|\langle w_i^{(0)},\mathbf{M}_j\rangle |\leq \frac{\sigma_0}{10000\sqrt{\log d}}$ . Therefore, with probability at least  $1 - o(1 / d^3)$ , there are  $\frac{1}{100000\sqrt{\log d}}$  indices  $j\in [d]$  satisfying  $|\langle w_i^{(0)},\mathbf{M}_j\rangle |\leq \frac{\sigma_0}{10000\sqrt{\log d}}$ .

# C Neuron Structure Change During Training

For analysis purpose, we consider two phases during training. In Phase I, the neurons have moved so little so that the accuracy remains  $50\%$  for binary classification; however, some neurons shall start to win lottery and form "singleton" structures. We summarize this as the following theorem.

Theorem C.1 (phase I). Suppose the high-probability initialization event in Lemma B.2 holds. Suppose  $\eta, \sigma_0 \in (0, \frac{1}{\mathsf{poly}(d)})$  and  $N \geq \mathsf{poly}(d)$ . With probability at least  $1 - e^{-\Omega (\log^2 d)}$ , the following holds for all  $t \leq T_{\mathsf{b}} \stackrel{\mathrm{def}}{=} \Theta\left(\frac{d^2 \sigma_0}{k \eta}\right)$

1.  $\mathcal{S}_{j,sure}^{(0)}\subseteq \mathcal{S}_{j,sure}^{(t)}$  for every  $j\in [d]$  
2.  $\mathcal{S}_{j, pot}^{(0)} \supseteq \mathcal{S}_{j, pot}^{(t)}$  for every  $j \in [d]$ .  
3.  $\mathcal{S}_{ept}^{(t)} = [m]$  
4.  $\mathcal{S}_{\text{opt+}}^{(t)} = [m]$  for every  $t \geq T_{\mathsf{a}} \stackrel{\mathrm{def}}{=} \Theta\left(\frac{d\sigma_0\log^{2.5}d}{\eta}\right)$ .  
5.  $\mathcal{S}_{ept + + }^{(t)} = [m]$  and  $\mathcal{S}_{j,pot}^{(0)}\supseteq \mathcal{S}_{j,pot + }^{(t)}$  for every  $j\in [d]$  at this iteration  $t = T_{\mathfrak{b}}$

(Recall according to Definition B.1 we have  $\mathcal{S}_{j,sure}^{(t)}\subseteq \mathcal{S}_{j,pot}^{(t)}$

In Phase II, the neurons start to move much more so that the network output becomes more meaningful; in phase II, the "singleton" neurons become even more singleton.

Theorem C.2 (phase II). In the same setting as Theorem C.1, with probability at least  $1 - e^{-\Omega (\log^2 d)}$ , the following holds for all  $t \in [T_{\mathsf{b}}, d^{O(\log d)} / \eta]$ .

1.  $\mathcal{S}_{\text{opt} + + }^{(t)} = \mathcal{S}_{\text{opt} + }^{(t)} = [m]$ .  
2.  $\mathcal{S}_{j,sure}^{(0)}\subseteq \mathcal{S}_{j,sure}^{(t)}$  for every  $j\in [d]$  
3.  $\mathcal{S}_{j,pot}^{(0)}\supseteq \mathcal{S}_{j,pot + }^{(t)}\supseteq \mathcal{S}_{j,pot}^{(t)}$  for every  $j\in [d]$  
4.  $\mathcal{S}_{j,sure}^{(0)}\subseteq \mathcal{S}_{j,sure + }^{(t)}$  for every  $j\in [d]$ , as long as  $t\geq T_{\mathbf{e}}\stackrel {\mathrm{def}}{=}\Theta \left(\frac{d}{\eta\Xi_2\log d}\right)$ .

(Recall according to Definition B.3 we have  $\mathcal{S}_{j,sure+}^{(t)} \subseteq \mathcal{S}_{j,pot}^{(t)}$ .)

Remark C.3. Theorem C.2 immediately implies the first claim of Theorem 5.1 and the first claim of Theorem 5.2, after plugging in the definitions of those neuron structure sets introduced in Section B. For instance, we can write

$$
w _ {i} ^ {(t)} = \sum_ {j \in [ d ]: i \in \mathcal {S} _ {j, p o t} ^ {(0)}} \langle w _ {i} ^ {(t)}, \mathbf {M} _ {j} \rangle \mathbf {M} _ {j} + \sum_ {j \in [ d ]: i \notin \mathcal {S} _ {j, p o t} ^ {(0)}} \langle w _ {i} ^ {(t)}, \mathbf {M} _ {j} \rangle \mathbf {M} _ {j}
$$

We make several observations, when  $t \geq T_{\mathsf{e}}$ :

-  $\mathcal{S}_{ept}^{(t)} = [m]$  implies the cardinality of  $\{j \in [d] \colon i \in \mathcal{S}_{j,pot}^{(0)}\}$  is  $\leq O(1)$ , so we can define it as  $\mathcal{N}_i$ .  
- For every  $i \notin S_{j,pot}^{(0)}$ , it also satisfies  $i \notin S_{j,pot+}^{(t)}$ , so we have  $|\langle w_i^{(t)}, \mathbf{M}_j \rangle| < \frac{k}{d\beta} b^{(t)} \leq \frac{k\Xi_2^2}{d}$  (using  $b^{(t)} \leq \beta \Xi_2^2$  from Definition C.12).  
- For every  $i \in S_{j,sure+}^{(t)} \subseteq S_{j,pot}^{(0)}$ , we have  $\langle w_i^{(t)}, \mathbf{M}_j \rangle \cdot \mathrm{sign}(w_i^*) \geq 2\sqrt{k} b^{(t)} > \frac{1}{\Xi_2^8}$  (using  $b^{(t)} = \beta \Xi_2^2$  from Definition C.12 and our choice of  $\beta$ ).

They together imply the first claim of Theorem 5.2. One can similarly derive the first claim of Theorem 5.1.

# C.1 Auxiliary Lemma 1: Geometry of Crossing Boundary

We present a lemma to bound the size of the pre-activation signal.

Lemma C.4 (pre-activation signal size). For every  $t \geq 0$ , every  $i \in [m]$ , every  $\lambda \geq 0$ , every  $j \in [d]$ :

(a) If  $i \in S_{\text{opt}}^{(t)}$  then

$$
\mathbf {P r} _ {z, \xi} \left[ \left\langle w _ {i} ^ {(t)}, \sum_ {j ^ {\prime} \neq j} \mathbf {M} _ {j ^ {\prime}} z _ {j ^ {\prime}} + \xi \right\rangle^ {2} \geq \lambda^ {2} (\sigma_ {w} ^ {(t)}) ^ {2} \right] \leq e ^ {- \Omega (\frac {\lambda}{\log^ {1 / 4} d})} + e ^ {- \log^ {1 / 4} d}
$$

(b) If  $i \in S_{\text{opt+}}^{(t)}$  then

$$
\mathbf {P r} _ {z, \xi} \left[ \left\langle w _ {i} ^ {(t)}, \sum_ {j ^ {\prime} \neq j} \mathbf {M} _ {j ^ {\prime}} z _ {j ^ {\prime}} + \xi \right\rangle^ {2} \geq \lambda^ {2} (\sigma_ {w} ^ {(t)}) ^ {2} \right] \leq e ^ {- \Omega (\lambda \log d)} + e ^ {- \Omega (\lambda^ {2} \log d)} + O \left(\frac {k}{d}\right)
$$

Proof of Lemma C.4a. Let  $\mathcal{E}$  be the event where there exists  $j' \in [d]$  with  $|z_{j'}| \geq \frac{1}{\log^2 d}$  and  $\langle w_i^{(t)}, \mathbf{M}_{j'} \rangle^2 \geq 2 (\sigma_w^{(t)})^2 \sqrt{\log d}$ . Since  $\mathbb{E}[z_{j'}^2] = O\left(\frac{1}{d}\right)$ , we know that  $\mathbf{Pr}\left[|z_{j'}| \geq \frac{1}{\log^2 d}\right] \leq O\left(\frac{\log^4 d}{d}\right)$ . By the definition of  $\mathcal{S}_{ept}^{(t)}$  and union bound, we know

$$
\mathbf {P r} [ \mathcal {E} ] \leq O \left(\frac {\log^ {4} d}{d}\right) \times 2 ^ {- \sqrt {\log d}} d \leq e ^ {- \log^ {1 / 4} d}
$$

Let  $\mathcal{F}$  be the event where there exists  $j' \in [d]$  with  $z_{j'} \neq 0$  and  $\langle w_i^{(t)}, \mathbf{M}_{j'} \rangle^2 \geq \Omega((\sigma_w^{(t)})^2 \log d)$ . Again, by the definition of  $\mathcal{S}_{ept}^{(t)}$ , we know that

$$
\Pr [ \mathcal {F} ] \leq O \left(\frac {k}{d}\right) \leq e ^ {- \log^ {1 / 4} d}
$$

Thus, when neither  $\mathcal{E}$  or  $\mathcal{F}$  happens, we have for every  $j' \in [d]$ :

$$
\langle w _ {i} ^ {(t)}, \mathbf {M} _ {j ^ {\prime}} z _ {j ^ {\prime}} \rangle^ {2} \leq \min \left\{2 (\sigma_ {w} ^ {(t)}) ^ {2} \sqrt {\log d} \cdot 1, O ((\sigma_ {w} ^ {(t)}) ^ {2} \log d) \cdot \frac {1}{\log^ {4} d} \right\} \leq 2 (\sigma_ {w} ^ {(t)}) ^ {2} \sqrt {\log d}.
$$

At the same time, we also have

$$
\sum_ {j ^ {\prime} \in [ d ]} \underset {z _ {j ^ {\prime}}} {\mathbb {E}} \langle w _ {i} ^ {(t)}, \mathbf {M} _ {j ^ {\prime}} z _ {j ^ {\prime}} \rangle^ {2} \leq \sum_ {j ^ {\prime} \in [ d ]} O (\frac {1}{d}) \langle w _ {i} ^ {(t)}, \mathbf {M} _ {j ^ {\prime}} \rangle^ {2} \leq O ((\sigma_ {w} ^ {(t)}) ^ {2})
$$

Apply Bernstein concentration bound we complete the proof that

$$
\mathbf {P r} _ {z, \xi} \left[ \left\langle w _ {i} ^ {(t)}, \sum_ {j ^ {\prime} \neq j} \mathbf {M} _ {j ^ {\prime}} z _ {j ^ {\prime}} \right\rangle^ {2} \geq \frac {\lambda^ {2}}{2} (\sigma_ {w} ^ {(t)}) ^ {2} \right] \leq e ^ {- \Omega (\frac {\lambda}{\log^ {1 / 4} d})} + e ^ {- \log^ {1 / 4} d}
$$

Finally, for the  $\xi$  part, let us recall  $\langle w_i^{(t)},\xi \rangle$  variable with variance at most  $O(\frac{\|w_i^{(t)}\|^2\sigma_x^2}{d})\leq O((\sigma_w^{(t)})^2)$  and each  $|\langle w_i^{(t)},\mathbf{M}_j\rangle \langle \mathbf{M}_j,\xi \rangle |\leq \frac{\sigma_w^{(t)}}{\log^2d}$  w.h.p. Using Bernstein concentration of random variables, we finish the proof.

Proof of Lemma C.4b. Let  $\mathcal{F}$  be the event where there exists  $j' \in [d]$  with  $z_{j'} \neq 0$  and  $|\langle w_i^{(t)}, \mathbf{M}_{j'} \rangle| \geq$

$\Omega \left(\frac{\sigma_w^{(t)}}{\log d}\right)$ . By the definition of  $S_{ept+}^{(t)}$ , we know that

$$
\operatorname {P r} [ \mathcal {F} ] \leq O \left(\frac {k}{d}\right)
$$

When  $\mathcal{F}$  does not happen, we have for every  $j' \in [d]$ :  $|\langle w_i^{(t)}, \mathbf{M}_{j'}z_{j'}\rangle| \leq O\left(\frac{\sigma_w^{(t)}}{\log d}\right)$  and at the same time

$$
\sum_ {j ^ {\prime} \in [ d ]} \mathbb {E} _ {z _ {j ^ {\prime}}} \langle w _ {i} ^ {(t)}, \mathbf {M} _ {j ^ {\prime}} z _ {j ^ {\prime}} \rangle^ {2} \leq \sum_ {j ^ {\prime} \in [ d ]} O (\frac {1}{d}) \langle w _ {i} ^ {(t)}, \mathbf {M} _ {j ^ {\prime}} \rangle^ {2} \leq O (\frac {(\sigma_ {w} ^ {(t)}) ^ {2}}{\log d})
$$

Apply Bernstein concentration bound we complete the proof that

$$
\mathbf {P r} _ {z, \xi} \left[ \left<   w _ {i} ^ {(t)}, \sum_ {j ^ {\prime} \neq j} \mathbf {M} _ {j ^ {\prime}} z _ {j ^ {\prime}} \right> ^ {2} \geq \frac {\lambda^ {2}}{2} (\sigma_ {w} ^ {(t)}) ^ {2} \right] \leq e ^ {- \Omega (\lambda \log d)} + e ^ {- \Omega (\lambda^ {2} \log d)} + O \left(\frac {k}{d}\right)
$$

Finally, for the  $\xi$  part, let us recall  $\langle w_i^{(t)},\xi \rangle$  is a random variable with variance at most  $O(\frac{\|w_i^{(t)}\|^2\sigma_x^2}{d})\leq O(\frac{(\sigma_w^{(t)})^2}{\log^2d})$ . Using the Bernstein concentration bound, we finish the proof.

# C.2 Auxiliary Lemma 2: A Critical Lemma for Gradient Bound

In this section we present a critical lemma that shall be used multiple times to bound the gradient in many of the following sections. Recall  $\frac{c_2}{c_1} \in (0, 0.1)$  is a constant from Lemma B.2.

Lemma C.5 (critical). Let  $Y(z, S_1) \colon \mathbb{R} \times \mathbb{R}^p \to [-1, 1]$  be a center symmetric function, meaning  $Y(z, S_1) = Y(-z, -S_1)$ . Let  $S_1 \in \mathbb{R}^p$  and  $S_2 \in \mathbb{R}$  be random variables, where  $(S_1, S_2)$  is symmetrically distributed, meaning  $(S_1, S_2)$  distributes the same as  $(-S_1, -S_2)$ .

For every  $\alpha > 0$ , suppose  $\rho \sim \mathcal{N}(0, \sigma_{\rho}^{2})$ , define quantity

$$
\Delta := \underset {S _ {1}, S _ {2}, \rho} {\mathbb {E}} \left[ Y (1, S _ {1}) \mathbb {1} _ {\alpha + S _ {2} + \rho \geq b} - Y (- 1, S _ {1}) \mathbb {1} _ {- \alpha + S _ {2} + \rho \geq b} \right]
$$

and define parameters  $V\coloneqq \mathbb{E}[(S_2)^2 ]$  and  $L\coloneqq \mathbb{E}_{S_1}[|Y(1,S_1) - Y(0,S_1)|]$ . Then,

(a) it always satisfies  $|\Delta| \leq O\left(\frac{\sqrt{V}}{\sigma_{\rho}} + L\right)$  
(b) if  $Y(z, S_1)$  is a monotonically non-decreasing in  $z \in \mathbb{R}$  for every  $S_1 \in \mathbb{R}^p$ , then  $\Delta \geq -\Omega \left( \frac{\sqrt{V}}{\sigma_\rho} \right)$ . Furthermore, suppose we can write  $S_1 = (S_1', S_1'')$  and  $S_2 = S_2' + S_2''$  for  $(S_1', S_2')$  and  $(S_1'', S_2'')$  being independent (although  $S_1', S_2'$  may be dependent, and  $S_1'', S_2''$  may be dependent). Then, we have

(c) if  $\alpha \leq b(1 - \frac{c_2}{2c_1})$ , then  $|\Delta| \leq \left(e^{-\Omega(b^2/\sigma_\rho^2)} + \Gamma\right)\left(\min\{1, O(\frac{\alpha}{\sigma_\rho})\} + L_y\right) + \Gamma_y$

where the parameters

$\Gamma \coloneqq \mathbf{Pr}\left[|S_2|\geq \frac{c_2}{10c_1}\cdot b\right]$  and  $\Gamma_y\coloneqq \mathbf{Pr}\left[|S_2^{\prime \prime}|\geq \frac{c_2}{10c_1}\cdot b\right]$  
$L_{y}\coloneqq \max_{S_{1}^{\prime}}\left\{\mathbb{E}_{S_{1}^{\prime \prime}}[|Y(1,S_{1}^{\prime},S_{1}^{\prime \prime}) - Y(0,S_{1}^{\prime},S_{1}^{\prime \prime})|]\right\} \leq 1$

Proof of Lemma C.5. We first focus on  $Y(1, S_1) \mathbb{1}_{\alpha + S_2 + \rho \geq b}$ , and write

$$
\begin{array}{l} Y (1, S _ {1}) \mathbb {1} _ {\alpha + S _ {2} + \rho \geq b} = Y (0, S _ {1}) \mathbb {1} _ {\alpha + S _ {2} + \rho \geq b} + \left(Y (1, S _ {1}) - Y (0, S _ {1})\right) \mathbb {1} _ {\alpha + S _ {2} + \rho \geq b} (C.1) \\ = Y \left(0, S _ {1}\right) \mathbb {1} _ {\alpha + S _ {2} + \rho \geq b} \pm \left| Y \left(1, S _ {1}\right) - Y \left(0, S _ {1}\right) \right| \mathbb {1} _ {\alpha + S _ {2} + \rho \geq b} (C.2) \\ \end{array}
$$

Focusing on the term  $Y(0, S_1) \mathbb{1}_{\alpha + S_2 + \rho \geq b}$ , by the symmetric properties of  $Y$  and  $(S_1, S_2)$ , we have

$$
\begin{array}{l} | \mathbb {E} [ Y (0, S _ {1}) \mathbb {1} _ {\alpha + S _ {2} + \rho \geq b} ] | = \frac {1}{2} | \mathbb {E} [ Y (0, S _ {1}) \mathbb {1} _ {\alpha + S _ {2} + \rho \geq b} + Y (0, - S _ {1}) \mathbb {1} _ {\alpha - S _ {2} + \rho \geq b} ] | \\ = \frac {1}{2} | \mathbb {E} [ Y (0, S _ {1}) (\mathbb {1} _ {\alpha + S _ {2} + \rho \geq b} - \mathbb {1} _ {\alpha - S _ {2} + \rho \geq b}) ] | \\ \leq \Pr [ \mathbb {1} _ {\alpha + S _ {2} + \rho \geq b} \neq \mathbb {1} _ {\alpha - S _ {2} + \rho \geq b} ] \\ \leq \Pr [ \rho \in [ b - \alpha - S _ {2}, b - \alpha + S _ {2} ] ] \\ = O \left(\frac {\mathbb {E} [ | S _ {2} | ]}{\sigma_ {\rho}}\right) = O \left(\frac {\sqrt {\mathbb {E} [ S _ {2} ^ {2} ]}}{\sigma_ {\rho}}\right) = O \left(\frac {\sqrt {V}}{\sigma_ {\rho}}\right) \tag {C.3} \\ \end{array}
$$

Putting (C.3) into (C.2), applying the bound  $L = \mathbb{E}_{S_1}[|Y(1,S_1) - Y(0,S_1)|]$ , and repeating the same analysis for  $Y(-1,S_1)\mathbb{1}_{-\alpha + S_2 + \rho \geq b}$  gives

$$
| \Delta | \leq O \left(\frac {\sqrt {V}}{\sigma_ {\rho}} + L\right).
$$

This proves Lemma C.5a. When  $Y(z, S_1)$  is a monotone non-decreasing in  $z \in \mathbb{R}$ , we have

$$
Y (1, S _ {1}) \mathbb {1} _ {\alpha + S _ {2} + \rho \geq b} \geq Y (0, S _ {1}) \mathbb {1} _ {\alpha + S _ {2} + \rho \geq b}
$$

$$
Y (- 1, S _ {1}) \mathbb {1} _ {- \alpha + S _ {2} + \rho \geq b} \leq Y (0, S _ {1}) \mathbb {1} _ {- \alpha + S _ {2} + \rho \geq b}
$$

so we can go back to (C.1) (and repeating for  $Y(-1, S_1)$ ) to derive that

$$
\Delta \geq - \Omega \left(\frac {\sqrt {V}}{\sigma_ {\rho}}\right)
$$

This proves Lemma C.5b. Finally, when  $\alpha \leq b\left(1 - \frac{c_2}{2c_1}\right)$ , we can bound  $\Delta$  differently

$$
\begin{array}{l} \left| \Delta \right| \leq 2 \Pr \left[ \mathbb {1} _ {\alpha + S _ {2} + \rho \geq b} \neq \mathbb {1} _ {- \alpha + S _ {2} + \rho \geq b} \right] + \mathbb {E} \left[ | Y (1, S _ {1}) - Y (- 1, S _ {1}) | \cdot \mathbb {1} _ {\alpha + S _ {2} + \rho \geq b} \right] \\ = 2 \Pr [ \rho \in [ b - S _ {2} - \alpha , b - S _ {2} + \alpha ] ] + \mathbb {E} \left[ | Y (1, S _ {1}) - Y (- 1, S _ {1}) | \cdot \mathbb {1} _ {\alpha + S _ {2} + \rho \geq b} \right] \tag {C.4} \\ \end{array}
$$

To bound the first term in (C.4) we consider two cases.:

- when  $|S_2| \leq \frac{b}{4}$ , we have  $\mathbf{Pr}_{\rho}[\rho \in [b - S_2 - \alpha, b - S_2 + \alpha]] \leq \min \{1, \frac{\alpha}{\sigma_{\rho}}\} e^{-\Omega (b^2 / \sigma_{\rho}^2)}$ ;  
- when  $|S_2| \geq \frac{b}{4}$  (happening w.p.  $\leq \Gamma$ ), we have  $\mathbf{Pr}_{\rho}[\rho \in [b - S_2 - \alpha, b - S_2 + \alpha]] \leq \min \{1, O\left(\frac{\alpha}{\sigma_{\rho}}\right)\}$ .

Putting together, we know that

$$
\Pr [ \rho \in [ b - S _ {2} - \alpha , b - S _ {2} + \alpha ] ] \leq \min  \{1, \frac {\alpha}{\sigma_ {\rho}} \} \cdot \left(e ^ {- \Omega \left(b ^ {2} / \sigma_ {\rho} ^ {2}\right)} + \Gamma\right) \tag {C.5}
$$

To bound the second term in (C.4), first recall  $S_{1} = \left(S_{1}^{\prime},S_{1}^{\prime \prime}\right)$  and  $S_{2} = S_{2}^{\prime} + S_{2}^{\prime \prime}$ , so we can write

$$
\begin{array}{l} \mathbb {E} \left[ | Y (1, S _ {1} ^ {\prime}, S _ {1} ^ {\prime \prime}) - Y (- 1, S _ {1} ^ {\prime}, S _ {1} ^ {\prime \prime}) | \cdot \mathbb {1} _ {\alpha + S _ {2} ^ {\prime} + S _ {2} ^ {\prime \prime} + \rho \geq b} \right] \\ \leq \mathbb {E} \left[ | Y (1, S _ {1} ^ {\prime}, S _ {1} ^ {\prime \prime}) - Y (- 1, S _ {1} ^ {\prime}, S _ {1} ^ {\prime \prime}) | \cdot \left(\mathbb {1} _ {| \alpha + S _ {2} ^ {\prime} + \rho | \geq (1 - \frac {c _ {2}}{1 0 c _ {1}}) \cdot b} + \mathbb {1} _ {| S _ {2} ^ {\prime \prime} | \geq \frac {c _ {2}}{1 0 c _ {1}} \cdot b}\right) \right] \\ \leq \mathbb {E} \left[ | Y \left(1, S _ {1} ^ {\prime}, S _ {1} ^ {\prime \prime}\right) - Y \left(- 1, S _ {1} ^ {\prime}, S _ {1} ^ {\prime \prime}\right) | \cdot \mathbb {1} _ {| \alpha + S _ {2} ^ {\prime} + \rho | \geq \left(1 - \frac {c _ {2}}{1 0 c _ {1}}\right) \cdot b} \right] + \Gamma_ {y} \tag {C.6} \\ \end{array}
$$

To bound the first term in (C.6), we can take expectation over  $S_1''$  and use the bound  $L_y$  to derive

$$
\mathbb {E} \left[ | Y (1, S _ {1} ^ {\prime}, S _ {1} ^ {\prime \prime}) - Y (- 1, S _ {1} ^ {\prime}, S _ {1} ^ {\prime \prime}) | \cdot \mathbb {1} _ {| \alpha + S _ {2} ^ {\prime} + \rho | \geq (1 - \frac {c _ {2}}{1 0 c _ {1}}) \cdot b} \right] \leq L _ {y} \mathbf {P r} \left[ | \alpha + S _ {2} ^ {\prime} + \rho | \geq \left(1 - \frac {c _ {2}}{1 0 c _ {1}}\right) \cdot b \right]
$$

but since  $\alpha \leq \left(1 - \frac{c_2}{2c_1}\right) \cdot b$  and  $S_2' = S_2 - S_2''$ , we can further bound

$$
\begin{array}{l} \mathbf {P r} \left[ | \alpha + S _ {2} ^ {\prime} + \rho | \geq \left(1 - \frac {c _ {2}}{1 0 c _ {1}}\right) \cdot b \right] \leq \mathbf {P r} \left[ | S _ {2} ^ {\prime \prime} | \geq \frac {c _ {2}}{1 0 c _ {1}} \cdot b \right] + \mathbf {P r} \left[ | S _ {2} | \geq \frac {c _ {2}}{1 0 c _ {1}} \cdot b \right] + \mathbf {P r} \left[ \rho \geq \frac {c _ {2}}{1 0 c _ {1}} \cdot b \right] \\ \leq \Gamma_ {y} + \Gamma + e ^ {- \Omega \left(b ^ {2} / \sigma_ {\rho} ^ {2}\right)} \\ \end{array}
$$

Putting these back to (C.6), we have

$$
\mathbb {E} \left[ | Y \left(1, S _ {1} ^ {\prime}, S _ {1} ^ {\prime \prime}\right) - Y \left(- 1, S _ {1} ^ {\prime}, S _ {1} ^ {\prime \prime}\right) | \mathbb {1} _ {\alpha + S _ {2} ^ {\prime} + S _ {2} ^ {\prime \prime} + \rho \geq b} \right] \leq \left(e ^ {- \Omega \left(b ^ {2} / \sigma_ {\rho} ^ {2}\right)} + \Gamma + \Gamma_ {y}\right) L _ {y} + \Gamma_ {y} \tag {C.7}
$$

Putting (C.5) and (C.7) back to (C.4), we conclude the when  $\alpha \leq b\left(1 - \frac{c_2}{2c_1}\right)$ , we have

$$
| \Delta | \leq \left(e ^ {- \Omega \left(b ^ {2} / \sigma_ {\rho} ^ {2}\right)} + \Gamma\right) \left(O (\frac {\alpha}{\sigma_ {\rho}}) + L _ {y}\right) + \Gamma_ {y}
$$

# C.3 Phase I: Winning lottery tickets near initialization

Definition C.6. In Phase I, we have two sub-phases:

- In Phase I.1, we pick  $b^{(t)} = \sqrt{c_1} \sigma_w^{(t)} \sqrt{\log d}$  and  $\sigma_{\rho}^{(t)} = \sigma_w^{(t)} (\log \log \log d)^3$ .

We grow  $b^{(t + 1)} = b^{(t)} + \frac{C\eta}{d}$  for  $T_{\mathsf{a}} = \Theta \left(\frac{d\sigma_0\log^{2.5}d}{\eta}\right)$  iterations.

- In Phase I.2, we pick  $b^{(t)} = \sqrt{c_1} \sigma_w^{(t)} \sqrt{\log d}$  and  $\sigma_{\rho}^{(t)} = \sigma_w^{(t)} \cdot \frac{(\log \log \log d)^3}{\sqrt{\log d}}$ .

We grow  $b^{(t + 1)} = b^{(t)} + \frac{C\eta}{d}$  for  $T_{\mathsf{b}} = \Theta \left(\frac{d^2\sigma_0}{k\eta}\right)$  iterations.

# C.3.1 Activation Probability

Recall  $x = \sum_{j}\mathbf{M}_{j}z_{j} + \xi$ . Recall also  $\frac{c_2}{c_1}\in (0,0.1)$  is a constant from Lemma B.2.

Lemma C.7 (activation probability). We define  $\Gamma_t$  to be any value such that

-  $\mathbf{Pr}_x\left[\left|\langle w_i^{(t)},\sum_{j'\neq j}\mathbf{M}_{j'}z_{j'} + \xi \rangle \right|\geq \frac{c_2}{10c_1} b^{(t)}\right]\leq \Gamma_t$  for every  $i\in [m]$  and  $j\in [d]$ ;  
-  $\mathbf{Pr}_x\left[\left|\langle w_i^{(t)},x\rangle \right|\geq \frac{c_2}{10c_1} b^{(t)}\right]\leq \Gamma_t$  for every  $i\in [m]$  
-  $\mathbf{Pr}_x\left[|\rho_i| \geq \frac{c_2}{10c_1} b^{(t)}\right] \leq \Gamma_t$  for every  $i \in [m]$

We define  $\Gamma_{t,y}$  to be any value such that

- for every  $i \in [m]$  and  $j \in [d]$ , there exists  $\Lambda \subseteq [d] \setminus \{j\}$  with  $|\Lambda| \geq \Omega\left(\frac{d}{\sqrt{\log d}}\right)$  satisfying

$$
\mathbf {P r} _ {x} \left[ \left| \left\langle w _ {i} ^ {(t)}, \sum_ {j ^ {\prime} \in \Lambda} \mathbf {M} _ {j ^ {\prime}} z _ {j ^ {\prime}} \right\rangle \right| \geq \frac {c _ {2}}{1 0 c _ {1}} b ^ {(t)} \right] \leq \Gamma_ {t, y}
$$

Then,

- If we are in Phase I.1 and  $\mathcal{S}_{\text{opt}}^{(t)} = [m]$ , then we can choose  $\Gamma_t = e^{-\Omega (\log^{1/4}d)}$  and  $\Gamma_{t,y} = \frac{1}{d^{10}}$ .  
- If we are in Phase I.2 and  $S_{\text{opt+}}^{(t)} = [m]$ , then we can choose  $\Gamma_t = O\left(\frac{k}{d}\right)$  and  $\Gamma_{t,y} = \frac{1}{d^{10}}$ .

Proof. Recall  $b^{(t)} = \Theta(\sigma_w^{(t)}\sqrt{\log d})$ . Applying Lemma C.4a and Lemma C.4b we immediately have

- If  $\mathcal{S}_{ept}^{(t)} = [m]$ , then  $\mathbf{Pr}_x\left[\left|\left\langle w_i^{(t)},\sum_{j'\neq j}\mathbf{M}_{j'}z_{j'} + \xi \right\rangle \right|\geq \frac{c_2}{10c_1} b^{(t)}\right]\leq e^{-\Omega (\log^{1 / 4}d)}$ ;  
- If  $\mathcal{S}_{ept+}^{(t)} = [m]$ , then  $\mathbf{Pr}_x\left[\left|\left\langle w_i^{(t)}, \sum_{j' \neq j} \mathbf{M}_{j'} z_{j'} + \xi \right\rangle \right| \geq \frac{c_2}{10c_1} b^{(t)}\right] \leq O\left(\frac{k}{d}\right)$ .

Now, recall  $x = \sum_{j' \in [d]} \mathbf{M}_{j'} z_{j'} + \xi$  so it differs from  $\sum_{j' \neq j} \mathbf{M}_{j'} z_{j'} + \xi$  only by one term. Therefore, we have the same bound on  $\mathbf{Pr}_x\left[\left|\left\langle w_i^{(t)}, x \right\rangle \right| \geq \frac{c_2}{10c_1} b^{(t)}\right]$  by modifying the statements of Lemma C.4a and Lemma C.4b (without changing the proofs) to include this missing term.

If  $\mathcal{S}_{ept}^{(t)} = [m]$ ,  $\mathbf{Pr}_{x,\rho}\left[\left|\left\langle w_i^{(t)}, x \right\rangle \right| \geq \frac{c_2}{10c_1} b^{(t)}\right] \leq e^{-\Omega (\log^{1/4}d)}$  
- If  $\mathcal{S}_{ept+}^{(t)} = [m]$ ,  $\mathbf{Pr}_{x,\rho}\left[\left|\left\langle w_i^{(t)}, x \right\rangle \right| \geq \frac{c_2}{10c_1} b^{(t)}\right] \leq O\left(\frac{k}{d}\right)$

At the same time, using  $\rho_{i}\sim \mathcal{N}(0,(\sigma_{\rho}^{(t)})^{2})$  , we also have

- In Phase I.1, because  $\sigma_{\rho}^{(t)} = \Theta (\frac{\sigma_w^{(t)}(\log\log\log d)^3}{\sqrt{\log d}})b^{(t)}$ , we have  $\mathbf{Pr}_{\rho}\left[|\rho_i|\geq \frac{c_2}{10c_1} b^{(t)}\right]\ll e^{-\Omega (\log^{1 / 4}d)}$  
- In Phase I.2, because  $\sigma_{\rho}^{(t)} = \Theta \left(\frac{\sigma_w^{(t)} (\log \log \log d)^3}{\log d}\right) b^{(t)}$ , we have  $\mathbf{Pr}_{\rho}\left[|\rho_i| \geq \frac{c_2}{10c_1} b^{(t)}\right] \ll O\left(\frac{k}{d}\right)$ .

As for the bound on  $\Gamma_{t,y}$ , for every  $i\in [m]$ ,  $j\in [d]$ , let  $\Lambda \subseteq [d]\setminus \{j\}$  be the subset containing all  $j^{\prime}\in [d]\setminus \{j\}$  with  $|\langle w_i^{(t)},\mathbf{M}_{j'}\rangle |\leq q\stackrel {\mathrm{def}}{=}\frac{\sigma_w^{(t)}}{\log d}$ . By the assumption  $\mathcal{S}_{opt}^{(t)} = [m]$ , we know  $|\Lambda |\geq \Omega (d / \log d)$ .

Since  $|\langle w_i^{(t)},\mathbf{M}_{j'}\rangle |\leq q,\mathbb{E}[z_j^2 ] = \Theta (1 / d)$  and  $|z_{j}|\leq 1$  , by Bernstein's inequality, we have

$$
\mathbf {P r} _ {x} \left[ \left| \left\langle w _ {i} ^ {(t)}, \sum_ {j ^ {\prime} \in \Lambda} \mathbf {M} _ {j ^ {\prime}} z _ {j ^ {\prime}} \right\rangle \right| \geq \frac {c _ {2}}{1 0 c _ {1}} b ^ {(t)} \right] \leq e ^ {- \Omega \left(\frac {(b ^ {(t)}) ^ {2}}{q ^ {2} | \Lambda | / d + q \cdot b ^ {(t)}}\right)} \leq e ^ {- \Omega (\log^ {1. 5} d)}.
$$

![](images/9e8030e633182a761f56da9a11854d34889472f1b4f200fe715d79cc802222a3.jpg)

# C.3.2 Growth Lemmas

Our first lemma here shall be used to (lower) bound how  $\langle w_i^{(t)},\mathbf{M}_j\rangle$  (i.e., the weight with respect to neuron  $i$  in direction  $\mathbf{M}_j$ ) grows for those  $i\in S_{j,sure}$ .

Lemma C.8 (signal growth). Suppose we (1) either are in Phase I.1 with  $S_{\text{opt}}^{(t)} = [m]$ , (2) or are in Phase I.2 with  $S_{\text{opt+}}^{(t)} = [m]$ . Then, for every  $j \in [d]$ , every  $i \in S_{j,\text{sure}}^{(t)}$ , as long as  $|\langle w_i^{(t)}, \mathbf{M}_j \rangle| = O(b^{(t)} \log \log \log d)$ , the following holds:

$$
\underset {x, y, \rho} {\mathbb {E}} \left[ y \mathbb {1} _ {\langle w _ {i} ^ {(t)}, x \rangle + \rho_ {i} \geq b ^ {(t)} z _ {j}} \right] = \Theta \left(\frac {1}{d}\right)
$$

Proof of Lemma C.8. Recall that  $i \in S_{j, sure}^{(t)}$  means  $\mathrm{sign}(\langle w_i^{(t)}, \mathbf{M}_j \rangle) = \mathrm{sign}(w_j^\star)$ . Without loss of generality, let us assume  $\mathrm{sign}(\langle w_i^{(t)}, \mathbf{M}_j \rangle) = \mathrm{sign}(w_j^\star) = 1$ .

First consider the case when  $|z_{j}| = 1$ . Since  $j \in S_{j, sure}^{(t)}$ , we have  $\langle w_{i}^{(t)}, \mathbf{M}_{j} \rangle \geq b^{(t)} \sqrt{1 + \frac{c_{2}}{c_{1}}}$  so applying Lemma C.7,

$$
\Pr \left[ \langle w _ {i} ^ {(t)}, x \rangle + \rho_ {i} \geq b ^ {(t)} \mid z _ {j} = 1 \right] \geq 1 - 2 \Gamma_ {t} = 1 - o (1)
$$

$$
\Pr \left[ \langle w _ {i} ^ {(t)}, x \rangle + \rho_ {i} \geq b ^ {(t)} \mid z _ {j} = - 1 \right] \leq 2 \Gamma_ {t} = o (1)
$$

Moreover, a simple calculation using  $|w_j^\star| = \Theta(1)$  gives us  $\mathbb{E}_{x,y}[y \mid z_j = 1] = \Theta(1)$  (can be proven by Lemma H.1) and therefore

$$
\underset {x, y, \rho} {\mathbb {E}} \left[ y \mathbf {1} _ {\langle w _ {i} ^ {(t)}, x \rangle + \rho_ {i} \geq b ^ {(t)}} z _ {j} \mid | z _ {j} | = 1 \right] = \Theta (1)
$$

For all other non-zero value  $|z_{j}| = s > 0$ , we have  $s \geq \frac{1}{\sqrt{k}}$  and wish to apply Lemma C.5 to bound

$$
\Delta_ {s} := \underset {x, y, \rho} {\mathbb {E}} \left[ y \mathbb {1} _ {\langle w _ {i} ^ {(t)}, x \rangle + \rho_ {i} \geq b ^ {(t)}} \mathrm {s i g n} (z _ {j}) | | z _ {j} | = s \right]
$$

In Phase I.1, to apply Lemma C.5, we choose parameters as follows:

-  $Y = y$ ,  $S_{1} = \sum_{j^{\prime}\neq j}w_{j^{\prime}}^{\star}z_{j^{\prime}}$ ,  $S_{2} = \left\langle w_{i}^{(t)},\sum_{j^{\prime}\neq j}\mathbf{M}_{j^{\prime}}z_{j^{\prime}} + \xi \right\rangle$ ,  $\alpha = \langle w_i^{(t)},\mathbf{M}_j\rangle \cdot s > 0$ ,  $\rho = \rho_{i}$  
-  $V = \mathbb{E}[S_2^2] = O((\sigma_w^{(t)})^2), L = \Theta(s)$  (using Lemma H.1),  $\Gamma = \Gamma_t$  (using Lemma C.7),  
- let  $\Lambda$  be the subset defined in Lemma C.7, then we can let  $S_1'' = (z_j)_{j \in \Lambda}$  and  $S_2'' = \left\langle w_i^{(t)}, \sum_{j' \in \Lambda} \mathbf{M}_{j'} z_{j'} \right\rangle$  
we have  $\Gamma_y = \Gamma_{t,y} = \frac{1}{d^{10}}$  (from Lemma C.7) and

$$
\begin{array}{l} L _ {y} = \max  _ {z _ {j ^ {\prime}} \text {f o r} j ^ {\prime} \in [ d ] \setminus \{j \} \setminus \Lambda} \left\{\underset {z _ {j ^ {\prime}} \text {f o r} j ^ {\prime} \in \Lambda} {\mathbb {E}} \left[ | \operatorname {s i g n} \left(w _ {j} ^ {\star} z _ {j} + S _ {1}\right) - \operatorname {s i g n} \left(S _ {1}\right) \right. \right\} \\ \leq \max  _ {z _ {j ^ {\prime}} \text {f o r} j ^ {\prime} \in [ d ] \setminus \{j \} \setminus \Lambda} \left\{\mathbf {P r} _ {z _ {j ^ {\prime}} \text {f o r} j ^ {\prime} \in \Lambda} [ S _ {1} ^ {\prime \prime} \in [ - S _ {1} ^ {\prime} - | w _ {j} ^ {\star} z _ {j} |, - S _ {1} ^ {\prime} + | w _ {j} ^ {\star} z _ {j} | ] ] \right\} \\ \stackrel {1} {\leq} O (\frac {s}{\sqrt {| \Lambda | / d}} + \frac {1}{\sqrt {| \Lambda | k / d}}) \leq O ((s + \frac {1}{\sqrt {k}}) \sqrt {\log d}) \leq O (s \cdot \sqrt {\log d}) \\ \end{array}
$$

where inequality ① uses Lemma H.1a and  $|\Lambda| \geq \Omega\left(\frac{d}{\log d}\right)$  from Lemma C.7.

Hence, invoking Lemma C.5, we have

-  $\Delta_s \geq -\frac{\sigma_w^{(t)}}{\sigma_\rho^{(t)}} \geq -\frac{O(1)}{(\log \log \log d)^3}$  and  $|\Delta_s| \leq \frac{\sigma_w^{(t)}}{\sigma_\rho^{(t)}} + s \leq \frac{O(1)}{(\log \log \log d)^3} + s$  when  $s = \Omega \left(\frac{1}{\log \log \log d}\right)$ .  
-  $|\Delta_s| \leq \left(e^{-\Omega \left(b^2 / \sigma_\rho^2\right)} + \Gamma_t\right) \left(O\left(\frac{\alpha}{\sigma_\rho}\right) + L_y\right) + \Gamma_{t,y} \leq e^{-\Omega (\log^{1/4}d)} \cdot s$  when  $s = O\left(\frac{1}{\log \log \log d}\right)$  (which implies  $\alpha < \frac{b^{(t)}}{4}$ )

Notice that  $\mathbb{E}[z_j^2] = O(1 / d)$ , which implies that

$$
\Pr \left[ | z _ {j} | = \Omega \left(\frac {1}{\log \log \log d}\right) \right] = O \left(\frac {(\log \log \log d) ^ {2}}{d}\right)
$$

This together gives us the bound that

$$
- O \left(\frac {1}{d \cdot \log \log \log d}\right) \leq \mathbb {E} _ {x, y, \rho} \left[ y \mathbb {1} _ {\langle w _ {i} ^ {(t)}, x \rangle + \rho_ {i} \geq b ^ {(t)}} z _ {j} \mid | z _ {j} | <   1 \right] \leq O \left(\frac {1}{d}\right)
$$

In Phase I.2, the analysis is similar with different parameters: in particular,

-  $V = \mathbb{E}[S_2^2] = O\left(\frac{(\sigma_w^{(t)})^2}{\log d}\right)$  which is tighter,

Therefore, we have

-  $\Delta_s \geq -\frac{\sigma_w^{(t)}}{\sigma_\rho^{(t)}\sqrt{\log d}} \geq -\frac{O(1)}{(\log\log\log d)^3}$  and  $|\Delta_s| \leq \frac{\sigma_w^{(t)}}{\sigma_\rho^{(t)}\sqrt{\log d}} + s \leq \frac{O(1)}{(\log\log\log d)^3} + s$  when  $s = \Omega\left(\frac{1}{\log\log\log d}\right)$  
-  $|\Delta_s| \leq \left( e^{-\Omega \left( b^2 / \sigma_\rho^2 \right)} + \Gamma_t \right) \left( O\left( \frac{\alpha}{\sigma_\rho} \right) + L_y \right) + \Gamma_{t,y} \leq O\left( \frac{ks\log d}{d} \right)$  when  $s = O\left( \frac{1}{\log\log\log d} \right)$  (which implies  $\alpha < \frac{b^{(t)}}{4}$ ).

(This uses  $\Gamma_t = O(k / d)$  and  $\frac{\alpha}{\sigma_{\rho}} \leq o(s \log d)$ .)

Taking expectation over  $z_{j}$  as before, and using  $k < d^{(1 - c_0) / 2}$  finishes the proof.

Our next lemma shall be used to upper bound how  $\langle w_i^{(t)},\mathbf{M}_j\rangle$  can grown for every  $i\in [m]$

Lemma C.9 (maximum growth). Suppose we (1) either are in Phase I.1 with  $S_{\text{opt}}^{(t)} = [m]$ , (2) or are in Phase I.2 with  $S_{\text{opt+}}^{(t)} = [m]$ . Then, for every  $j \in [d]$ , every  $i \in [m]$ , the following holds:

$$
\mid \mathbb {E} _ {x, y, \rho} \left[ y \mathbb {1} _ {\langle w _ {i} ^ {(t)}, x \rangle + \rho_ {i} \geq b ^ {(t)} z _ {j}} \right] \mid = O \left(\frac {1}{d}\right) .
$$

Proof. Proof is analogous to that of Lemma C.8, and the reason we no longer need the requirement  $|\langle w_i^{(t)},\mathbf{M}_j\rangle | = O(b^{(t)}\log \log \log d)$  is because, when invoking Lemma C.5, it suffices for us to apply Lemma C.5a for every non-zero values of  $z$  (as opposed to only those  $z = \Omega \left(\frac{1}{\log\log\log d}\right)$ ) which no longer requires  $\alpha \leq b$ .

Our next lemma shall be used to upper bound how  $\langle w_i^{(t)},\mathbf{M}_j\rangle$  can grown for every  $i\in [m]\backslash \mathcal{S}_{j,pot}^{(t)}$ .

Lemma C.10 (non-signal growth). Suppose we (1) either are in Phase I.1 with  $S_{ept}^{(t)} = [m]$ , (2) or are in Phase I.2 with  $S_{ept+}^{(t)} = [m]$ . Then, for every  $j \in [d]$ , every  $i \in [m] \setminus S_{j,pot}^{(t)}$ , the following holds:

$$
\left| \underset {x, y, \rho} {\mathbb {E}} \left[ y \mathbb {1} _ {\langle w _ {i} ^ {(t)}, x \rangle + \rho_ {i} \geq b ^ {(t)}} z _ {j} \right] \right| = O (\frac {\Gamma_ {t} \cdot \log d}{d})
$$

where  $\Gamma_t$  is given from Lemma C.7.

Proof of Lemma C.10. Suppose  $|z_{j}| = s$  and without loss of generality  $\langle w_i^{(t)},\mathbf{M}_j\rangle \geq 0$ . We choose  $\alpha = \langle w_i^{(t)},\mathbf{M}_j\rangle \cdot s$  as before. Then, we have  $\alpha \leq \sqrt{c_1 - c_2}\sigma_w^{(t)})\sqrt{\log d} = b^{(t)}\sqrt{1 - \frac{c_2}{c_1}}\leq b^{(t)}(1 - \frac{c_2}{2c_1})$  always holds.

Therefore, using the same notation as the proof of Lemma C.8, we always have the bound

$$
| \Delta_ {s} | \leq \left(e ^ {- \Omega (b ^ {2} / \sigma_ {\rho} ^ {2})} + \Gamma_ {t}\right) \left(O (\frac {\alpha}{\sigma_ {\rho}}) + L _ {y}\right) + \Gamma_ {t, y}
$$

Plugging in the parameters we finish the proof.

Our final lemma shall be used to upper bound how  $\langle w_i^{(t)},\mathbf{M}_j\rangle$  can grown with respect to the noise  $\xi$  in the input.

Lemma C.11 (noise growth). For every  $i \in [m]$ , every  $j \in [d]$ , the following holds:

$$
\left| \underset {x, y, \rho} {\mathbb {E}} \left[ y \mathbb {1} _ {\langle w _ {i} ^ {(t)}, x \rangle + \rho_ {i} \geq b ^ {(t)}} \langle \xi , \mathbf {M} _ {j} \rangle \right] \right| = O \left(\Gamma_ {t} \frac {\sigma_ {x} ^ {2}}{d \sigma_ {\rho} ^ {(t)}} | \langle w _ {i} ^ {(t)}, \mathbf {M} _ {j} \rangle |\right)
$$

Proof of Lemma C.11. We can define  $\alpha = |\langle \mathbf{M}_j,\xi \rangle \rangle |$  and study

$$
\Delta_ {s} := \mathbb {E} _ {x, y, \rho} \left[ y \mathbf {1} _ {\langle w _ {i} ^ {(t)}, x \rangle + \rho_ {i} \geq b ^ {(t)}} \operatorname {s i g n} \left(\langle \xi , \mathbf {M} _ {j} \rangle\right) \mid | \langle \xi , \mathbf {M} _ {j} \rangle | = \alpha \right] \tag {C.8}
$$

This time we have  $L = L_{y} = 0$ ,  $\Gamma_{y} = 0$ ,  $V = \mathbb{E}[S_2^2] = O((\sigma_w^{(t)})^2)$ , so applying Lemma C.5 we have,

when  $\alpha \leq b^{(t)} / 4, |\Delta_s| \leq \frac{|\langle w_i^{(t)},\mathbf{M}_j\rangle|\langle\mathbf{M}_j,\xi\rangle|}{\|\mathbf{M}_j\|_2\sigma_\rho^{(t)}} \cdot \left(e^{-\Omega \left(b^2 /\sigma_\rho^2\right)} + \Gamma_t\right)$  
- when  $\alpha > b^{(t)} / 4$  (which happens with exponentially small prob.),  $|\Delta_s| \leq O\left(\frac{\sigma_w^{(t)}}{\sigma_\rho}\right)$ .

Together, using the fact that  $\mathbb{E}[|\Delta_s|] \leq \sqrt{\mathbb{E}[\Delta_s^2]}$  we have:

$$
\left| \underset {x, y, \rho} {\mathbb {E}} \left[ y \mathbb {1} _ {\langle w _ {i} ^ {(t)}, x \rangle + \rho_ {i} \geq b ^ {(t)}} \langle \xi , \mathbf {M} _ {j} \rangle \right] \right| = O \left(\Gamma_ {t} \frac {\sigma_ {x} ^ {2}}{d \sigma_ {\rho} ^ {(t)}} | \langle w _ {i} ^ {(t)}, \mathbf {M} _ {j} \rangle |\right)
$$

# C.3.3 Proof of Theorem C.1

Suppose in Lemma C.8 the hidden constant is  $20C$  for the lower bound, that is,

$$
\underset {x, y, \rho} {\mathbb {E}} \left[ y \mathbb {1} _ {\langle w _ {i} ^ {(t)}, x \rangle + \rho_ {i} \geq b ^ {(t)} z _ {j}} \right] \geq \frac {2 0 C}{d}
$$

Proof of Theorem C.1. Let us prove by induction with respect to  $t$ . Suppose the properties all hold at  $t = 0$ . Recall from Fact A.1, for iteration  $t$ , for every neuron  $i \in [m]$

$$
\nabla_ {w _ {i}} \mathbf {L o s s} _ {t} (w ^ {(t)}; x, y, \rho) = - y \ell_ {t} ^ {\prime} (w ^ {(t)}; x, y, \rho) \left(\mathbb {1} _ {\langle w _ {i} ^ {(t)}, x \rangle + \rho_ {i} \geq b ^ {(t)}} + \mathbb {1} _ {- \langle w _ {i} ^ {(t)}, x \rangle + \rho_ {i} \geq b ^ {(t)}}\right) \cdot x.
$$

Using the bound on  $\| w_i^{(t)}\|_2$  (from  $S_{ept}^{(t)} = [m]$ ) and the fact  $\sigma_0 \leq \frac{1}{\mathrm{poly}(d)}$ , we know  $\ell_t'(w^{(t)}; x, y, \rho) = \frac{1}{2} \pm \frac{1}{\mathrm{poly}(d)}$ . Also, recall also from Lemma A.2 that

$$
\underset {x \sim \mathcal {D}, y = y (x), \rho} {\mathbb {E}} \left[ \nabla_ {w _ {i}} \mathbf {L o s s} _ {t} (w ^ {(t)}; x, y, \rho) \right] = \nabla_ {w _ {i}} \widetilde {\mathbf {L o s s}} _ {t} (w ^ {(t)}) \pm \frac {1}{\mathsf {p o l y} (d)}.
$$

Together, we have a clean formulation for our gradient update rule:

$$
w _ {i} ^ {(t + 1)} = w _ {i} ^ {(t)} (1 - \eta \lambda - \eta \lambda \| w _ {i} ^ {(t)} \| _ {2}) + \underset {x, y = y (x), \rho} {\mathbb {E}} \left[ y \left(\mathbb {1} _ {\langle w _ {i} ^ {(t)}, x \rangle + \rho_ {i} \geq b ^ {(t)}} + \mathbb {1} _ {- \langle w _ {i} ^ {(t)}, x \rangle + \rho_ {i} \geq b ^ {(t)}}\right) \cdot x \right] \pm \frac {\eta}{\mathrm {p o l y} (d)}.
$$

and as a result for every  $j \in [d]$

$$
\begin{array}{l} \langle w _ {i} ^ {(t + 1)}, \mathbf {M} _ {j} \rangle = \langle w _ {i} ^ {(t)}, \mathbf {M} _ {j} \rangle (1 - \eta \lambda - \eta \lambda \| w _ {i} ^ {(t)} \| _ {2}) \pm \frac {\eta}{\operatorname {p o l y} (d)} \\ + \underset {x, y = y (x), \rho} {\mathbb {E}} \left[ y \left(\mathbb {1} _ {\langle w _ {i} ^ {(t)}, x \rangle + \rho_ {i} \geq b ^ {(t)}} + \mathbb {1} _ {- \langle w _ {i} ^ {(t)}, x \rangle + \rho_ {i} \geq b ^ {(t)}}\right) \cdot \left(z _ {j} + \langle \xi , \mathbf {M} _ {j} \rangle\right) \right]. \tag {C.9} \\ \end{array}
$$

We now prove each statement separately (and note our proofs apply both to Phase I.1 and I.2).

1. For every  $i \notin S_{j,pot}^{(t)}$ , by substituting Lemma C.10 and Lemma C.11 into (C.9), we have

$$
\langle w _ {i} ^ {(t + 1)} - w _ {i} ^ {(t)}, \mathbf {M} _ {j} \rangle \leq \frac {\eta \cdot e ^ {- \Omega (\log^ {1 / 4} d)}}{d} \ll \sqrt {c _ {1} - c _ {2}} \left(\sigma_ {w} ^ {(t + 1)} - \sigma_ {w} ^ {(t)}\right) \sqrt {\log d} \tag {C.10}
$$

so we also have  $\langle w_i^{(t + 1)},\mathbf{M}_j\rangle < \sqrt{c_1 - c_2}\sigma_w^{(t + 1)}\sqrt{\log d}$  and thus  $i\notin S_{j,pot}^{(t + 1)}$

2. For every  $i \in S_{j, sure}^{(t)}$ , suppose wlog  $\langle w_i^{(t)}, \mathbf{M}_j \rangle$  is positive. Then, either  $\langle w_i^{(t)}, \mathbf{M}_j \rangle > \Omega(b^{(t)} \log \log \log d)$  in such a case we still have  $\langle w_i^{(t+1)}, \mathbf{M}_j \rangle \geq \sqrt{c_1 + c_2} \sigma_w^{(t+1)} \sqrt{\log d}$ . Otherwise, if  $\langle w_i^{(t)}, \mathbf{M}_j \rangle \leq \Omega(b^{(t)} \log \log \log d)$  then by substituting Lemma C.8 and Lemma C.11 into (C.9), we have (using  $\sigma_0 \leq \frac{1}{\mathrm{poly}(d)}$  and  $\lambda \leq \frac{\log d}{d}$ )

$$
\langle w _ {i} ^ {(t + 1)}, \mathbf {M} _ {j} \rangle \geq (1 - \eta \lambda) \langle w _ {i} ^ {(t)}, \mathbf {M} _ {j} \rangle + \frac {2 0 C \eta}{d} \geq \langle w _ {i} ^ {(t)}, \mathbf {M} _ {j} \rangle + \sqrt {c _ {1} + c _ {2}} (\sigma_ {w} ^ {(t + 1)} - \sigma_ {w} ^ {(t)}) \sqrt {\log d}.
$$

so by induction we also have  $\langle w_i^{(t + 1)},\mathbf{M}_j\rangle \geq \sqrt{c_1 + c_2}\sigma_w^{(t + 1)}\sqrt{\log d}$ . Combining this with  $S_{j,pot}^{(0)}\supseteq S_{j,pot}^{(t + 1)}$ , we conclude that  $i\in S_{j,sure}^{(t + 1)}$ .

3. To check  $\mathcal{S}_{\text{opt}}^{(t + 1)} = [m]$ , we need to verify four things:

$\langle w_i^{(t)},\mathbf{M}_j\rangle^2\geq (c_1 - c_2)(\sigma_w^{(t)})^2\log d$

for at most  $O(1)$  many  $j \in [d]$ .

This is so because  $\mathcal{S}_{ept}^{(0)} = [m]$  and  $\mathcal{S}_{j,pot}^{(0)}\supseteq \mathcal{S}_{j,pot}^{(t + 1)}$

$\langle w_i^{(t + 1)},\mathbf{M}_j\rangle^2\geq 2(\sigma_w^{(t + 1)})^2\sqrt{\log d}$

for at most  $2^{-\sqrt{\log d}}d$  many  $j \in [d]$ .

This can be derived from (C.10) in the same way.

$|\langle w_i^{(t + 1)},\mathbf{M}_j\rangle |\leq \frac{\sigma_w^{(t + 1)}}{\log d}$

for at least  $\Omega\left(\frac{d}{\log d}\right)$  many  $j \in [d]$ .

This can be derived from (C.10) in the same way.

$\| w_i^{(t)}\| _2^2\leq 2(\sigma_w^{(t)})^2 d$

For every  $i\in [m]$ , suppose  $\mathrm{wlog}\langle w_i^{(t)},\mathbf{M}_j\rangle$  is positive. Then, by substituting Lemma C.9 and Lemma C.11 into (C.9), we have

$$
\langle w _ {i} ^ {(t + 1)} - w _ {i} ^ {(t)}, \mathbf {M} _ {j} \rangle \leq O (\frac {\eta}{d})
$$

Applying this formula for  $t + 1$  times, we derive that

$$
| \langle w _ {i} ^ {(t + 1)}, \mathbf {M} _ {j} \rangle | \leq O \left(\frac {\eta (t + 1)}{d}\right) + | \langle w _ {i} ^ {(0)}, \mathbf {M} _ {j} \rangle | \tag {C.11}
$$

and therefore applying this together with (C.10),

$$
\begin{array}{l} \| w_{i}^{(t + 1)}\|_{2}^{2} = \sum_{j: i\in \mathcal{S}_{j,pot}^{(0)}}|\langle w_{i}^{(t + 1)},\mathbf{M}_{j}\rangle |^{2} + \sum_{j: i\notin \mathcal{S}_{j,pot}^{(0)}}|\langle w_{i}^{(t + 1)},\mathbf{M}_{j}\rangle |^{2} \\ \stackrel {1} {\leq} 1. 5 \| w _ {i} ^ {(0)} \| _ {2} ^ {2} + O (1) \cdot \left(\frac {\eta (t + 1)}{d}\right) ^ {2} + d \cdot \left(\frac {\eta (t + 1)}{d}\right) ^ {2} \cdot e ^ {- \Omega (\log^ {1 / 4} d)} \leq 2 (\sigma_ {w} ^ {(t + 1)}) ^ {2} d \\ \end{array}
$$

(Above, inequality ① uses that there are at most  $O(1)$  indices  $j \in [d]$  such that  $i \in S_{j,pot}^{(0)}$ .)

4. Finally, to check  $\mathcal{S}_{ept+}^{(t+1)} = [m]$  for  $t \geq T_{\mathsf{a}} = \Theta\left(\frac{d\sigma_0\log^{2.5}d}{\eta}\right)$ , we first derive that

$$
\sigma_ {w} ^ {(t + 1)} = \sigma_ {0} + \Theta (\frac {\eta}{d \sqrt {\log d}}) \cdot (t + 1) \geq \sigma_ {0} \cdot \Omega (\log^ {2} d).
$$

- For every  $i \notin S_{j, pot}^{(t+1)}$ , (C.10) gives

$$
\begin{array}{l} | \langle w _ {i} ^ {(t + 1)}, \mathbf {M} _ {j} \rangle | \leq | \langle w _ {i} ^ {(0)}, \mathbf {M} _ {j} \rangle | + \frac {e ^ {- \Omega (\log^ {1 / 4} d)}}{d} \cdot \eta (t + 1) \\ \leq O (\sigma_ {0} \sqrt {\log d}) + \frac {e ^ {- \Omega (\log^ {1 / 4} d)}}{d} \cdot \eta (t + 1) \leq O (\frac {\sigma_ {w} ^ {(t + 1)}}{\log^ {1 . 5} d}) \\ \end{array}
$$

In particular, this together with  $\mathcal{S}_{\text{opt}}^{(0)} = [m]$  ensures that for every  $i \in [m]$ ,  $\left|\langle w_i^{(t+1)}, \mathbf{M}_j \rangle \right| \geq \frac{\sigma_w^{(t+1)}}{\log d}$  for at most  $O(1)$  many  $j \in [d]$ .

- For any  $i \in S_{j, pot}^{(0)}$ , using (C.11) we have

$$
\left| \langle w _ {i} ^ {(t + 1)}, \mathbf {M} _ {j} \rangle \right| \leq \left| \langle w _ {i} ^ {(0)}, \mathbf {M} _ {j} \rangle \right| + O \left(\frac {\eta (t + 1)}{d}\right) \leq O \left(\sigma_ {0} \sqrt {\log d}\right) + O \left(\frac {\eta (t + 1)}{d}\right) = \Theta \left(\sigma_ {w} ^ {(t + 1)} \sqrt {\log d}\right) \tag {C.12}
$$

Using this together with the previous item, as well as  $|\mathcal{S}_{j,pot}^{(0)}| \leq O(1)$ , we have  $\| w_i^{(t + 1)}\|^2 \leq O\left(\frac{(\sigma_w^{(t + 1)})^2d}{\log^3d}\right)$ .

Putting them together we have  $\mathcal{S}_{\text{opt+}}^{(t+1)} = [m]$  for every  $t \geq T_{\mathsf{a}}$ .

5. After  $t = T_{\mathsf{b}}$  iterations, we have  $\sigma_w^{(t)} = \Theta (\sigma_0 + \frac{\eta}{d\sqrt{\log d}}\cdot T_{\mathsf{b}})$ , for every  $i\notin S_{j,pot}^{(0)}$ , by Lemma C.10 and Lemma C.11

$$
| \langle w _ {i} ^ {(t)}, \mathbf {M} _ {j} \rangle | \leq | \langle w _ {i} ^ {(T _ {\mathsf {a}})}, \mathbf {M} _ {j} \rangle | + \frac {O (k / d) \cdot \sqrt {\log d}}{d} \cdot \eta (T _ {\mathsf {b}} - T _ {\mathsf {a}}) \leq O (\sigma_ {w} ^ {(t)} \cdot \frac {k \log d}{d})
$$

Combining this with (C.11), we immediately have

$$
\begin{array}{l} \| w_{i}^{(t)}\|^{2} = \sum_{j: i\in \mathcal{S}_{j,pot}^{(0)}}|\langle w_{i}^{(t + 1)},\mathbf{M}_{j}\rangle |^{2} + \sum_{j: i\notin \mathcal{S}_{j,pot}^{(0)}}|\langle w_{i}^{(t + 1)},\mathbf{M}_{j}\rangle |^{2} \\ \leq (\sigma_ {w} ^ {(t)}) ^ {2} \cdot O (\frac {k ^ {2} \log^ {2} d}{d} + \log d) \\ \end{array}
$$

This implies  $\mathcal{S}_{ept + + }^{(t)} = [m]$  and  $\mathcal{S}_{j,pot}^{(0)}\supseteq \mathcal{S}_{j,pot + }^{(t)}$  at this iteration  $t$ .

![](images/81b63781047edb2540aa8d56986f27a3d6eeeb6b3f63177a5199be38d8cb00f8.jpg)

# C.4 Phase II: Signal Growth After Winning Lottery

Definition C.12. In phase II we make the following parameter choices.

- In Phase II, we pick  $b^{(t)} = \sqrt{c_1} \sigma_w^{(t)} \sqrt{\log d}$  and  $\sigma_{\rho}^{(t)} = \sigma_w^{(t)} \cdot \frac{(\log \log \log d)^3}{\sqrt{\log d}}$ .

We grow  $b^{(t + 1)} = b^{(t)} + \frac{C\eta}{d}$  as before (the same as phase I.2 in Definition C.6) for each iteration, but stop growing  $b^{(t)}$  when it reaches a threshold  $b^{(t)} = \beta \Xi_2^2$ .

We first introduce a notation on a (high-probability) version of the coordinate Lipschitz continuity.

Definition C.13 (coordinate Lipschitzness). At every iteration  $t$ , for every  $j \in [d]$ , we define  $L_{t,j} > e^{-\Omega(\log^2 d)}$  to be the smallest value such that w.p. at least  $1 - e^{-\Omega(\log^2 d)}$  over the choice of  $\{z_{j'}\}_{j' \neq j}$  and  $\xi$ , for every  $z \in [-1,1]$  and  $z = (z_1, \dots, z_{j-1}, z, z_{j+1}, \dots, z_d)$ ,  $z' = (z_1, \dots, z_{j-1}, 0, z_{j+1}, \dots, z_d)$ ,  $x = \mathbf{M}z + \xi$  and  $x' = \mathbf{M}z' + \xi$ :

$$
\left| f _ {t} (x) - f _ {t} \left(x ^ {\prime}\right) \right| \leq L _ {t, j} | z | .
$$

# C.4.1 Growth Lemmas

In this subsection, we provide new growth lemmas Lemma C.14, Lemma C.15, Lemma C.16, Lemma C.17 that are specific to Phase II, to replace the user of the old growth lemmas Lemma C.8, Lemma C.9, Lemma C.10, Lemma C.11 from Phase I.

Lemma C.14 (signal growth II). Suppose we  $\mathcal{S}_{\text{opt+}}^{(t)} = \mathcal{S}_{\text{opt++}}^{(t)} = [m]$ . Then, for every  $j \in [d]$ , every  $i \in \mathcal{S}_{j,\text{sure}}^{(t)}$ , the following holds:

$$
\underset {x, y, \rho} {\mathbb {E}} \left[ y \ell_ {t} ^ {\prime} (w ^ {(t)}; x, y, \rho) \mathbb {1} _ {\langle w _ {i} ^ {(t)}, x \rangle + \rho_ {i} \geq b ^ {(t)} z _ {j}} \right] = \Theta \left(\frac {1}{d}\right) \pm O \left(\frac {L _ {t , j}}{d} + \frac {\sqrt {k} \sigma_ {\rho} ^ {(t)} \log d}{d} + \frac {\sqrt {k}}{\beta d ^ {3 / 2}}\right)
$$

Proof of Lemma C.14. First, without loss of generality, assuming that  $\mathsf{sign}(\langle w_i^{(t)},\mathbf{M}_j\rangle) = \mathsf{sign}(w_j^\star) =$

1. Let us define  $z' = (z_1, \dots, z_{j-1}, 0, z_{j+1}, \dots, z_d)$  and  $x' = \mathbf{M}z' + \xi$ . Define

$$
\begin{array}{l} f _ {t, i} (w ^ {(t)}; x, \rho) \stackrel {\mathrm {d e f}} {=} \sum_ {j \neq i} \left(\mathsf {R e L U} (\langle w _ {j} ^ {(t)}, x \rangle + \rho_ {j} + b _ {j} ^ {(t)}) - \mathsf {R e L U} (- \langle w _ {j} ^ {(t)}, x \rangle + \rho_ {j} + b _ {j} ^ {(t)})\right) \\ + \left(\operatorname {R e L U} \left(\left\langle w _ {i} ^ {(t)}, x \right\rangle + b _ {i} ^ {(t)}\right) - \operatorname {R e L U} \left(- \left\langle w _ {i} ^ {(t)}, x \right\rangle + b _ {i} ^ {(t)}\right)\right) \\ \end{array}
$$

$$
\ell_ {t, i} ^ {\prime} (w ^ {(t)}; x, y, \rho) \stackrel {\mathrm {d e f}} {=} \frac {d}{d s} [ \log (1 + e ^ {s}) ] | _ {s = - y f _ {t, i} (w ^ {(t)}; x, \rho)}
$$

Now, since  $\frac{e^s}{1 + e^s}$  is an  $O(1)$ -Lipschitz function in  $s$ , we know that w.p. at least  $1 - e^{-\Omega (\log^2 d)}$

$$
\left| \ell_ {t} ^ {\prime} \left(w ^ {(t)}; x, y, \rho\right) - \ell_ {t, i} ^ {\prime} \left(w ^ {(t)}; x ^ {\prime}, y, \rho\right) \right| = O \left(L _ {t, j} \cdot \left| z _ {j} \right| + \sigma_ {\rho} ^ {(t)} \log d\right)
$$

and this implies that

$$
\begin{array}{l} \left| \underset {x, y, \rho} {\mathbb {E}} \left[ y (\ell_ {t} ^ {\prime} (w ^ {(t)}; x, y, \rho) - \ell_ {t, i} ^ {\prime} (w ^ {(t)}; x ^ {\prime}, y, \rho)) \mathbb {1} _ {\langle w _ {i} ^ {(t)}, x \rangle + \rho_ {i} \geq b ^ {(t)} z _ {j}} \right] \right| \\ \leq O \left(L _ {t, j} \mathbb {E} [ z _ {j} ^ {2} ] + \sigma_ {\rho} ^ {(t)} \log d \cdot \mathbb {E} [ | z _ {j} | ]\right) + e ^ {- \Omega (\log^ {2} d)} \\ = O \left(\frac {L _ {t , j}}{d} + \frac {\sigma_ {\rho} ^ {(t)} \log d \cdot \sqrt {k}}{d}\right) + e ^ {- \Omega (\log^ {2} d)} \\ \end{array}
$$

so we only need to bound  $\mathbb{E}_{x,y,\rho}\left[y\ell_{t,i}^{\prime}(w^{(t)};x^{\prime},y,\rho)\mathbb{1}_{\langle w_i^{(t)},x\rangle +\rho_i\geq b^{(t)}}z_j\right]$ .

Let us first focus on the case that  $|z_{j}| = 1$ . As before, since  $j \in S_{j,sure}^{(t)}$ , we have  $\langle w_{i}^{(t)}, \mathbf{M}_{j} \rangle \geq b^{(t)} \sqrt{1 + \frac{c_{2}}{c_{1}}}$  so applying Lemma C.7,

$$
\begin{array}{l} \Pr [ \langle w _ {i} ^ {(t)}, x \rangle + \rho_ {i} \geq b ^ {(t)} \mid z _ {j} = 1 ] \geq 1 - 2 \Gamma_ {t} = 1 - o (1) \\ \Pr [ \langle w _ {i} ^ {(t)}, x \rangle + \rho_ {i} \geq b ^ {(t)} \mid z _ {j} = - 1 ] \leq 2 \Gamma_ {t} = o (1) \\ \end{array}
$$

This means

$$
\underset {x, y, \rho} {\mathbb {E}} \left[ y \ell_ {t, i} ^ {\prime} (w ^ {(t)}; x ^ {\prime}, y, \rho) \mathbb {1} _ {\langle w _ {i} ^ {(t)}, x \rangle + \rho_ {i} \geq b ^ {(t)}} z _ {j} \mid | z _ {j} | = 1 \right] \geq \frac {1}{2} \underset {x, y, \rho} {\mathbb {E}} \left[ y \ell_ {t, i} ^ {\prime} (w ^ {(t)}; x ^ {\prime}, y, \rho) \mid z _ {j} = 1 \right] - o (1)
$$

Now recall  $y(z_{j},z) = \mathrm{sign}(w_{j}^{\star}z_{j} + \langle w^{\star},z\rangle)$ .

- When  $|\langle w^{\star}, z \rangle| > |w_{j}^{\star}|$ , then we know that  $y(z_{j}, z)$  and  $y(z_{j}, -z)$  have different signs, but  $\ell_{t,i}'(w^{(t)}; x', y, \rho) = \ell_{t,i}'(w^{(t)}; -x', -y, \rho)$  remains the same if we flip  $z$  to  $-z$ . By symmetry, we have

$$
\mathbb {E} _ {x, y, \rho} \left[ y \ell_ {t, i} ^ {\prime} \left(w ^ {(t)}; x ^ {\prime}, y, \rho\right) \mid z _ {j} = 1 \wedge | \langle w ^ {\star}, z \rangle | > | w _ {j} ^ {\star} | \right] = 0
$$

- Suppose otherwise  $|\langle w^{\star}, z \rangle| \leq |w_{j}^{\star}|$ . Since  $|w_{j}^{\star}| = \Theta(1)$ , by Lemma H.1, this event happens with at least constant probability. When it happens, we have  $y(z_{j}, z) = y(z_{j}, -z) = +1$ , but  $\ell_{t,i}'(w^{(t)}; x', y, \rho) + \ell_{t,i}'(w^{(t)}; -x', y, \rho) = 1$ . Therefore,

$$
\underset {x, y, \rho} {\mathbb {E}} \left[ y \ell_ {t, i} ^ {\prime} (w ^ {(t)}; x ^ {\prime}, y, \rho) \mid z _ {j} = 1 \land | \langle w ^ {\star}, z \rangle | \leq | w _ {j} ^ {\star} | \right] \geq \frac {1}{2}
$$

Together, we have

$$
\underset {x, y, \rho} {\mathbb {E}} \left[ y \ell_ {t, i} ^ {\prime} \left(w ^ {(t)}; x ^ {\prime}, y, \rho\right) \mathbb {1} _ {\left\langle w _ {i} ^ {(t)}, x \right\rangle + \rho_ {i} \geq b ^ {(t)}} z _ {j} \mid | z _ {j} | = 1 \right] = \Omega (1) \tag {C.13}
$$

Next, conditioning on  $|z_j| = s$  for some  $0 < s < 1$ , we can apply Lemma C.5 with  $Y = y\ell_{t,i}'(w^{(t)};x',y,\rho)$  on  $s = \mathsf{sign}(z_j)$ ,  $\alpha = \langle w_i^{(t)},\mathbf{M}_j\rangle z_j$ ,  $S_1 = z$ ,  $S_2 = \sum_{j'\neq j}\langle w_i^{(t)},\mathbf{M}_{j'}\rangle z_{j'}$  and  $\rho = \rho_{i}$ .

Since  $\ell_t^\prime (w^{(t)};x',y,\rho)\geq 0$ , we can conclude that  $Y$  is a monotone non-decreasing function of in  $s$ . One can verify that  $L = O(s)$  using Lemma H.1. Moreover, since  $i\in S_{ept + + }^{(t)}$ , we have  $\frac{\mathbb{E}[S_2^2]}{\left(\sigma_\rho^{(t)}\right)^2}\leq \frac{1}{d\beta^2}$ . Let us denote by

$$
\Delta_ {s} := \underset {x, y, \rho} {\mathbb {E}} \left[ y \ell_ {t, i} ^ {\prime} (w ^ {(t)}; x ^ {\prime}, y, \rho) \mathbb {1} _ {\langle w _ {i} ^ {(t)}, x \rangle + \rho_ {i} \geq b ^ {(t)}} \mathsf {s i g n} (z _ {j}) | | z _ {j} | = s \right]
$$

so according to Lemma C.5 we have

$$
\Omega \left(\frac {1}{\beta \sqrt {d}}\right) \leq \Delta_ {s} = O \left(\frac {1}{\beta \sqrt {d}} + s\right)
$$

This implies, using  $\mathbb{E}[|z_j|] \leq \frac{\sqrt{k}}{d}$ , that

$$
\underset {x, y, \rho} {\mathbb {E}} \left[ y \ell_ {t, i} ^ {\prime} (w ^ {(t)}; x ^ {\prime}, y, \rho) \mathbb {1} _ {\langle w _ {i} ^ {(t)}, x \rangle + \rho_ {i} \geq b ^ {(t)}} z _ {j} \cdot \mathbb {1} _ {| z _ {j} | <   1} \right] \leq \mathbb {E} [ \Delta_ {z _ {j}} \cdot | z _ {j} | \cdot \mathbb {1} _ {| z _ {j} | <   1} ] \leq O \left(\frac {\mathbb {E} [ | z _ {j} | ]}{\beta \sqrt {d}} + \frac {1}{d}\right) \leq O \left(\frac {\sqrt {k}}{\beta d ^ {1 . 5}} + \frac {1}{d}\right)
$$

$$
\underset {x, y, \rho} {\mathbb {E}} \left[ y \ell_ {t, i} ^ {\prime} (w ^ {(t)}; x ^ {\prime}, y, \rho) \mathbb {1} _ {\langle w _ {i} ^ {(t)}, x \rangle + \rho_ {i} \geq b ^ {(t)}} z _ {j} \cdot \mathbb {1} _ {| z _ {j} | <   1} \right] \geq \mathbb {E} [ \Delta_ {z _ {j}} | z _ {j} | \cdot \mathbb {1} _ {| z _ {j} | <   1} ] \geq - \Omega \left(\frac {\mathbb {E} [ | z _ {j} | ]}{\beta \sqrt {d}}\right) \geq - \Omega \left(\frac {\sqrt {k}}{\beta d ^ {1 . 5}}\right)
$$

Combining this with (C.13), and using  $\mathbf{Pr}[|z_j| = 1] \geq \Omega(1 / d)$  finishes the proof.

![](images/ab8aeb08470c945c5b759a080e8547f3fd206ccc23696d7576ce806721e9485f.jpg)

Similarly, we have the following Lemma

Lemma C.15 (maximum growth II). Suppose we  $\mathcal{S}_{\text{opt+}}^{(t)} = \mathcal{S}_{\text{opt++}}^{(t)} = [m]$ . Then, for every  $j \in [d]$ , every  $i \in [m]$ , the following holds:

$$
\left| \mathbb {E} _ {\left. x, y, \rho \right\rbrack} \left[ y \ell_ {t} ^ {\prime} (w ^ {(t)}; x, y, \rho) \mathbb {1} _ {\langle w _ {i} ^ {(t)}, x \rangle + \rho_ {i} \geq b ^ {(t)} z _ {j}} \right] \right| = O \left(\frac {1 + L _ {t , j}}{d} + \frac {\sqrt {k} \sigma_ {\rho} ^ {(t)} \log d}{d} + \frac {\sqrt {k}}{\beta d ^ {3 / 2}}\right)
$$

Lemma C.16 (non-signal growth II). Suppose we  $S_{\text{opt+}}^{(t)} = S_{\text{opt++}}^{(t)} = [m]$ . Then, for every  $j \in [d]$ , every  $i \notin S_{j,\text{pot}}^{(t)}$  and  $i \in [m]$ , the following holds:

$$
\left| \mathop {\mathbb {E}} _ {x, y, \rho} \left[ y \ell_ {t} ^ {\prime} (w ^ {(t)}; x, y, \rho) \mathbb {1} _ {\langle w _ {i} ^ {(t)}, x \rangle + \rho_ {i} \geq b ^ {(t)} z _ {j}} \right] \right| \leq \Gamma_ {t} \cdot O \left(\frac {\log d + L _ {t , j}}{d} + \frac {\sqrt {k} \sigma_ {\rho} ^ {(t)} \log d}{d}\right)
$$

Proof of Lemma C.16. In the same notation as the proof of Lemma C.14, we have

$$
\begin{array}{l} \left| \mathbb {E} _ {x, y, \rho} \left[ y \left(\ell_ {t} ^ {\prime} \left(w ^ {(t)}; x, y, \rho\right) - \ell_ {t, i} ^ {\prime} \left(x ^ {\prime}, y\right)\right) \mathbb {1} _ {\langle w _ {i} ^ {(t)}, x \rangle + \rho_ {i} \geq b ^ {(t)} z _ {j}} \right] \right| \\ \leq O \left(L _ {t, j} \mathbb {E} [ z _ {j} ^ {2} \cdot \mathbb {1} _ {\langle w _ {i} ^ {(t)}, x \rangle + \rho_ {i} \geq b ^ {(t)} ]} + \sigma_ {\rho} ^ {(t)} \log d \cdot \mathbb {E} [ | z _ {j} | \cdot \mathbb {1} _ {\langle w _ {i} ^ {(t)}, x \rangle + \rho_ {i} \geq b ^ {(t)} ]}\right) + e ^ {- \Omega (\log^ {2} d)} \\ \leq \Gamma_ {t} O \left(\frac {L _ {t , j}}{d} + \frac {\sigma_ {\rho} ^ {(t)} \log d \cdot \sqrt {k}}{d}\right) + e ^ {- \Omega (\log^ {2} d)} \\ \end{array}
$$

where the last inequality uses Lemma C.7 and the fact  $i \notin S_{j,pot}^{(t)}$  (which, as before, implies if we choose  $\alpha = \langle w_i^{(t)}, \mathbf{M}_j \rangle \cdot z$  then  $\alpha^2 \leq (c_1 - c_2)(\sigma_w^{(t)})^2 \log d \leq \left(\frac{b^{(t)}}{4}\right)^2$ ).

Thus, we only need to bound

$$
\underset {x, y, \rho} {\mathbb {E}} \left[ y \ell_ {t, i} ^ {\prime} (x ^ {\prime}, y) \mathbb {1} _ {\langle w _ {i} ^ {(t)}, x \rangle + \rho_ {i} \geq b ^ {(t)}} z _ {j} \right].
$$

Conditioning on  $|z_j| = s$  for some  $0 < s \leq 1$ , we can apply Lemma C.5 again with  $Y = y\ell_{t,i}'(x',y)$  on  $s = \mathsf{sign}(z_j)$ ,  $\alpha = \langle w_i^{(t)},\mathbf{M}_j\rangle z_j$ ,  $S_1 = z$ ,  $S_2 = \sum_{j'\neq j}\langle w_i^{(t)},\mathbf{M}_{j'}\rangle z_{j'}$  and  $\rho = \rho_{i}$ . This time, we use

$\Gamma_y = \frac{1}{d^{10}}$  and  $L_{y}\leq O(z\cdot \log^{1 / 4}d)$  . Define

$$
\Delta_ {s} := \underset {x, y, \rho} {\mathbb {E}} \left[ y \ell_ {t, i} ^ {\prime} (x ^ {\prime}, y) \mathbb {1} _ {\langle w _ {i} ^ {(t)}, x \rangle + \rho_ {i} \geq b ^ {(t)}} \operatorname {s i g n} (z _ {j}) \mid | z _ {j} | = s \right]
$$

Since  $\alpha <  \frac{b^{(t)}}{4}$  , Lemma C.5 tells us

$$
\left| \Delta_ {s} \right| \leq \left(e ^ {- \Omega \left(b ^ {2} / \sigma_ {\rho} ^ {2}\right)} + \Gamma_ {t}\right) \left(O \left(\frac {\alpha}{\sigma_ {\rho}}\right) + L _ {y}\right) + \Gamma_ {y} \leq O \left(\Gamma_ {t} \log d\right) \cdot z
$$

and therefore

$$
\left| \mathbb {E} _ {x, y, \rho} \left[ y \ell_ {t, i} ^ {\prime} (x ^ {\prime}, y) \mathbb {1} _ {\langle w _ {i} ^ {(t)}, x \rangle + \rho_ {i} \geq b ^ {(t)} z _ {j}} \right] \right| = \left| \mathbb {E} [ \Delta_ {z _ {j}} | z _ {j} | ] \right| \leq O (\Gamma_ {t} \log d) \cdot \mathbb {E} [ z _ {j} ^ {2} ] = O (\frac {\Gamma_ {t} \log d}{d})
$$

Combining this with (C.13), and using  $\mathbf{Pr}[|z_j| = 1] \geq \Omega(1 / d)$  finishes the proof.

![](images/f90674591824837ec66603c6c62f5d4459f8211a2265920029ccd7e190ab6615.jpg)

Finally, we derive a more fine-grind bound for the noise:

Lemma C.17 (noise growth II). Suppose we  $S_{\text{opt+}}^{(t)} = S_{\text{opt++}}^{(t)} = [m]$ . Then, for every  $j \in [d]$ ,

(a) for every  $j \in [d]$ ,

$$
\left| \underset {x, y, \rho} {\mathbb {E}} \left[ y \ell_ {t} ^ {\prime} (w ^ {(t)}; x, y, \rho) \mathbb {1} _ {\langle w _ {i} ^ {(t)}, x \rangle + \rho_ {i} \geq b ^ {(t)}} \langle \xi , \mathbf {M} _ {j} \rangle \right] \right| = O \left(\frac {\Gamma_ {t}}{d \sigma_ {\rho} ^ {(t)}} | \langle w _ {i} ^ {(t)}, \mathbf {M} _ {j} \rangle | \sigma_ {x} ^ {2} + \frac {\Gamma_ {t} L _ {t , j} \sigma_ {x} ^ {2}}{d} + e ^ {- \Omega (\log^ {2} d)}\right)
$$

(b) suppose also  $\mathcal{S}_{j,pot}^{(0)}\supseteq \mathcal{S}_{j,pot + }^{(t)}$  for every  $j\in [d]$ , then

$$
\sum_ {j \in [ d ]} \left| \underset {x, y, \rho} {\mathbb {E}} \left[ y \ell_ {t} ^ {\prime} (w ^ {(t)}; x, y, \rho) \mathbb {1} _ {\langle w _ {i} ^ {(t)}, x \rangle + \rho_ {i} \geq b ^ {(t)}} \langle \xi , \mathbf {M} _ {j} \rangle \right] \right| \leq O \left(\frac {k ^ {3} \Xi_ {2} ^ {4}}{d ^ {2}} \sigma_ {x} ^ {2} + e ^ {- \Omega (\log^ {2} d)}\right)
$$

Proof of Lemma C.17. We can first decompose the noise  $\xi$  into

$$
\xi = \left(\mathbf {I} - \mathbf {M} _ {j} \mathbf {M} _ {j} ^ {\top}\right) \xi + \langle \mathbf {M} _ {j}, \xi \rangle \mathbf {M} _ {j} =: \xi_ {j} ^ {\prime} + \langle \mathbf {M} _ {j}, \xi \rangle \mathbf {M} _ {j}.
$$

Let us define  $x_{j}^{\prime} = \mathbf{M}z + \xi_{j}^{\prime}$ .

- On one hand we have with probability at least  $1 - \Gamma_t$ ,  $|\langle w_i^{(t)},x_j^{\prime}\rangle |\leq \frac{b^{(t)}}{10}$  (using a variant of Lemma C.7). Using the randomness of  $\langle \xi ,\mathbf{M}_j\rangle$  and  $\rho_{i}$  we also have with probability at least  $1 - e^{-\Omega (\log^2 d)}$  it satisfies  $|\langle w_i^{(t)},\mathbf{M}_j\rangle \cdot \langle \mathbf{M}_j,\xi \rangle | + |\rho_i|\leq \frac{b^{(t)}}{10}$ . Therefore, with probability at least  $1 - \Gamma_t - e^{-\Omega (\log^2 d)}$ , we have  $\mathbb{1}_{\langle w_i^{(t)},x\rangle +\rho_i\geq b^{(t)}} = \mathbb{1}_{\langle w_i^{(t)},x_j^{\prime}\rangle +\rho_i\geq b^{(t)}}$ .  
- Otherwise, in the event that  $|\langle w_i^{(t)},x_j^{\prime}\rangle |\geq \frac{b^{(t)}}{10}$ , using the randomness of  $\rho_{i}$ , we have that

$$
\mathbf {P r} _ {\rho_ {i}} \left[ \mathbb {1} _ {\langle w _ {i} ^ {(t)}, x \rangle + \rho_ {i} \geq b ^ {(t)}} \neq \mathbb {1} _ {\langle w _ {i} ^ {(t)}, x _ {j} ^ {\prime} \rangle + \rho_ {i} \geq b ^ {(t)}} \right] \leq O \left(\frac {\mathbb {E} \left[ \left| \langle \xi , \mathbf {M} _ {j} \rangle \langle w _ {i} ^ {(t)} , \mathbf {M} _ {j} \rangle \right| \right]}{\sigma_ {\rho} ^ {(t)}}\right)
$$

Together, we have

$$
\begin{array}{l} \left| \underset {x, y, \rho} {\mathbb {E}} \left[ y \ell_ {t} ^ {\prime} (w ^ {(t)}; x, y, \rho) \left(\mathbb {1} _ {\langle w _ {i} ^ {(t)}, x \rangle + \rho_ {i} \geq b ^ {(t)}} - \mathbb {1} _ {\langle w _ {i} ^ {(t)}, x _ {j} ^ {\prime} \rangle + \rho_ {i} \geq b ^ {(t)}}\right) \langle \xi , \mathbf {M} _ {j} \rangle \right] \right| \\ \leq O \left(\Gamma_ {t} \frac {\mathbb {E} \left[ \left| \langle \xi , \mathbf {M} _ {j} \rangle^ {2} \langle w _ {i} ^ {(t)} , \mathbf {M} _ {j} \rangle \right| \right]}{\sigma_ {\rho} ^ {(t)}}\right) = O \left(\frac {\Gamma_ {t}}{d \sigma_ {\rho} ^ {(t)}} | \langle w _ {i} ^ {(t)}, \mathbf {M} _ {j} \rangle | \sigma_ {x} ^ {2}\right) \tag {C.14} \\ \end{array}
$$

Using the coordinate Lipscthiziness, we also have

$$
\begin{array}{l} \left| \underset {x, y, \rho} {\mathbb {E}} \left[ y (\ell_ {t} ^ {\prime} (w ^ {(t)}; x, y, \rho) - \ell_ {t} ^ {\prime} (x _ {j} ^ {\prime}, y)) \mathbb {1} _ {\langle w _ {i} ^ {(t)}, x _ {j} ^ {\prime} \rangle + \rho_ {i} \geq b ^ {(t)}} \langle \xi , \mathbf {M} _ {j} \rangle \right] \right| \\ \leq L _ {t, j} \mathbb {E} [ \langle \xi , \mathbf {M} _ {j} \rangle^ {2} ] \Pr [ \langle w _ {i} ^ {(t)}, x _ {j} ^ {\prime} \rangle + \rho_ {i} \geq b ^ {(t)} ] = O \left(\frac {\Gamma_ {t} L _ {t , j} \sigma_ {x} ^ {2}}{d}\right) \tag {C.15} \\ \end{array}
$$

Finally, we have  $\mathbb{E}_{x,y,\rho}\left[y\ell_t'(x_j',y)\mathbb{1}_{\langle w_i^{(t)},x_j'\rangle +\rho_i\geq b^{(t)}}\langle \xi ,\mathbf{M}_j\rangle \right] = 0$  . We can thus combine (C.14) and (C.15) to complete the proof of Lemma C.17a.

Next, we want to prove Lemma C.17b. We have: denote

$$
q _ {i ^ {\prime}} = (\mathbb {1} _ {\langle w _ {i ^ {\prime}} ^ {(t)}, \mathbf {M} z \rangle + \rho_ {i ^ {\prime}} + b _ {i ^ {\prime}} ^ {(t)} \geq - | b ^ {(t)} | / 1 0} + \mathbb {1} _ {- \langle w _ {i ^ {\prime}} ^ {(t)}, \mathbf {M} z \rangle + \rho_ {i ^ {\prime}} + b _ {i ^ {\prime}} ^ {(t)} \geq - | b ^ {(t)} | / 1 0}
$$

Since w.p. at least  $1 - e^{-\Omega (\log^2 d)}$ $\left|\langle w_{i'}^{(t)},\xi \rangle \right|\leq \frac{|b^{(t)}}{10}$  in this case, we know that

$$
