\begin{array}{l} \sum_ {j \in [ d ]} | \ell_ {t} ^ {\prime} (w ^ {(t)}; x, y, \rho) - \ell_ {t} ^ {\prime} (x _ {j} ^ {\prime}, y) | | \langle \xi , \mathbf {M} _ {j} \rangle | \\ \leq \sum_ {j \in [ d ]} \langle \mathbf {M} _ {j}, \xi \rangle^ {2} \cdot \sum_ {i ^ {\prime} \in [ m ]} | \langle w _ {i ^ {\prime}} ^ {(t)}, \mathbf {M} _ {j} \rangle | \cdot (\mathbb {1} _ {\langle w _ {i ^ {\prime}} ^ {(t)}, x \rangle + \rho_ {i ^ {\prime}} + b _ {i ^ {\prime}} ^ {(t)} \geq 0} + \mathbb {1} _ {- \langle w _ {i ^ {\prime}} ^ {(t)}, x \rangle + \rho_ {i ^ {\prime}} + b _ {i ^ {\prime}} ^ {(t)} \geq 0}) \\ \leq \sum_ {j \in [ d ]} \langle \mathbf {M} _ {j}, \xi \rangle^ {2} \cdot \sum_ {i ^ {\prime} \in [ m ]} | \langle w _ {i ^ {\prime}} ^ {(t)}, \mathbf {M} _ {j} \rangle | \cdot q _ {i ^ {\prime}} \\ \end{array}
$$

Note also we have:

$$
\begin{array}{l} \sum_ {j \in [ d ]} | \langle w _ {i ^ {\prime}} ^ {(t)}, \mathbf {M} _ {j} \rangle | \leq O (1) \cdot \| w _ {i ^ {\prime}} ^ {(t)} \| _ {2} + \sum_ {j: i ^ {\prime} \notin \mathcal {S} _ {j, p o t +}} | \langle w _ {i ^ {\prime}} ^ {(t)}, \mathbf {M} _ {j} \rangle | \\ \leq O (1) \cdot \frac {\sigma_ {w} ^ {(t)}}{\beta} + d \cdot \frac {k}{d \beta} b ^ {(t)} \leq 2 \frac {k}{\beta} \cdot b ^ {(t)} \\ \end{array}
$$

By Lemma C.19, we have that

$$
\begin{array}{l} \sum_ {j \in [ d ]} \left| \underset {x, y, \rho} {\mathbb {E}} \left[ y \left(\ell_ {t} ^ {\prime} \left(w ^ {(t)}; x, y, \rho\right) - \ell_ {t} ^ {\prime} \left(x _ {j} ^ {\prime}, y\right)\right) \mathbb {1} _ {\langle w _ {i} ^ {(t)}, x \rangle + \rho_ {i} \geq b ^ {(t)}} \langle \xi , \mathbf {M} _ {j} \rangle \right] \right| \\ \leq e ^ {- \Omega (\log^ {2} d)} + \sum_ {j \in [ d ]} \mathbb {E} _ {x, y, \rho} \left[ | (\ell_ {t} ^ {\prime} (w ^ {(t)}; x, y, \rho) - \ell_ {t} ^ {\prime} (x _ {j} ^ {\prime}, y)) | | \langle \xi , \mathbf {M} _ {j} \rangle | \mathbb {1} _ {\langle w _ {i} ^ {(t)}, x \rangle + \rho_ {i} \geq b ^ {(t)}} \right] \\ \leq e ^ {- \Omega (\log^ {2} d)} + \sum_ {j \in [ d ]} \mathbb {E} _ {x, y, \rho} \left[ | (\ell_ {t} ^ {\prime} (w ^ {(t)}; x, y, \rho) - \ell_ {t} ^ {\prime} (x _ {j} ^ {\prime}, y)) | | \langle \xi , \mathbf {M} _ {j} \rangle | q _ {i} \right] \\ \leq e ^ {- \Omega (\log^ {2} d)} + \sum_ {j \in [ d ]} \underset {x, y, \rho} {\mathbb {E}} \left[ \langle \mathbf {M} _ {j}, \xi \rangle^ {2} \cdot \sum_ {i ^ {\prime} \in [ m ]} | \langle w _ {i ^ {\prime}} ^ {(t)}, \mathbf {M} _ {j} \rangle | \cdot q _ {i ^ {\prime}} q _ {i} \right] \quad \dots \mathrm {t a k i n g e x p e c t a t i o n w . r . t .} \xi \mathrm {f i r s t}. \\ \leq e ^ {- \Omega (\log^ {2} d)} + O \left(\frac {\sigma_ {x} ^ {2}}{d}\right) \cdot \sum_ {j \in [ d ]} \mathbb {E} _ {x, y, \rho} \left[ \sum_ {i ^ {\prime} \in [ m ]} | \langle w _ {i ^ {\prime}} ^ {(t)}, \mathbf {M} _ {j} \rangle | \cdot q _ {i ^ {\prime}} q _ {i} \right] \\ \leq O \left(\frac {\sigma_ {x} ^ {2}}{d}\right) \cdot O \left(\frac {k}{\beta} b ^ {(t)} \cdot k \Xi_ {2}\right) \cdot O \left(\frac {k}{d}\right) + e ^ {- \Omega \left(\log^ {2} d\right)} \leq O \left(\frac {k ^ {3} \Xi_ {2} ^ {4}}{d ^ {2}} \sigma_ {x} ^ {2} + e ^ {- \Omega \left(\log^ {2} d\right)}\right) \tag {C.16} \\ \end{array}
$$

Next, similar to the (C.14), we also have

$$
\begin{array}{l} \sum_ {j \in [ d ]} \left| \mathbb {E} _ {x, y, \rho} \left[ y \ell_ {t} ^ {\prime} \left(x _ {j} ^ {\prime}, y\right) \left(\mathbb {1} _ {\langle w _ {i} ^ {(t)}, x \rangle + \rho_ {i} \geq b ^ {(t)}} - \mathbb {1} _ {\langle w _ {i} ^ {(t)}, x _ {j} ^ {\prime} \rangle + \rho_ {i} \geq b ^ {(t)}}\right) \langle \xi , \mathbf {M} _ {j} \rangle \right] \right| \\ \leq \sum_ {j \in [ d ]} O \left(\frac {\Gamma_ {t}}{d \sigma_ {\rho} ^ {(t)}} | \langle w _ {i} ^ {(t)}, \mathbf {M} _ {j} \rangle | \sigma_ {x} ^ {2}\right) \leq O \left(\frac {k}{d ^ {2} \sigma_ {\rho} ^ {(t)}} \sigma_ {x} ^ {2}\right) \cdot \sum_ {j \in [ d ]} | \langle w _ {i} ^ {(t)}, \mathbf {M} _ {j} \rangle | \leq O \left(\frac {k ^ {2} \log d}{d ^ {2} \beta} \sigma_ {x} ^ {2}\right) \tag {C.17} \\ \end{array}
$$

Combining (C.16) and (C.17) we finish the proof of Lemma C.17b.

![](images/6c7f50ac3ef067027159b36e7aba883fca2ff49683f27f652011d22f410ea011.jpg)

# C.4.2 Growth Coupling

We also have the following lemma which says, essentially, that all those neurons  $i \in [m]$  satisfying  $|\langle w_i^{(t)}, \mathbf{M}_j \rangle| \geq 2\sqrt{k} b^{(t)}$  for the same  $j$ , grows roughly in the same direction that is independent of  $i$ .

Lemma C.18 (growth coupling). Suppose at iteration  $t$ ,  $S_{\text{opt+}}^{(t)} = [m]$ . Then, for every  $j \in [d]$ , every  $i \in [m]$  such that  $|\langle w_i^{(t)}, \mathbf{M}_j \rangle| \geq 2\sqrt{k} b^{(t)}$ , we have:

$$
\underset {x, y, \rho} {\mathbb {E}} \left[ y \ell_ {t} ^ {\prime} (w ^ {(t)}; x, y, \rho) \big (\mathbb {1} _ {\langle w _ {i} ^ {(t)}, x \rangle + \rho_ {i} \geq b ^ {(t)}} + \mathbb {1} _ {- \langle w _ {i} ^ {(t)}, x \rangle + \rho_ {i} \geq b ^ {(t)}} \big) z _ {j} \right] = \underset {x, y, \rho} {\mathbb {E}} \left[ y \ell_ {t} ^ {\prime} (w ^ {(t)}; x, y, \rho) z _ {j} \right] \pm O \left(\frac {k ^ {3 / 2}}{d ^ {2}}\right)
$$

Proof of Lemma C.18. We first focus on the case when  $\langle w_i^{(t)},\mathbf{M}_j\rangle |\geq 2\sqrt{k} b^{(t)}$  is positive, and the reverse case is analogous. Conditional on  $|z_{j}| = s > 0$ , we know that  $s\geq \frac{1}{\sqrt{k}}$ . Thus, when  $|\langle w_i^{(t)},\mathbf{M}_j\rangle |\geq 2\sqrt{k} b^{(t)}$ ,  $|\langle w_i^{(t)},\mathbf{M}_j\rangle s|\geq 2b^{(t)}$ . Now, using  $S_{ept+}^{(t)} = [m]$  and Lemma C.7, we can conclude that

- when  $z_{j} > 0$ ,  $\mathbf{Pr}[\langle w_{i}^{(t)},x\rangle +\rho_{i}\geq b^{(t)}\mid z_{j} = s]\geq 1 - O\left(\frac{k}{d}\right)$ ;  
- when  $z_{j} < 0$ ,  $\mathbf{Pr}[\langle w_{i}^{(t)}, x \rangle + \rho_{i} \geq b^{(t)} \mid z_{j} = -s] \leq O\left(\frac{k}{d}\right)$ .

Thus, we can obtain

$$
\begin{array}{l} \underset {x, y, \rho} {\mathbb {E}} \left[ y \ell_ {t} ^ {\prime} (w ^ {(t)}; x, y, \rho) \mathbb {1} _ {\langle w _ {i} ^ {(t)}, x \rangle + \rho_ {i} \geq b ^ {(t)}} z _ {j} \right] = \underset {x, y, \rho} {\mathbb {E}} \left[ y \ell_ {t} ^ {\prime} (w ^ {(t)}; x, y, \rho) z _ {j} \mathbb {1} _ {z _ {j} > 0} \right] \pm O \left(\frac {k}{d}\right) \times \mathbb {E} | z _ {j} | \\ = \underset {x, y, \rho} {\mathbb {E}} \left[ y \ell_ {t} ^ {\prime} (w ^ {(t)}; x, y, \rho) z _ {j} \mathbb {1} _ {z _ {j} > 0} \right] \pm O \left(\frac {k ^ {3 / 2}}{d ^ {2}}\right) \\ \end{array}
$$

In the symmetric case, we also have

$$
\underset {x, y, \rho} {\mathbb {E}} \left[ y \ell_ {t} ^ {\prime} \left(w ^ {(t)}; x, y, \rho\right) \mathbb {1} _ {- \left\langle w _ {i} ^ {(t)}, x \right\rangle + \rho_ {i} \geq b ^ {(t)}} z _ {j} \right] = \underset {x, y, \rho} {\mathbb {E}} \left[ y \ell_ {t} ^ {\prime} \left(w ^ {(t)}; x, y, \rho\right) z _ {j} \mathbb {1} _ {z _ {j} <   0} \right] \pm O \left(\frac {k ^ {3 / 2}}{d ^ {2}}\right)
$$

# C.4.3 Activation Probabilities

Lemma C.19 (activation after  $\mathrm{ept + }$  ). Suppose  $\mathcal{S}_{\text{ept + }}^{(t)} = [m]$  and  $\mathcal{S}_{j,\text{pot}}^{(0)} \supseteq \mathcal{S}_{j,\text{pot + }}^{(t)}$  for every  $j \in [d]$ . Then, with probability at least  $1 - e^{-\Omega (\log^2 d)}$ ,

-  $\left|\left\{i\in [m]s.t.|\langle w_i^{(t)},x\rangle |\geq \frac{b^{(t)}}{10}\right\} \right|\leq O(k\Xi_2)$  
-  $\left|\left\langle w_i^{(t)},\sum_{j\in [d]:i\notin S_{j,pot + }^{(t)}}\mathbf{M}_jz_j + \xi \right\rangle \right|\leq \frac{b^{(t)}}{10}$  for every  $i\in [m]$ .

Proof. For every  $i \in [m]$  and  $j \in [d]$  with  $i \notin S_{j,pot+}^{(t)}$ , we have  $|\langle w_i^{(t)}, \mathbf{M}_j \rangle| \leq \frac{k}{d\beta} b^{(t)}$ . Therefore, by Bernstein's inequality (similar to Lemma C.4b), we know with probability at least  $1 - e^{-\Omega (\log^2 d)}$  for every  $i \in [m]$ ,

$$
\left| \left\langle w _ {i} ^ {(t)}, \sum_ {j \in [ d ]: i \notin S _ {j, p o t +} ^ {(t)}} \mathbf {M} _ {j} z _ {j} + \xi \right\rangle \right| \leq \frac {b ^ {(t)}}{1 0} \tag {C.18}
$$

With probability at least  $1 - e^{-\Omega (\log^2 d)}$  it satisfies  $\sum_{j\in [d]}\mathbb{1}_{z_j\neq 0}\leq O(k)$  (since each  $z_{j}\neq 0$  with probability at most  $O(\frac{k}{d}))$ . Therefore, denoting by  $\Lambda = \bigcup_{j\in [d]:}z_{j}\neq 0\mathcal{S}_{j,pot + }^{(t)}$ , we have  $|\Lambda |\leq O(k\Xi_2)$  (since every  $|\mathcal{S}_{j,pot + }^{(t)}|\leq \Xi_2$ ). Now, for any  $i\in [m]\setminus \Lambda$ , inequality (C.18) immediately gives

$$
\left| \left\langle w _ {i} ^ {(t)}, x \right\rangle \right| \leq \frac {b ^ {(t)}}{1 0}.
$$

Therefore, the number of  $i\in [m]$  satisfying  $\left|\left\langle w_i^{(t)},x\right\rangle \right|\geq \frac{b^{(t)}}{10}$  cannot be more than  $O(k\Xi_2)$ .

# C.4.4 Coordinate Lipscthizness Bound

Lemma C.20 (coordinate Lipschitzness). For every  $j \in [d]$ , let us define  $\gamma_j^{(t)} = \sum_{i \in S_{j,pot+}^{(t)}} |\langle w_i^{(t)}, \mathbf{M}_j \rangle|$ . Then, suppose  $S_{ept+}^{(t)} = [m]$  and suppose  $S_{j,pot}^{(0)} \supseteq S_{j,pot+}^{(t)}$ , we have

$$
L _ {t, j} \leq \gamma_ {j} ^ {(t)} + O \left(\frac {k}{\sqrt {d}}\right) \leq \gamma_ {j} ^ {(t)} + O \left(\frac {1}{\Xi_ {2} ^ {3}}\right)
$$

Proof of Lemma C.20. Clearly we have

$$
L _ {t, j} \leq \sum_ {i \in \mathcal {S} _ {j, p o t +} ^ {(0)}} | \langle w _ {i} ^ {(t)}, \mathbf {M} _ {j} \rangle | + \sum_ {i \notin \mathcal {S} _ {j, p o t +} ^ {(0)}} | \langle w _ {i} ^ {(t)}, \mathbf {M} _ {j} \rangle | \cdot (\mathbb {1} _ {\langle w _ {i} ^ {(t)}, x \rangle + \rho_ {i} \geq 0. 9 b _ {i} ^ {(t)}} + \mathbb {1} _ {- \langle w _ {i} ^ {(t)}, x \rangle + \rho_ {i} \geq 0. 9 b _ {i} ^ {(t)})
$$

By Lemma C.19 and the randomness of  $\rho_{i}$ , we know with probability at least  $1 - e^{-\Omega (\log^2 d)}$ , the number of activate neurons  $i\notin S_{j,pot + }^{(t)}$  —meaning  $\langle w_i^{(t)},x\rangle +\rho_i\geq 0.9b_i^{(t)}$  or  $-\langle w_i^{(t)},x\rangle +\rho_i\geq 0.9b_i^{(t)}$  —is at most  $O(k\Xi_2)$ . On the other hand, when  $i\notin S_{j,pot + }^{(t)}$ , we know that

$$
| \langle w _ {i} ^ {(t)}, {\bf M} _ {j} \rangle | \leq \frac {k}{d \beta} b ^ {(t)} \leq \frac {k \Xi_ {2} ^ {2}}{d}
$$

Therefore, together, the total contribution from these active neurons with  $i \notin S_{j,pot+}^{(t)}$  is at most  $\frac{k\Xi_2^2}{d} \cdot O(k\Xi_2) < O(\frac{k^2\Xi_2^3}{d}) < O\left(\frac{1}{\Xi_2^3}\right)$ . This completes the proof.

# C.4.5 Regularization

Following the same argument as (C.9) from phase I, we know at any iteration  $t$ , as long as  $S_{ept+}^{(t)} = [m]$ ,

$$
\begin{array}{l} \langle w _ {i} ^ {(t + 1)}, \mathbf {M} _ {j} \rangle = \langle w _ {i} ^ {(t)}, \mathbf {M} _ {j} \rangle (1 - \eta \lambda - \eta \lambda \| w _ {i} ^ {(t)} \| _ {2}) \pm \frac {\eta}{\mathrm {p o l y} (d)} \\ + \underset {x, y = y (x), \rho} {\mathbb {E}} \left[ y \ell_ {t} ^ {\prime} \left(w ^ {(t)}; x, y, \rho\right) \sum_ {i = 1} ^ {m} \left(\mathbb {1} _ {\langle w _ {i} ^ {(t)}, x \rangle + \rho_ {i} \geq b ^ {(t)}} + \mathbb {1} _ {- \langle w _ {i} ^ {(t)}, x \rangle + \rho_ {i} \geq b ^ {(t)}}\right) \cdot \left(z _ {j} + \langle \xi , \mathbf {M} _ {j} \rangle\right) \right] \quad . \tag {C.19} \\ \end{array}
$$

In this and the next subsection, we shall repeatedly apply growth lemmas to (C.19). Before doing so, let us note  $\sigma_{\rho}^{(t)} = o(b^{(t)}\log d)\leq o(\beta \Xi_2^2\log d)$ , so using our parameter choice of  $\beta$  and using  $k\leq d^{1 - c_0}$

$$
\frac {\sqrt {k} \sigma_ {\rho} ^ {(t)} \log d}{d} + \frac {\sqrt {k}}{\beta d ^ {3 / 2}} = o \left(\frac {\sqrt {k} \beta \Xi_ {2} ^ {2} \log^ {2} d}{d}\right) + \frac {\sqrt {k}}{\beta d ^ {3 / 2}} = o \left(\frac {1}{d}\right) \tag {C.20}
$$

This means, when applying the aforementioned growth lemmas Lemma C.14, Lemma C.15, Lemma C.16, the additional terms  $\frac{\sqrt{k}\sigma_{\rho}^{(t)}\log d}{d}$  and  $\frac{\sqrt{k}}{\beta d^{3 / 2}}$  are negligible.

We also have the following regularity lemma:

Lemma C.21 (regularity). For every  $T \leq d^{O(\log d)} / \eta$ , suppose  $S_{\text{opt+}}^{(t)} = S_{\text{opt++}}^{(t)} = [m]$  and  $S_{j,pot}^{(0)} \supseteq S_{j,pot+}^{(t)}$  hold for every  $t \leq T$  and  $j \in [d]$ . Then, we have for every  $t \leq T$ , with probability at least  $1 - e^{-\Omega (\log^2 d)}$ ,

$$
\forall j \in [ d ], \forall i \in [ m ] \colon L _ {t, j} \leq O \left(\Xi_ {2} ^ {2}\right), \| w _ {i} ^ {(t)} \| _ {2} \leq O \left(\Xi_ {2} ^ {2}\right), | f _ {t} (x) | \leq O \left(\Xi_ {2} ^ {2} \log d\right).
$$

Proof of Lemma C.21. By substituting Lemma C.15, Lemma C.17a and (C.20) into (C.19), we have for every  $j \in [d]$ :

$$
\begin{array}{l} | \langle w _ {i} ^ {(t + 1)}, \mathbf {M} _ {j} \rangle | \leq | \langle w _ {i} ^ {(t)}, \mathbf {M} _ {j} \rangle | (1 - \eta \lambda \| w _ {i} ^ {(t)} \| _ {2}) + \eta O \left(\frac {1 + L _ {t , j}}{d}\right) \\ \leq | \langle w _ {i} ^ {(t)}, \mathbf {M} _ {j} \rangle | \left(1 - \eta \lambda | \langle w _ {i} ^ {(t)}, \mathbf {M} _ {j} \rangle |\right) + \eta O \left(\frac {1 + L _ {t , j}}{d}\right) \\ \end{array}
$$

Summing up over all  $i \in S_{j,pot}^{(0)}$ , and using Cauchy-Schwarz inequality together with  $|\mathcal{S}_{j,pot}^{(0)}| \leq \Xi_2$  we have

$$
\sum_ {i \in \mathcal {S} _ {j, p o t} ^ {(0)}} | \langle w _ {i} ^ {(t + 1)}, \mathbf {M} _ {j} \rangle | \leq \sum_ {i \in \mathcal {S} _ {j, p o t} ^ {(0)}} | \langle w _ {i} ^ {(t)}, \mathbf {M} _ {j} \rangle | - \frac {\eta \lambda}{\Xi_ {2}} \left(\sum_ {i \in \mathcal {S} _ {j, p o t} ^ {(0)}} | \langle w _ {i} ^ {(t)}, \mathbf {M} _ {j} \rangle |\right) ^ {2} + \eta O \left(\frac {1 + L _ {t , j}}{d}\right) \cdot \Xi_ {2}
$$

Combining this with  $L_{t,j} \leq \sum_{i \in S_{j,pot}^{(0)}} |\langle w_i^{(t)}, \mathbf{M}_j \rangle| + O\left(\frac{1}{\Xi_2^3}\right)$  from Lemma C.20 and our choice  $\lambda \geq \frac{1}{d}$ , we have (for every  $j \in [d]$  and  $t \leq T$ ),

$$
\sum_ {i \in \mathcal {S} _ {j, p o t} ^ {(0)}} | \langle w _ {i} ^ {(t)}, \mathbf {M} _ {j} \rangle | \leq O (\Xi_ {2} ^ {2})
$$

This also implies  $L_{t,j} \leq O(\Xi_2^2)$  as well as

$$
\| w _ {i} ^ {(t)} \| ^ {2} = \sum_ {j: i \in \mathcal {S} _ {j, p o t +} ^ {(t)}} | \langle w _ {i} ^ {(t)}, \mathbf {M} _ {j} \rangle | ^ {2} + \sum_ {j: i \notin \mathcal {S} _ {j, p o t +} ^ {(t)}} | \langle w _ {i} ^ {(t)}, \mathbf {M} _ {j} \rangle | ^ {2} \leq O (\Xi_ {2} ^ {4}) + O (\frac {k ^ {2}}{d \beta^ {2}} (b ^ {(t)}) ^ {2}) \leq O (\Xi_ {2} ^ {4}).
$$

Finally, for the objective value, we wish use  $L_{t,j} \leq O(\Xi_2^2)$  and apply a high-probability Bernstein variant of the McDiarmid's inequality (see Lemma H.3).

Specifically, consider random  $z,\xi ,\rho$  . For notation simplicity, let us write  $\xi = \sum_{j\in [d]}\mathbf{M}_j\xi_j$  for i.i.d. random  $\xi_{j}\sim \mathcal{N}(0,\frac{\sigma_{x}^{2}}{d})$

Now, for every  $j \in [m]$ , suppose we change  $z_{j}$  to  $z_{j}^{\prime}$  and  $\xi_{j}$  to  $\xi_{j}^{\prime}$  with the same distribution. Then, with probability at least  $1 - e^{-\Omega (\log^{2}d)}$ ,

$$
\left| f _ {t} (z, \xi , \rho) - f _ {t} \left(z _ {- j}, z _ {j} ^ {\prime}, \xi_ {- j}, \xi_ {j} ^ {\prime}, \rho\right) \right| \leq L _ {t, j} \cdot \left(\left| z _ {j} \right| + \left| z _ {j} ^ {\prime} \right| + \left| \xi_ {j} \right| + \left| \xi_ {j} ^ {\prime} \right|\right)
$$

This implies with probability at least  $1 - e^{-\Omega (\log^2 d)}$

$$
\underset {z _ {j}, z _ {j} ^ {\prime}} {\mathbb {E}} \left| f _ {t} (z, \xi , \rho) - f _ {t} (z _ {- j}, z _ {j} ^ {\prime}, \xi , \rho) \right| ^ {2} \leq O (L _ {t, j} ^ {2}) \cdot \frac {1}{d}
$$

Therefore, we can apply Lemma H.3 to derive that with probability at least  $1 - e^{-\Omega (\log^2 d)}$ ,

$$
| f _ {t} (x, \rho) - \mathbb {E} _ {z, \xi} [ f _ {t} (x, \rho) ] | \leq O (\Xi_ {2} ^ {2} \log d)
$$

Finally, noticing that for every  $\rho$ , by symmetry  $\mathbb{E}_{z,\xi}[f_t(x,\rho)] = 0$ . This finishes the bound on the objective value.

We also prove this Lemma, which gives a lower bound on the loss:

Lemma C.22 (loss lower bound). In every iteration  $t$ , define  $L_{\max} := \max_{j \in [d]} \{L_{t,j}\}$  and suppose  $L_{\max} \leq O(\Xi_2^2)$ . Then we have:

$$
\underset {x, \rho} {\mathbb {E}} [ \ell_ {t} ^ {\prime} (w ^ {(t)}; x, y, \rho) ] = \Omega \left(\min \left\{1, \frac {1}{L _ {\max} ^ {2} \log^ {2} d} \right\}\right)
$$

Proof of Lemma C.22. Let  $\alpha \in \left[\frac{1}{(\Xi_2)^5}, 1\right]$  be a fixed value to be chosen later, and  $S_0 \subseteq [d]$  be an arbitrary subset of size  $|\mathcal{S}_0| = \alpha d$ . Consider a randomly sampled vector  $z$  and let  $x = \mathbf{M}z + \xi$  be the corresponding input. We construct another  $z'$  that is generated from the following process

1. Let  $\mathcal{S}_{re,z} \subseteq \mathcal{S}_0$  be the set consisting of all  $i \in \mathcal{S}_0$  with  $|z_i| = \Theta\left(\frac{1}{\sqrt{k}}\right)$ .  
2. For all  $i \notin S_{re,z}$ , pick  $z_i' = z_i$ .  
3. For all  $i \in S_{re,z}$ , pick  $z_i' = z_i$  or  $z_i' = -z_i$  each with probability 0.5, independently at random. Obviously,  $z'$  has the same distribution as  $z$ . Now, let us define  $x' = \mathbf{M}z' + \xi$ ,  $y' = \mathrm{sign}(\langle w^\star, z' \rangle)$ .

Since  $|\mathcal{S}_0| = \alpha d$ , recalling the distribution property that  $\mathbf{Pr}\left[|z_i| = \Theta\left(\frac{1}{\sqrt{k}}\right)\right] = \Omega\left(\frac{k}{d}\right)$ , we know with probability at least  $1 - e^{-\Omega (\log^2 d)}$  over the choice of  $z$ ,  $|\mathcal{S}_{re,z}| = \Theta (\alpha k)$ . We call this event  $\mathcal{E}_1(z)$ .

Let us denote by  $b_{i} = \frac{z_{i}^{\prime}}{z_{i}} \in \{-1, 1\}$  for every  $i \in S_{re,z}$ . We can therefore write  $f_{t}(w^{(t)}; x^{\prime}, \rho) = f(z, b, \xi, \rho)$  to emphasize that the randomness comes from  $z, b, \xi, \rho$ . Using the definition of coordinate Lipschitzness, we know with probability at least  $1 - e^{-\Omega (\log^2 d)}$  over  $z, \xi, \rho$ , it satisfies

$$
\forall k \in \mathcal {S} _ {r e, z}, \forall b \in \{- 1, 1 \} ^ {\mathcal {S} _ {r e, z}}, \forall b _ {k} ^ {\prime} \in \{- 1, + 1 \}: \quad | f (z, b, \xi , \rho) - f (z, (b _ {- k}, b _ {k}), \xi , \rho) | \leq O \left(\frac {L _ {\max}}{\sqrt {k}}\right)
$$

Let  $\mathcal{E}_2(z,\xi ,\rho)$  denote the event where the above statement holds.

Now, conditioning on  $\mathcal{E}_1(z)$  and  $\mathcal{E}_2(z,\xi,\rho)$  both hold, we can apply standard MiDiarmid's inequality (see Lemma H.2) over the randomness of  $b$ , and derive that with probability at least  $1 - e^{-\Omega (\log^2 d)}$  over  $b$ ,

$$
\left| f (z, b, \xi , \rho) - \underset {b} {\mathbb {E}} [ f (z, b, \xi , \rho) ] \right| \leq O \left(L _ {\max } \sqrt {\alpha} \log d\right)
$$

Let  $\mathcal{E}_3(b||z,\xi ,\rho)$  denote the (conditional) event where the above statement holds.

In sum, by combining  $\mathcal{E}_1,\mathcal{E}_2,\mathcal{E}_3$ , we know with probability at least  $1 - e^{-\Omega (\log^2 d)}$  over  $z,z^{\prime},\xi ,\rho$ , it satisfies

$$
\left| f _ {t} \left(w ^ {(t)}; x ^ {\prime}, \rho\right) - \underset {z ^ {\prime}} {\mathbb {E}} \left[ f _ {t} \left(w ^ {(t)}; x ^ {\prime}, \rho\right) \mid z, \xi , \rho \right] \right| \leq O \left(L _ {\max } \sqrt {\alpha} \log d\right).
$$

As a simple corollary, if we generate another copy  $z''$  in the same way as  $z'$ , and denote by  $x'' = \mathbf{M}z'' + \xi$ , then with probability at least  $1 - e^{-\Omega(\log^2 d)}$  over  $z, z', z'', \xi, \rho$ , it satisfies

$$
\left| f _ {t} \left(w ^ {(t)}; x ^ {\prime}, \rho\right) - f _ {t} \left(w ^ {(t)}; x ^ {\prime \prime}, \rho\right) \right| \leq O \left(L _ {\max } \sqrt {\alpha} \log d\right). \tag {C.21}
$$

Now, let us denote by  $y' = \mathrm{sign}(\langle w^{\star}, z' \rangle)$  and  $y'' = \mathrm{sign}(\langle w^{\star}, z'' \rangle)$  and compare them. Let us write

$$
A = \sum_ {i \in [ d ] \backslash \mathcal {S} _ {r e, z}} w _ {i} ^ {\star} z _ {i}, \quad B = \sum_ {i \in \mathcal {S} _ {r e, z}} w _ {i} ^ {\star} z _ {i} ^ {\prime}, \quad C = \sum_ {i \in \mathcal {S} _ {r e, z}} w _ {i} ^ {\star} z _ {i} ^ {\prime \prime}.
$$

Thus, we have  $y' = \operatorname{sign}(A + B)$  and  $y'' = \operatorname{sign}(A + C)$ .

First using a minor variant of Lemma H.1b, we have<sup>21</sup>

$$
\Pr \left[ A \in [ 0, \sqrt {\alpha} ] \right] \geq \Omega (\sqrt {\alpha})
$$

Denote this event by  $\mathcal{E}_4(z)$

Next, conditioning on any fixed  $z$  which satisfies  $\mathcal{E}_1(z)$  and  $\mathcal{E}_4(z)$ , we know that  $B$  and  $C$  become independent, each controlled by  $|\mathcal{S}_{re,z}| = \Theta (\alpha k)$  random Bernoulli variables. Therefore, we can apply a Wasserstein distance version of the central limit theorem (that can be derived from [111], full statement see [6, Appendix A.2]) to derive that, for a Gaussian variable  $g\sim (0,V^2)$  where  $V^{2} = \sum_{j\in \mathcal{S}_{re,z}}(z_{j})^{2} = \Theta (\alpha)$ , the Wasserstein distance:

$$
\mathcal {W} _ {2} (B, g) \leq O \left(\frac {\log k}{\sqrt {k}}\right) \quad \text {a n d} \quad \mathcal {W} _ {2} (C, g) \leq O \left(\frac {\log k}{\sqrt {k}}\right)
$$

This means with probability at least  $\Omega(1)$ , it satisfies  $B \in [0, \sqrt{\alpha}]$  and  $C \leq -5\sqrt{\alpha}$ .

To sum up, we know with probability at least  $\Omega (\sqrt{\alpha})$ , it satisfies  $A,B\in [0,\sqrt{\alpha} ]$  and  $C\leq -5\sqrt{\alpha}$ . This means  $y^\prime \neq y^{\prime \prime}$ , or in symbols,

$$
\Pr \left[ y ^ {\prime} \neq y ^ {\prime \prime} \right] \geq \Omega (\sqrt {\alpha}). \tag {C.22}
$$

Finally, conditioning on both (C.21) and (C.22) happen, we know that

- either  $\operatorname{sign}(f_t(w^{(t)}; x', \rho)) = \operatorname{sign}(f_t(w^{(t)}; x'', \rho)$ , in which case  $\ell_t'(w^{(t)}; x', y', \rho) + \ell'(w^{(t)}; x'', y'', \rho) \geq \frac{1}{2}$ ,  
- or  $|f_t(w^{(t)}; x', \rho)| \leq O(L_{\max} \sqrt{\alpha} \log d)$  and  $|f_t(w^{(t)}; x'', \rho)| \leq O(L_{\max} \sqrt{\alpha} \log d)$ , in which case if we choose  $\alpha = \min \left\{ \frac{1}{2}, \frac{1}{L_{\max}^2 \log^2 d} \right\}$ , then we have  $|f_t(w^{(t)}; x', \rho)|, |f_t(w^{(t)}; x'', \rho)| \leq O(1)$  and therefore  $\ell_t'(w^{(t)}; x', y', \rho) + \ell_t'(w^{(t)}; x'', y'', \rho) \geq \Omega(1)$ .

To sum up, we have

$$
\begin{array}{l} \underset {x, y = y (x), \rho} {\mathbb {E}} [ \ell_ {t} ^ {\prime} (w ^ {(t)}; x, y, \rho) ] = \frac {1}{2} \underset {z, z ^ {\prime}, z ^ {\prime \prime}, \xi , \rho} {\mathbb {E}} [ \ell_ {t} ^ {\prime} (w ^ {(t)}; x ^ {\prime}, y ^ {\prime}, \rho) + \ell_ {t} ^ {\prime} (w ^ {(t)}; x ^ {\prime \prime}, y ^ {\prime \prime}, \rho) ] \\ \geq \Omega (\sqrt {\alpha}) = \Omega \left(\min  \{1, \frac {1}{L _ {\max } ^ {2} \log^ {2} d} \}\right). \\ \end{array}
$$

# C.4.6 Proof of Theorem C.2

Proof of Theorem C.2. We first prove that for every  $t \geq T_{\mathsf{b}}$

$$
\mathcal {S} _ {j, p o t} ^ {(t)} \subseteq \mathcal {S} _ {j, p o t +} ^ {(t)} \subseteq \mathcal {S} _ {j, p o t} ^ {(0)} \tag {C.23}
$$

Note from the definitions the relationship  $S_{j, pot}^{(t)} \subseteq S_{j, pot+}^{(t)}$  always holds, so we only need to prove the second inclusion.

Suppose (C.23) holds until iteration  $t$ . Then, for every  $i \notin S_{j,pot}^{(0)}$ , let us apply Lemma C.16, Lemma C.17a together with (C.20) and  $L_{t,j} \leq O(\Xi_2^2)$  (using Lemma C.21) to (C.19). We get

$$
| \langle w _ {i} ^ {(t + 1)}, \mathbf {M} _ {j} \rangle | \leq | \langle w _ {i} ^ {(t)}, \mathbf {M} _ {j} \rangle | (1 - \eta \lambda) + O \left(\frac {\eta k \Xi_ {2} ^ {2}}{d ^ {2}}\right)
$$

Therefore, for those  $t$  that are sufficiently large so that  $b^{(t + 1)} = \beta \Xi_2^2$ , we have (using  $\lambda \geq \frac{1}{d}$ )

$$
\left| \langle w _ {i} ^ {(t + 1)}, \mathbf {M} _ {j} \rangle \right| \leq O \left(\frac {\eta k \Xi_ {2} ^ {2}}{d ^ {2}}\right) \cdot \frac {1}{\eta \lambda} = O \left(\frac {k \Xi_ {2} ^ {2}}{d \cdot d \lambda}\right) \leq \frac {k}{d \beta} b ^ {(t + 1)}
$$

and for those  $t$  that are still small so that  $b^{(t + 1)} = \Theta (\frac{\eta(t + 1)}{d})$ , we have

$$
| \langle w _ {i} ^ {(t + 1)}, \mathbf {M} _ {j} \rangle | \leq O \left(\frac {\eta k \Xi_ {2} ^ {2}}{d ^ {2}} \cdot (t + 1)\right) \ll \frac {k}{d \beta} b ^ {(t + 1)}
$$

Together, this means  $i \notin S_{j,pot+}^{(t+1)}$  so (C.23) holds for all  $t \geq T_{\mathsf{b}}$  and  $T \leq d^{O(\log d)} / \eta$ .

Phase II.1. We will construct a threshold  $T_{\mathsf{e}}$  and prove inductively for all  $t \in [T_{\mathsf{b}}, T_{\mathsf{e}}]$ . Initially at  $t = T_{\mathsf{b}}$ , by Lemma C.20 we have  $L_{t,j} = o(1)$ . As long as  $L_{t,j} = o(1)$  holds for all  $j \in [d]$ , we have

- for every  $i \in [m]$ , substituting Lemma C.15, Lemma C.17a and (C.20) into (C.19),

$$
| \langle w _ {i} ^ {(t + 1)}, \mathbf {M} _ {j} \rangle | \leq | \langle w _ {i} ^ {(t)}, \mathbf {M} _ {j} \rangle | + O \left(\frac {\eta}{d}\right) \leq \dots \leq O \left(\frac {\eta}{d} \cdot t\right)
$$

- for every  $i \notin S_{j, pot}^{(t)}$ , substituting Lemma C.16, Lemma C.17a and (C.20) into (C.19),

$$
\left| \langle w _ {i} ^ {(t + 1)}, \mathbf {M} _ {j} \rangle \right| \leq \left| \langle w _ {i} ^ {(t)}, \mathbf {M} _ {j} \rangle \right| + O \left(\frac {\eta k \log d}{d ^ {2}}\right) \leq \dots \leq O \left(\frac {\eta k \log d}{d ^ {2}} \cdot t\right)
$$

Since for each  $i$ , the number of  $j$  satisfying  $i \in S_{j, pot}^{(t)}$  is at most  $O(1)$  (using  $S_{j, pot}^{(t)} \subseteq S_{j, pot}^{(0)}$  and  $S_{ept}^{(0)} = [m]$ ), we have

$$
\left\| w _ {i} ^ {(t + 1)} \right\| \leq O \left(\frac {\eta}{d} \cdot t\right) \tag {C.24}
$$

These bounds together mean several things:

-  $L_{t,j} = o(1)$  for all  $j \in [d]$  and  $t \in [T_{\mathsf{b}}, T_{\mathsf{e}}]$  with  $T_{\mathbf{e}} = \Theta \left(\frac{d}{\eta \Xi_2 \log d}\right)$ .

Indeed, (C.24) gives  $|\langle w_i^{(t)}, \mathbf{M}_j \rangle| \leq O\left(\frac{1}{\Xi_2 \log d}\right)$ , but the number of  $j$  satisfying  $i \in S_{j,pot}^{(t)}$  is at most  $O(1)$ . So we can apply Lemma C.20 to get  $L_{t,j} = o(1)$ .

-  $S_{\text{opt} + + }^{(t)} = [m]$  for all  $t \in [T_{\mathbf{b}}, T_{\mathbf{e}}]$ .

Indeed,

- for those  $t$  that are small so that  $\sigma_w^{(t)} = \Theta \left(\frac{\eta}{d\sqrt{\log d}} t\right)$ , we have (C.24) implies  $\| w_i^{(t)}\| \leq O(\sqrt{\log d}\cdot \sigma_w^{(t)}) \ll \frac{\sigma_w^{(t)}}{\beta}$ ; and  
- for those  $t$  that are large so that  $\sigma_w^{(t)} = \Theta \left( \frac{\beta \Xi_2^2}{\sqrt{\log d}} \right)$ , we have (C.24) implies  $\| w_i^{(t)} \| \leq O \left( \frac{1}{\Xi_2 \log d} \right) \ll \frac{\sigma_w^{(t)}}{\beta}$ .

Together we have  $i \in S_{\text{opt} + + }^{(t)}$ .

-  $\mathcal{S}_{\text{opt+}}^{(t)} = [m]$  for all  $t \in [T_{\mathsf{b}}, T_{\mathsf{e}}]$ .

This is a direct corollary of  $S_{\text{opt} + + }^{(t)} = [m]$  together with the property that the number of  $j$  satisfying  $i \in S_{j, \text{pot} +}^{(t)}$  is at most  $O(1)$ .

Next, let us consider any  $j \in [d]$  with  $i \in S_{j,sure}^{(0)}$ . At any iteration  $t \in [T_{\mathbf{b}}, T_{\mathbf{e}}]$ , substituting Lemma C.14, Lemma C.17a, (C.24), and (C.20) into (C.19),

$$
\begin{array}{l} | \langle w _ {i} ^ {(t + 1)}, \mathbf {M} _ {j} \rangle | \geq | \langle w _ {i} ^ {(t)}, \mathbf {M} _ {j} \rangle | (1 - \eta \lambda - \eta \lambda \| w _ {i} ^ {(t)} \| _ {2}) + \Omega \left(\frac {\eta}{d}\right) \\ \geq | \langle w _ {i} ^ {(t)}, {\bf M} _ {j} \rangle | (1 - 2 \eta \lambda) + \Omega \left(\frac {\eta}{d}\right) \\ \end{array}
$$

This means two things:

- The value  $|\langle w_i^{(t)}, \mathbf{M}_j \rangle|$  keeps increasing as  $t$  increases, until it reaches  $\Theta \left( \frac{\eta}{d} \cdot \frac{1}{\eta \lambda} \right) = \Theta \left( \frac{1}{d \lambda} \right)$  and at that point it may decrease but will not fall below  $\Theta \left( \frac{1}{d \lambda} \right)$ . This ensures  $i \in S_{j, sure}^{(t)}$ .  
- At  $t = T_{\mathbf{e}}$ , we must have  $i \in S_{j, sure+}^{(t)}$  because

$$
\begin{array}{l} | \langle w _ {i} ^ {(t)}, {\bf M} _ {j} \rangle | \geq \Omega (\frac {\eta}{d} T _ {\bf e}) \geq \Omega \left(\frac {1}{\Xi_ {2} \log d}\right) \geq \Omega \left(\frac {1}{\Xi_ {2} \log d}\right) \cdot \frac {(b ^ {(t)}) ^ {2}}{\beta^ {2} \Xi_ {2} ^ {4}} \\ \geq \Omega \left(\frac {1}{k \beta^ {2} \Xi_ {2} ^ {5} \log d}\right) \cdot 4 k (b ^ {(t)}) ^ {2} \geq 4 k \big (b ^ {(t)} \big) ^ {2} \\ \end{array}
$$

To sum up, at iteration  $t = T_{\mathbf{e}}$ , we have

for  $i\in \mathcal{S}_{j,sure}^{(0)}$ $|\langle w_i^{(t)},\mathbf{M}_j\rangle |\geq \Omega \left(\frac{1}{\Xi_2\log d}\right)$  
for  $i\in \mathcal{S}_{j,pot}^{(0)}$ $|\langle w_i^{(t)},\mathbf{M}_j\rangle |\leq O(\frac{1}{\Xi_2\log d})$  
for  $i \notin \mathcal{S}_{j,pot}^{(0)}$ ,  $|\langle w_i^{(t)}, \mathbf{M}_j \rangle| \leq O\left(\frac{k}{d\Xi_2}\right)$ .

Phase II.2. We first make a quick observation that

-  $S_{\text{opt+}}^{(t)} = S_{\text{opt++}}^{(t)} = [m]$  for all  $t \geq T_{\mathsf{e}}$ .

Indeed, from iteration  $t = T_{\mathbf{e}}$  on, we have  $b^{(t)} = \beta \Xi_2^2$ . Using Lemma C.21 we have for every  $i \in [m]$ ,  $\| w_i^{(t)} \| \leq O(\Xi_2^2) \leq \frac{\sigma_w^{(t)}}{\beta}$ . Thus,  $S_{ept++}^{(t)} = [m]$  holds for all  $t \geq T_{\mathbf{e}}$ . As for  $S_{ept+}^{(t)} = [m]$ , it is a simple corollary of  $S_{ept++}^{(t)} = [m]$  together with the property that the number of  $j$  satisfying  $i \in S_{j,pot+}^{(t)}$  is at most  $O(1)$ .

Next, we claim for every  $i \in S_{j,sure+}^{(T_{\mathbf{e}})}$  and every  $t \geq T_{\mathbf{e}}$ , it must hold that

$$
\left| \langle w _ {i} ^ {(t)}, \mathbf {M} _ {j} \rangle \right| \geq \frac {1}{C ^ {\prime}} \left(\max  _ {i ^ {\prime} \in [ m ]} \left| \langle w _ {i ^ {\prime}} ^ {(t)}, \mathbf {M} _ {j} \rangle \right|\right) \quad \text {a n d} \quad i \in \mathcal {S} _ {j, s u r e +} ^ {(t)} \tag {C.25}
$$

for some sufficiently large constant  $C' > 1$ . We prove by induction. Suppose (C.25) holds for  $t$  and we consider  $t + 1$ . By the definition of  $i \in S_{j, sure+}^{(t)}$ , we know  $|\langle w_i^{(t)}, \mathbf{M}_j \rangle| \geq 2\sqrt{k} b^{(t)}$ . Now, consider every other  $i' \in [m] \setminus \{i\}$

- if  $|\langle w_{i'}^{(t)}, \mathbf{M}_j \rangle| < 2C' |\langle w_i^{(t)}, \mathbf{M}_j \rangle|$ , then after one iteration we still have  $|\langle w_{i'}^{(t+1)}, \mathbf{M}_j \rangle| < C' |\langle w_i^{(t+1)}, \mathbf{M}_j \rangle|$ .

- if  $|\langle w_{i'}^{(t)}, \mathbf{M}_j \rangle| > 2C' |\langle w_i^{(t)}, \mathbf{M}_j \rangle|$ , then we have

$$
\begin{array}{l} \| w _ {i} ^ {(t)} \| ^ {2} = | \langle w _ {i} ^ {(t)}, {\bf M} _ {j} \rangle | ^ {2} + \sum_ {j ^ {\prime} \neq j} | \langle w _ {i} ^ {(t)}, {\bf M} _ {j ^ {\prime}} \rangle | ^ {2} \leq | \langle w _ {i} ^ {(t)}, {\bf M} _ {j} \rangle | ^ {2} + (d - 1) \cdot \frac {k ^ {2}}{d ^ {2} \beta^ {2}} (b ^ {(t)}) ^ {2} \\ \leq 1 0 \left| \langle w _ {i} ^ {(t)}, \mathbf {M} _ {j} \rangle \right| ^ {2} \leq \frac {1 0}{2 C ^ {\prime}} \left| \langle w _ {i ^ {\prime}} ^ {(t)}, \mathbf {M} _ {j} \rangle \right| ^ {2} \leq \left\| w _ {i ^ {\prime}} ^ {(t)} \right\| ^ {2} \tag {C.26} \\ \end{array}
$$

Therefore, applying Lemma C.18 and Lemma C.17a (for  $i$  and  $i'$ ), and using  $\beta \leq \frac{1}{\sqrt{k}}$ , we have

$$
| \langle w _ {i} ^ {(t + 1)}, {\bf M} _ {j} \rangle | = | \langle w _ {i} ^ {(t)}, {\bf M} _ {j} \rangle | (1 - \eta \lambda - \eta \lambda \| w _ {i} ^ {(t)} \| _ {2}) + \eta \underset {x, y, \rho} {\mathbb {E}} \left[ y \ell_ {t} ^ {\prime} (w ^ {(t)}; x, y, \rho) z _ {j} \right] \pm O \left(\frac {\eta k ^ {1 . 5}}{d ^ {2}}\right)
$$

$$
| \langle w _ {i ^ {\prime}} ^ {(t + 1)}, \mathbf {M} _ {j} \rangle | = | \langle w _ {i ^ {\prime}} ^ {(t)}, \mathbf {M} _ {j} \rangle | (1 - \eta \lambda - \eta \lambda \| w _ {i ^ {\prime}} ^ {(t)} \| _ {2}) + \eta \underset {x, y, \rho} {\mathbb {E}} \left[ y \ell_ {t} ^ {\prime} (w ^ {(t)}; x, y, \rho) z _ {j} \right] \pm O \left(\frac {\eta k ^ {1 . 5}}{d ^ {2}}\right)
$$

Taking the difference and using (C.26), we have

$$
\begin{array}{l} | \langle w _ {i ^ {\prime}} ^ {(t + 1)}, \mathbf {M} _ {j} \rangle | - | \langle w _ {i} ^ {(t + 1)}, \mathbf {M} _ {j} \rangle | \leq \left(| \langle w _ {i ^ {\prime}} ^ {(t)}, \mathbf {M} _ {j} \rangle | - | \langle w _ {i} ^ {(t)}, \mathbf {M} _ {j} \rangle |\right) (1 - \eta \lambda - \eta \lambda \| w _ {i} ^ {(t)} \| _ {2}) + O \left(\frac {\eta k ^ {1 . 5}}{d ^ {2}}\right) \\ \leq \left(\left| \langle w _ {i ^ {\prime}} ^ {(t)}, \mathbf {M} _ {j} \rangle \right| - \left| \langle w _ {i} ^ {(t)}, \mathbf {M} _ {j} \rangle \right|\right) - \Omega (\eta \lambda (\sqrt {k} b ^ {(t)}) ^ {3}) + O \left(\frac {\eta k ^ {1 . 5}}{d ^ {2}}\right) \\ \leq \left(\left| \langle w _ {i ^ {\prime}} ^ {(t)}, \mathbf {M} _ {j} \rangle \right| - \left| \langle w _ {i} ^ {(t)}, \mathbf {M} _ {j} \rangle \right|\right) \\ \end{array}
$$

thus we continue to have  $|\langle w_{i'}^{(t + 1)},\mathbf{M}_j\rangle |\leq C'|\langle w_i^{(t + 1)},\mathbf{M}_j\rangle |.$

Putting these together we show that the first half of (C.25) holds at  $t + 1$ .

As for why  $i \in S_{j, sure+}^{(t+1)}$ , we consider two cases.

- If  $|\langle w_i^{(t)}, \mathbf{M}_j \rangle| \geq 4\sqrt{k} b^{(t)}$ , then in one iteration we should still have  $|\langle w_i^{(t+1)}, \mathbf{M}_j \rangle| \geq 2\sqrt{k} b^{(t)} = 2\sqrt{k} b^{(t+1)}$ .  
- If  $|\langle w_i^{(t)}, \mathbf{M}_j \rangle| \leq 4\sqrt{k} b^{(t)}$ , then by the first half of (C.25) together with Lemma C.20, we know the Lipschitzness  $L_{t,j} \leq O(\sqrt{k} b^{(t)} \cdot \Xi_2) + O\left(\frac{1}{\Xi_2^3}\right) \leq o(1)$ . In this case, we also have (see (C.26))  $\| w_i^{(t)}\|^2 \leq O(k(b^{(t)})^2) = o(1)$ . Applying Lemma C.14 and Lemma C.17a again we have

$$
\begin{array}{l} | \langle w _ {i} ^ {(t + 1)}, \mathbf {M} _ {j} \rangle | \geq | \langle w _ {i} ^ {(t)}, \mathbf {M} _ {j} \rangle | (1 - \eta \lambda - \eta \lambda \| w _ {i} ^ {(t)} \| _ {2}) + \Omega \left(\frac {\eta}{d}\right) \\ \geq | \langle w _ {i} ^ {(t)}, \mathbf {M} _ {j} \rangle | (1 - 2 \eta \lambda) + \Omega \left(\frac {\eta}{d}\right) \geq | \langle w _ {i} ^ {(t)}, \mathbf {M} _ {j} \rangle | \\ \end{array}
$$

Putting both cases together we have  $i \in S_{j, sure+}^{(t+1)}$  so the second half of (C.25) holds at  $t + 1$ .

# D Clean Accuracy Convergence Analysis

In this section we show the upper bound on how the clean training of a two-layer neural network can learn the labeling function from  $N$  training samples  $\{x_{i},y_{i}\}_{i = 1}^{N}$  up to small generalization error.

Theorem D.1. Suppose the high-probability initialization event in Lemma B.2 holds, and suppose  $\eta, \sigma_0 \in (0, \frac{1}{\mathsf{poly}(d)})$  and  $N \geq \mathsf{poly}(d)$ . With probability at least  $1 - e^{-\Omega (\log^2 d)}$ , for any  $T \geq T_{\mathsf{c}} \stackrel{\mathrm{def}}{=} \Omega \left( \frac{d \Xi_2^6}{\eta} \right)$  and  $T \leq d^{O(\log d)} / \eta$ , if we run the Algorithm 1 for  $T_{\mathsf{f}} = T_{\mathsf{e}} + T$  iterations, we have

$$
\frac {1}{T} \sum_ {t = T _ {\mathrm {e}}} ^ {T _ {\mathrm {e}} + T - 1} \mathbb {E} _ {x, y, \rho} \mathbf {O b j} _ {t} (w ^ {(t)}; x, y, \rho) \leq o (1)
$$

In other words, at least  $99\%$  of the iterations  $t = T_{\mathrm{e}}, T_{\mathrm{e}} + 1, \ldots, T_{\mathrm{e}} + T - 1$  will have population risk  $o(1)$  and clean population accuracy  $\geq 1 - o(1)$ .

Remark D.2. With additional efforts, one can also prove that Theorem D.1 holds with high probability  $1 - e^{-\Omega (\log^2 d)}$  for all  $T$  in the range  $T \in [T_{\mathbf{c}}, T_{\mathbf{f}}]$ . We do not prove it here since it completes the notations and is not beyond the scope of this paper.

# D.1 Proof of Theorem D.1: Convergence Theorem

Our convergence analysis will rely on the following (what we call) coupling function which is the first-order approximation of the neural network.

Definition D.3 (coupling). At every iteration  $t$ , we define a linear function in  $\mu$

$$
g _ {t} (\mu ; x, \rho) \stackrel {\text {d e f}} {=} \sum_ {i = 1} ^ {m} \left(\mathbb {1} _ {\langle w _ {i} ^ {(t)}, x \rangle + \rho_ {i} \geq b ^ {(t)}} \cdot \left(\langle \mu_ {i}, x \rangle + \rho_ {i} - b ^ {(t)}\right) - \mathbb {1} _ {- \langle w _ {i} ^ {(t)}, x \rangle + \rho_ {i} \geq b ^ {(t)}} \cdot \left(- \langle \mu_ {i}, x \rangle + \rho_ {i} - b ^ {(t)}\right)\right)
$$

and it equals the output of the real network at point  $\mu = w^{(t)}$  both on zero and first order:

$$
g _ {t} (w ^ {(t)}; x, \rho) = f _ {t} (w ^ {(t)}; x, \rho) \quad a n d \quad \left. \nabla_ {\mu} g _ {t} (\mu ; x, \rho) \right| _ {\mu = w ^ {(t)}} = \left. \nabla_ {w} f _ {t} (w; x, \rho) \right| _ {w = w ^ {(t)}}
$$

In the analysis, we shall also identify a special choice  $\mu^{\star}$  defined as follows.

Definition D.4. Recall  $\mathcal{S}_{1,sure}^{(0)},\ldots ,\mathcal{S}_{d,sure}^{(0)}\subseteq [m]$  are disjoint, so we construct  $\mu_1^\star ,\dots,\mu_m^\star$  by

$$
\mu_ {i} ^ {\star} \stackrel {{\mathrm {d e f}}} {{=}} \left\{ \begin{array}{l l} \alpha \left(\frac {w _ {j} ^ {\star}}{| \mathcal {S} _ {j , s u r e} ^ {(0)} |}\right) \mathbf {M} _ {j}, & i \in \mathcal {S} _ {j, s u r e} ^ {(0)} \text {f o r s o m e} j \in [ d ]; \\ \vec {0}, & \text {o t h e r w i s e}. \end{array} \right.
$$

Above,  $\alpha = o(1)$  is a parameter to be chosen later. One can easily check (using Lemma B.2) that

Claim D.5.  $\sum_{i\in [m]}\| \mu_i^\star \| ^2\leq O(\frac{\alpha^2}{\Xi_1} d)$  and  $\sum_{i\in [m]}\| \mu_i^\star \| ^3\leq O(\frac{\alpha^3}{\Xi_1^2} d)$

More interestingly, our so-constructed  $\mu^{\star}$  satisfies (to be proved in Section D.2)

Lemma D.6. Suppose  $\mathcal{S}_{\text{opt+}}^{(t)} = \mathcal{S}_{\text{opt++}}^{(t)} = [m]$ ,  $\mathcal{S}_{j, \text{pot}}^{(0)} \supseteq \mathcal{S}_{j, \text{pot+}}^{(t)}$  and  $\mathcal{S}_{j, \text{sure}}^{(0)} \subseteq \mathcal{S}_{j, \text{sure+}}^{(t)}$  for every  $j \in [d]$ . Then,

(a) with probability at least  $1 - e^{-\Omega (\log^2 d)}$  over  $x,\rho ,g_t(\mu^\star ;x,\rho) = \alpha \langle w^\star ,z\rangle \pm O(\frac{1}{\Xi_2^2})$  
(b)  $\mathbb{E}_{x,y = y(x),\rho}\left[\log \left(1 + e^{-y\cdot g_t(\mu^\star ;x,\rho)}\right)\right]\leq O\left(\frac{1}{\alpha^2} +\frac{1}{\Xi_2^2}\right)$

We are now ready to prove Theorem D.1. Since  $w_{i}^{(t + 1)} = w_{i}^{(t)} - \eta \nabla_{w_{i}}\widetilde{\mathbf{Obj}}_{t}(w^{(t)})$ , we have the identity

$$
\eta \langle \nabla \widetilde {\mathbf {O b j}} _ {t} (w ^ {(t)}), w ^ {(t)} - \mu^ {\star} \rangle = \frac {\eta^ {2}}{2} \| \nabla \widetilde {\mathbf {O b j}} _ {t} (w ^ {(t)}) \| _ {F} ^ {2} + \frac {1}{2} \| w ^ {(t)} - \mu^ {\star} \| _ {F} ^ {2} - \frac {1}{2} \| w ^ {(t + 1)} - \mu^ {\star} \| _ {F} ^ {2}
$$

Applying Lemma A.2, we know that by letting  $\mathbf{Obj}_t(w^{(t)}) = \mathbb{E}_{x,y,\rho}\mathbf{Obj}_t(w^{(t)};x,y,\rho)$ , it satisfies

$$
\eta \langle \nabla \mathbf {O b j} _ {t} (w ^ {(t)}), w ^ {(t)} - \mu^ {\star} \rangle \leq \eta^ {2} \cdot \mathsf {p o l y} (d) + \frac {1}{2} \| w ^ {(t)} - \mu^ {\star} \| _ {F} ^ {2} - \frac {1}{2} \| w ^ {(t + 1)} - \mu^ {\star} \| _ {F} ^ {2} + \frac {\eta}{\mathsf {p o l y} (d)}
$$

Let us define a pseudo objective

$$
\mathbf {O b j} _ {t} ^ {\prime} (\mu) \stackrel {\text {d e f}} {=} \underset {x, y = y (x), \rho} {\mathbb {E}} \left[ \log \left(1 + e ^ {- y \cdot g _ {t} (\mu ; x, \rho)}\right) \right] + \lambda \sum_ {i \in [ m ]} \mathbf {R e g} (\mu_ {i}) ,
$$

which is a convex function in  $\mu$  because  $g_{t}(\mu ;x,\rho)$  is linear in  $\mu$ . We have, for every  $t\geq T_{\mathsf{e}}$

$$
\begin{array}{l} \langle \nabla \mathbf {O b j} _ {t} (w ^ {(t)}), w ^ {(t)} - \mu^ {\star} \rangle \stackrel {①} {=} \langle \nabla \mathbf {O b j} _ {t} ^ {\prime} (w ^ {(t)}), w ^ {(t)} - \mu^ {\star} \rangle \\ \geq \mathbf {O b j} _ {t} ^ {\prime} (w ^ {(t)}) - \mathbf {O b j} _ {t} ^ {\prime} (\mu^ {\star}) = \mathbf {O b j} _ {t} (w ^ {(t)}) - \mathbf {O b j} _ {t} ^ {\prime} (\mu^ {\star}) \\ \stackrel {2} {\geq} \mathbf {O b j} _ {t} (w ^ {(t)}) - \lambda \sum_ {i \in [ m ]} \left(\frac {\| \mu_ {i} ^ {\star} \| ^ {3}}{3} + \frac {\| \mu_ {i} ^ {\star} \| ^ {2}}{2}\right) - O \left(\frac {1}{\alpha^ {2}} + \frac {1}{\Xi_ {2} ^ {2}}\right) \\ \stackrel {3} {\geq} \mathbf {O b j} _ {t} (w ^ {(t)}) - O \left(\frac {\alpha^ {3} \log d}{\Xi_ {1} ^ {2}} + \frac {\alpha^ {2} \log d}{\Xi_ {1}} + \frac {1}{\alpha^ {2}} + \frac {1}{\Xi_ {2} ^ {2}}\right) \\ \geq \mathbf {O b j} _ {t} (w ^ {(t)}) - O \left(\frac {\sqrt {\log d}}{\sqrt {\Xi_ {1}}}\right) \\ \end{array}
$$

Above, ① uses the definition of  $g_{t}$ , ② uses Lemma D.6b (and Theorem C.2 for the prerequisite for Lemma D.6b), and ③ uses Claim D.5 for the bound on  $\| \mu_i^\star \|^2$  and  $\| \mu_i^\star \|^3$ .

Putting these together, we have

$$
\eta \left(\mathbf {O b j} _ {t} (w ^ {(t)}) - O \left(\frac {\sqrt {\log d}}{\sqrt {\Xi_ {1}}}\right)\right) \leq \eta^ {2} \cdot \mathsf {p o l y} (d) + \frac {1}{2} \| w ^ {(t)} - \mu^ {\star} \| _ {F} ^ {2} - \frac {1}{2} \| w ^ {(t + 1)} - \mu^ {\star} \| _ {F} ^ {2} + \frac {\eta}{\mathsf {p o l y} (d)}
$$

Therefore, after telescoping for  $t = T_{\mathsf{e}},T_{\mathsf{e}} + 1,\ldots ,T_{\mathsf{e}} + T - 1$  , and using  $\eta \leq \frac{1}{\mathrm{poly}(d)}$  , we have

$$
\frac {1}{T} \sum_ {t = T _ {\mathrm {e}}} ^ {T _ {\mathrm {e}} + T - 1} \left(\mathbf {O b j} _ {t} (w ^ {(t)}) - O \left(\frac {\sqrt {\log d}}{\sqrt {\Xi_ {1}}}\right)\right) \leq \frac {O (\| w ^ {(T _ {\mathrm {e}})} - \mu^ {\star} \| _ {F} ^ {2})}{\eta T} \leq \frac {O (\Xi_ {2} ^ {4} m)}{\eta T}
$$

Finally, we calculate

$$
\begin{array}{l} \| w ^ {(T _ {\mathrm {e}})} \| _ {F} ^ {2} \leq \sum_ {i \in \bigcup_ {j} \mathcal {S} _ {j, p o t} ^ {(0)}} \| w _ {i} ^ {(T _ {\mathrm {e}})} \| _ {2} ^ {2} + \sum_ {i \notin \bigcup_ {j} \mathcal {S} _ {j, p o t} ^ {(0)}} \| w _ {i} ^ {(T _ {\mathrm {e}})} \| _ {2} ^ {2} \\ \leq d \Xi_ {2} \cdot O (\Xi_ {2} ^ {4}) + m \cdot O (\frac {k ^ {2}}{d ^ {2}} \Xi_ {2} ^ {4}) \leq O (d \Xi_ {2} ^ {5}) \\ \end{array}
$$

and this finishes the proof.

# D.2 Proof of Claim D.6: Main Coupling

The proof of Lemma D.6a comes from Claim D.7 and Claim D.8 below. In the two claims, we split  $g_{t}(\mu^{\star};x) = g_{t,1} + g_{t,4}$  into two terms, and bound them separately. Define

$$
g _ {t, 1} (\boldsymbol {\mu} ^ {\star}; x, \rho) = \sum_ {j \in [ d ]} \frac {\alpha \cdot w _ {j} ^ {\star}}{| \mathcal {S} _ {j , s u r e} ^ {(0)} |} \sum_ {i \in \mathcal {S} _ {j, s u r e} ^ {(0)}} \left(\mathbb {1} _ {\langle w _ {i} ^ {(t)}, x \rangle + \rho_ {i} \geq b ^ {(t)}} + \mathbb {1} _ {- \langle w _ {i} ^ {(t)}, x \rangle + \rho_ {i} \geq b ^ {(t)}}\right) \cdot z _ {j}
$$

$$
g _ {t, 4} (x, \rho) = \sum_ {i \in [ m ]} \Big ((\rho_ {i} - b ^ {(t)}) \mathbb {1} _ {\langle w _ {i} ^ {(t)}, x \rangle + \rho_ {i} \geq b ^ {(t)}} + (b ^ {(t)} - \rho_ {i}) \mathbb {1} _ {- \langle w _ {i} ^ {(t)}, x \rangle + \rho_ {i} \geq b ^ {(t)}} \Big)
$$

Claim D.7.  $\mathbf{Pr}_{x,\rho}[g_{t,1}(\mu^{\star};x,\rho) = \alpha \langle w^{\star},z\rangle ]\geq 1 - e^{-\Omega (\log^{2}d)}$

Proof of Claim D.7. Recall for each  $i \in S_{j, sure}^{(0)}$ ,

- it satisfies  $i \in S_{j, sure+}^{(t)}$  so  $|\langle w_i^{(t)}, \mathbf{M}_j \rangle| \geq 2\sqrt{k} b^{(t)}$ .  
- it also implies  $i \notin S_{j',pot+}^{(t)}$  for any  $j' \neq j$ , so  $|\langle w_i^{(t)}, \mathbf{M}_{j'} \rangle| \leq \frac{k}{d\beta} b^{(t)}$ ;  
recall  $\rho_{i}\sim \mathcal{N}(0,(\sigma_{\rho}^{(t)})^{2})$  for  $\sigma_{\rho}^{(t)} = \Theta (b^{(t)}\cdot \frac{(\log\log\log d)^3}{\log d})$  
- recall  $\langle w_i^{(t)},\xi \rangle$  is a variable with variance at most  $O\left(\frac{\|w_i^{(t)}\|^2\sigma_x^2}{d}\right)$  for  $\sigma_{x} = O(1)$ .

Applying Lemma C.19, we know with probability at least  $1 - e^{-\Omega (\log^2 d)}$  it satisfies

$$
\left| \langle w _ {i} ^ {(t)}, \sum_ {j ^ {\prime} \neq j} \mathbf {M} _ {j ^ {\prime}} z _ {j ^ {\prime}} + \xi \rangle \right| + | \rho_ {i} | \leq \frac {b ^ {(t)}}{2} = \frac {\beta \Xi_ {2} ^ {2}}{2} \tag {D.1}
$$

and when this happens it satisfies, whenever  $z_{j}\neq 0$

$$
\mathbb {1} _ {\langle w _ {i} ^ {(t)}, x \rangle + \rho_ {i} \geq b ^ {(t)}} + \mathbb {1} _ {- \langle w _ {i} ^ {(t)}, x \rangle + \rho_ {i} \geq b ^ {(t)}} = 1
$$

Summing up over all  $i\in \mathcal{S}_{j,sure}^{(0)}$  and  $j\in [d]$ , we have with probability at least  $1 - e^{-\Omega (\log^2 d)}$  over  $x,\rho$ :  $g_{t,1}(\mu^{\star};x) = \alpha \langle w^{\star},z\rangle$ .

Claim D.8.  $\mathbf{Pr}_{x,\rho}[|g_{t,4}(x,\rho)|\leq O(\frac{1}{\Xi_2^2})]\geq 1 - e^{-\Omega (\log^2 d)}$

Proof of Claim D.8. Let us write  $\xi = \sum_{j\in [d]}\mathbf{M}_j\xi_j$  where each  $\xi_{j}$  is i.i.d. Let us write

$$
\begin{array}{l} g _ {t, 4} (x, \rho) = \sum_ {i \in [ m ]} g _ {t, 4, i} (x, \rho_ {i}) \\ \text {f o r} \quad g _ {t, 4, i} (x, \rho_ {i}) \stackrel {{\text {d e f}}} {{=}} \left((\rho_ {i} - b ^ {(t)}) \mathbb {1} _ {\langle w _ {i} ^ {(t)}, x \rangle + \rho_ {i} \geq b ^ {(t)}} + (b ^ {(t)} - \rho_ {i}) \mathbb {1} _ {- \langle w _ {i} ^ {(t)}, x \rangle + \rho_ {i} \geq b ^ {(t)}}\right) \\ \end{array}
$$

We note that  $g_{t,4}(x,\rho)$  is a random variable that depends on independent variables

$$
\xi_ {1}, \dots , \xi_ {d}, z _ {1}, \dots , z _ {d}, \rho_ {1}, \dots , \rho_ {m}
$$

so we also want to write it as  $g_{t,4}(z,\xi ,\rho)$  and  $g_{t,4,i}(z,\xi ,\rho_i)$ .

We can without loss of generality assume as if  $|\rho_i| \leq \frac{b^{(t)}}{10}$  and  $|\xi_j| \leq \frac{b^{(t)}}{\Xi_2^{10}} \coloneqq B$  always hold, both of which happen with probability at least  $1 - e^{-\Omega (\log^2 d)}$ . In the rest of the proof we condition on this happens. By symmetry we have

$$
\forall \rho \colon \underset {\xi , z} {\mathbb {E}} [ g _ {t, 4} (z, \xi , \rho) ] = 0.
$$

We wish to apply a high-probability version of the McDiarmid's inequality (see Lemma H.3) to bound  $g_{t,4}$ . In order to do so, we need to check the sensitivity of  $g_{t,4}(x,\rho)$  regarding every random variable.

- For every  $z_j$ , suppose we perturb it to an arbitrary  $z_j' \in [-1, 1]$ . We also write  $z' = (z_{-j}, z_j')$  and  $x' = \mathbf{M}z' + \xi$ .

- Now, for every  $i \in S_{j,pot+}^{(t)}$ , we have the naive bound

$$
\left| g _ {t, 4, i} \left(z, \xi , \rho_ {i}\right) - g _ {t, 4, i} \left(z ^ {\prime}, \xi , \rho_ {i}\right) \right| \leq 2 b ^ {(t)} \cdot \mathbb {1} _ {z _ {j} \neq z _ {j} ^ {\prime}}
$$

and there are at most  $|S_{j, pot + }^{(t)}| \leq \Xi_2$  such neurons  $i$ .

- For every  $i \notin S_{j,pot+}^{(t)}$ , we have  $|\langle w_i^{(t)}, \mathbf{M}_j \rangle| \leq \frac{k}{d\beta} b^{(t)}$ . Define event

$$
\mathcal {E} _ {i} = \left\{\left| \langle w _ {i} ^ {(t)}, \sum_ {j ^ {\prime} \neq j} \mathbf {M} _ {j ^ {\prime}} z _ {j ^ {\prime}} + \xi \rangle \right| \geq \frac {b ^ {(t)}}{2} \right\}
$$

* When event  $\mathcal{E}_i$  does not happen, we have  $\mathbb{1}_{\langle w_i^{(t)},x\rangle +\rho_i\geq b^{(t)}} = \mathbb{1}_{\langle w_i^{(t)},x'\rangle +\rho_i\geq b^{(t)}}$ , and thus

$$
g _ {t, 4, i} (z, \xi , \rho_ {i}) = g _ {t, 4, i} (z ^ {\prime}, \xi , \rho_ {i}).
$$

* When  $\mathcal{E}_i$  happens, using the randomness of  $\rho_i$ , we have

$$
\begin{array}{l} \mathop{\mathrm{Pr}}\limits_{\rho_{i}}\left[\mathbb{1}_{\langle w_{i}^{(t)},x\rangle +\rho_{i}\geq b^{(t)}}\neq \mathbb{1}_{\langle w_{i}^{(t)},x^{\prime}\rangle +\rho_{i}\geq b^{(t)}}\right]\leq O\left(\frac{|\langle w_{i}^{(t)},\mathbf{M}_{j}\rangle| \cdot |z_{j} - z_{j}^{\prime}|}{\sigma_{\rho}^{(t)}}\right) \\ \leq O \left(\frac {k \log d}{d \beta}\right) \cdot | z _ {j} - z _ {j} ^ {\prime} | \\ \end{array}
$$

and thus

$$
| g _ {t, 4, i} (z, \xi , \rho_ {i}) - g _ {t, 4, i} (z ^ {\prime}, \xi , \rho_ {i}) | = \left\{ \begin{array}{l l} 0, & \mathrm {w . p .} \geq 1 - O \left(\frac {k \log d}{d \beta} | z _ {j} - z _ {j} ^ {\prime} |\right) \mathrm {o v e r} \rho_ {i}; \\ O (b ^ {(t)}), & \mathrm {o t h e r w i s e}. \end{array} \right.
$$

Note with probability at least  $1 - e^{-\Omega (\log^2 d)}$ , the number of  $i \in [m]$  with  $\mathcal{E}_i$  holds is at most  $O(k\Xi_2)$  (using Lemma C.19). Therefore, by applying Chernoff bound, we know

$$
| g _ {t, 4} (z, \xi , \rho) - g _ {t, 4} (z ^ {\prime}, \xi , \rho) | \leq O (b ^ {(t)}) \cdot \left(\frac {k \log d}{d \beta} | z _ {j} - z _ {j} ^ {\prime} | \cdot k \Xi_ {2} + \Xi_ {2}\right)
$$

This means two things that both hold with probability at least  $1 - e^{-\Omega (\log^2 d)}$  over  $z_{-j},\xi ,\rho$ :

$$
\begin{array}{l} - \text {F o r a l l} z _ {j}, z _ {j} ^ {\prime}, | g _ {t, 4} (x, \rho) - g _ {t, 4} (x ^ {\prime}, \rho) | \leq O (b ^ {(t)}) \cdot \left(\frac {k \log d}{d \beta} \cdot k \Xi_ {2} + \Xi_ {2}\right) \leq O (\sqrt {k} b ^ {(t)}) <   o (\frac {1}{\Xi_ {2} ^ {2}}) \\ - \mathbb {E} _ {z _ {j}, z _ {j} ^ {\prime}} | g _ {t, 4} (x, \rho) - g _ {t, 4} (x ^ {\prime}, \rho) | ^ {2} \leq O \big ((b ^ {(t)}) ^ {2} \big) \cdot \mathbb {E} _ {z _ {j}, z _ {j} ^ {\prime}} \left(\left(\frac {k \log d}{d \beta} \cdot k \Xi_ {2}\right) ^ {2} | z _ {j} - z _ {j} ^ {\prime} | ^ {2} + \Xi_ {2} ^ {2} \mathbb {1} _ {z _ {j} \neq z _ {j} ^ {\prime}}\right) \leq \\ O \left(\left(\frac {k ^ {4} \Xi_ {2} ^ {2} \log^ {2} d}{d ^ {2} \beta^ {2}} \frac {1}{d} + \Xi_ {2} ^ {2} \frac {k}{d}\right) (b ^ {(t)}) ^ {2}\right) <   o (\frac {1}{d \Xi_ {2} ^ {4}}) \\ \end{array}
$$

- For every  $\xi_j$ , suppose we perturb it to  $\xi_j' \in [-B, B]$ . We write  $\xi' = \xi + \mathbf{M}_j(\xi_j' - \xi_j)$  and  $x' = \mathbf{M}z + \xi'$ .

- Now, for every  $i \in S_{j,pot+}^{(t)}$ , with probability at least  $1 - e^{-\Omega(\log^2 d)}$  we have  $|\langle w_i^{(t)}, \sum_{j' \neq j} \mathbf{M}_{j'} \xi_{j'} \rangle| \leq \frac{b^{(t)}}{10}$ . Therefore, if it also happens that  $|\langle w_i^{(t)}, \mathbf{M}z \rangle| \leq \frac{b^{(t)}}{10}$ , then  $g_{t,4,i}(z, \xi, \rho_i) = g_{t,4,i}(z, \xi', \rho_i)$ .

In other words, we have

$$
| g _ {t, 4, i} (z, \xi , \rho_ {i}) - g _ {t, 4, i} (z ^ {\prime}, \xi , \rho_ {i}) | \leq 2 b ^ {(t)} \cdot \mathbb {1} _ {| \langle w _ {i} ^ {(t)}, \mathbf {M} z \rangle | \geq \frac {b (t)}{1 0}}.
$$

Summing up over  $i \in S_{j,pot+}^{(t)}$ , and taking expectation in  $z$ , we have

$$
\left| \mathbb {E} _ {z} \sum_ {i \in \mathcal {S} _ {j, p o t +} ^ {(t)}} g _ {t, 4, i} (z, \xi , \rho_ {i}) - g _ {t, 4, i} (z ^ {\prime}, \xi , \rho_ {i}) \right| \leq 2 b ^ {(t)} \cdot \mathbb {E} _ {z} \left[ \sum_ {i \in \mathcal {S} _ {j, p o t +} ^ {(t)}} \mathbb {1} _ {| \langle w _ {i} ^ {(t)}, \mathbf {M} z \rangle | \geq \frac {b ^ {(t)}}{1 0}} \right] \leq O \left(b ^ {(t)} \frac {k \Xi_ {2}}{d}\right)
$$

where the last inequality uses a variant of Lemma C.7 and  $|\mathcal{S}_{j,pot+}^{(t)}| \leq \Xi_2$ .

- For every  $i \notin S_{j, pot+}^{(t)}$ , we have  $|\langle w_i^{(t)}, \mathbf{M}_j \rangle| \leq \frac{k}{d\beta} b^{(t)}$ . Define event

$$
\mathcal {E} _ {i} = \left\{\left| \langle w _ {i} ^ {(t)}, \mathbf {M} z + \sum_ {j ^ {\prime} \neq j} \mathbf {M} _ {j ^ {\prime}} \xi_ {j ^ {\prime}} \rangle \right| \geq \frac {b ^ {(t)}}{2} \right\}
$$

* When event  $\mathcal{E}_i$  does not happen, we have  $\mathbb{1}_{\langle w_i^{(t)},x\rangle +\rho_i\geq b^{(t)}} = \mathbb{1}_{\langle w_i^{(t)},x'\rangle +\rho_i\geq b^{(t)}}$ , and thus

$$
g _ {t, 4, i} (z, \xi , \rho_ {i}) = g _ {t, 4, i} \left(z ^ {\prime}, \xi , \rho_ {i}\right).
$$

* When  $\mathcal{E}_i$  happens, using the randomness of  $\rho_i$ , we have

$$
\begin{array}{l} \mathbf {P r} _ {\rho_ {i}} \left[ \mathbb {1} _ {\langle w _ {i} ^ {(t)}, x \rangle + \rho_ {i} \geq b ^ {(t)}} \neq \mathbb {1} _ {\langle w _ {i} ^ {(t)}, x ^ {\prime} \rangle + \rho_ {i} \geq b ^ {(t)}} \right] \leq O \left(\frac {| \langle w _ {i} ^ {(t)} , \mathbf {M} _ {j} \rangle | \cdot | \xi_ {j} - \xi_ {j} ^ {\prime} |}{\sigma_ {\rho} ^ {(t)}}\right) \\ \leq O \left(\frac {k \log d}{d \beta}\right) \cdot | \xi_ {j} - \xi_ {j} ^ {\prime} | \\ \end{array}
$$

and thus

$$
| g _ {t, 4, i} (z, \xi , \rho_ {i}) - g _ {t, 4, i} (z, \xi^ {\prime}, \rho_ {i}) | = \left\{ \begin{array}{l l} 0, & \text {w . p .} \geq 1 - O \left(\frac {k \log d}{d \beta} | \xi_ {j} - \xi_ {j} ^ {\prime} |\right) \text {o v e r} \rho_ {i}; \\ O (b ^ {(t)}), & \text {o t h e r w i s e}. \end{array} \right.
$$

Note with probability at least  $1 - e^{-\Omega (\log^2 d)}$ , the number of  $i \in [m]$  with  $\mathcal{E}_i$  holds is at most  $O(k\Xi_2)$  (using a minor variant of Lemma C.19). Therefore, by applying Chernoff bound, we know with probability at least  $1 - e^{-\Omega (\log^2 d)}$  over  $z,\xi_{-j},\rho$

$$
\mid \sum_ {i \notin \mathcal {S} _ {j, p o t +} ^ {(t)}} g _ {t, 4, i} (z, \xi , \rho_ {i}) - g _ {t, 4, i} (z, \xi^ {\prime}, \rho_ {i}) \mid \leq O (b ^ {(t)}) \cdot \left(\frac {k \log d}{d \beta} | \xi_ {j} - \xi_ {j} ^ {\prime} | \cdot k \Xi_ {2}\right)
$$

Taking expectation over  $z$ , we have with probability at least  $1 - e^{-\Omega (\log^2 d)}$  over  $\xi_{-j}, \rho$ :

$$
\left|\mathbb{E}_{z}\sum_{i\notin \mathcal{S}^{(t)}_{j,pot + }}g_{t,4,i}(z,\xi ,\rho_{i}) - g_{t,4,i}(z,\xi^{\prime},\rho_{i})\right|\leq O(b^{(t)})\cdot \left(\frac{\sqrt{k}\log d}{\sqrt{d}} |\xi_{j} - \xi_{j}^{\prime}| \cdot k\Xi_{2}\right)
$$

Putting the two cases together, we have with probability at least  $1 - e^{-\Omega (\log^2 d)}$  over  $\xi_{-j},\rho$

$$
\left| \mathbb {E} _ {z} g _ {t, 4} (z, \xi , \rho) - \mathbb {E} _ {z} g _ {t, 4} (z, \xi^ {\prime}, \rho) \right| \leq O (b ^ {(t)}) \cdot O \left(\frac {k \log d}{d \beta} | \xi_ {j} - \xi_ {j} ^ {\prime} | \cdot k \Xi_ {2} + \frac {k \Xi_ {2}}{d}\right)
$$

This means two things that both hold with probability at least  $1 - e^{-\Omega (\log^2 d)}$  over  $\xi_{-j},\rho$  ..

$$
\begin{array}{l} - \text {F o r a l l} \xi_ {j}, \xi_ {j} ^ {\prime}, | \mathbb {E} _ {z} g _ {t, 4} (z, \xi , \rho) - \mathbb {E} _ {z} g _ {t, 4} (z, \xi^ {\prime}, \rho) | \leq O (b ^ {(t)}) \cdot \left(\frac {k \log d}{d \beta} B \cdot k \Xi_ {2} + \frac {k \Xi_ {2}}{d}\right) \ll o (\frac {1}{\Xi_ {2} ^ {2}}) \\ - \mathbb {E} _ {\xi_ {j}, \xi_ {j} ^ {\prime}} | \mathbb {E} _ {z} g _ {t, 4} (z, \xi , \rho) - \mathbb {E} _ {z} g _ {t, 4} (z, \xi^ {\prime}, \rho) | ^ {2} \leq O \big ((b ^ {(t)}) ^ {2} \big) \cdot \mathbb {E} _ {z _ {j}, z _ {j} ^ {\prime}} \left(\left(\frac {k \log d}{d \beta} \cdot k \Xi_ {2}\right) ^ {2} | \xi_ {j} - \xi_ {j} ^ {\prime} | ^ {2} + \frac {k ^ {2}}{d ^ {2}} \Xi_ {2} ^ {2}\right) \leq \\ O \left(\left(\frac {k ^ {4} \Xi_ {2} ^ {2} \log^ {2} d}{d ^ {2} \beta^ {2}} \frac {1}{d} + \frac {k ^ {2}}{d ^ {2}} \Xi_ {2} ^ {2}\right) (b ^ {(t)}) ^ {2}\right) \ll o (\frac {1}{d \Xi_ {2} ^ {4}}) \\ \end{array}
$$

We are now ready to apply the high-probability version of the McDiarmid's inequality (see Lemma H.3). We apply it twice. In the first time, we use the perturbation on  $z$  to derive that, with probability at least  $1 - e^{-\Omega (\log^2 d)}$  over  $z,\xi ,\rho$  ..

$$
| g _ {t, 4} (z, \xi , \rho) - \underset {z} {\mathbb {E}} g _ {t, 4} (z, \xi , \rho) | \leq O (\frac {1}{\Xi_ {2} ^ {2}})
$$

In the second time, we use the perturbation on  $\xi$  to derive that, with probability at least  $1 - e^{-\Omega (\log^2 d)}$  over  $\xi ,\rho$

$$
| \underset {z} {\mathbb {E}} g _ {t, 4} (z, \xi , \rho) - \underset {z, \xi} {\mathbb {E}} g _ {t, 4} (z, \xi , \rho) | \leq O (\frac {1}{\Xi_ {2} ^ {2}})
$$

Finally, noticing that  $\mathbb{E}_{z,\xi}g_{t,4}(z,\xi ,\rho) = 0$  for every  $\rho$ , we finish the proof.

![](images/b70e16d5efac15f314da8c28e81c9a43f30c2e4b8b89ee738efb7c4f9ed76153.jpg)

This finishes the proof of Lemma D.6a. We are only left to prove Lemma D.6b.

By Lipschitz continuity of the  $\log (1 + e^{-x})$  function, we know with probability at least  $1 - e^{-\Omega (\log^2 d)}$ ,

$$
\log \left(1 + e ^ {- y (x) \cdot g _ {t} (\mu^ {\star}; x)}\right) = \log \left(1 + e ^ {- y (x) \cdot \alpha \langle w ^ {\star}, z \rangle}\right) \pm O (\frac {1}{\Xi_ {2} ^ {2}}) = \log \left(1 + e ^ {- \alpha | \langle w ^ {\star}, z \rangle |}\right) \pm O (\frac {1}{\Xi_ {2} ^ {2}})
$$

Taking expectation (and using the exponential tail) we have

$$
\mathbb {E} \left[ \log \left(1 + e ^ {- y (x) \cdot g _ {t} (\mu^ {\star}; x)}\right) \right] = \mathbb {E} \left[ \log \left(1 + e ^ {- \alpha | \langle w ^ {\star}, z \rangle |\}\right) \right] \pm O (\frac {1}{\Xi_ {2} ^ {2}})
$$

Note if we take expectation over  $z$ , we have

$$
\begin{array}{l} \mathbb {E} _ {z} [ \log (1 + e ^ {- \alpha | \langle w ^ {\star}, z \rangle |}) ] \leq \int_ {t \geq 0} \log (1 + e ^ {- \alpha t}) \cdot \mathbf {P r} [ | \langle w ^ {\star}, z \rangle | \leq t ] d t \\ \stackrel {1} {\leq} O (1) \cdot \int_ {t \geq 0} e ^ {- \alpha t} \cdot \left(t + \frac {1}{\sqrt {k}}\right) \leq O (\frac {1}{\alpha^ {2}} + \frac {1}{\sqrt {k}}) \\ \end{array}
$$

where ① uses Lemma H.1a. This finishes the proof of Lemma D.6b.

# E Why Clean Training is Non-Robust

In this section we shall show that clean training will not achieve robustness against  $\ell_2$  perturbation of size  $\tau = \Omega\left(\frac{d^{0.4999}}{k^2}\right)$  as long as  $k = \widetilde{\Omega}(d^{0.3334})$ . Recall  $\|\mathbf{M}\|_1 = \sum_{j \in [d]} \|\mathbf{M}_j\|_\infty$ .

Theorem E.1 (clean training is non-robust). Suppose the high-probability initialization event in Lemma B.2 holds. Suppose  $k > d^{(1 - c_0) / 3}$  and consider any iteration  $t \geq \Omega \left(\frac{1}{\eta \lambda \Xi_2^2}\right)$  and  $t \leq \min \{d^{\log d} / \eta, T_{\mathsf{f}}\}$ . With probability at least  $1 - e^{-\Omega (\log^2 d)}$  the following holds. If we perturb every input  $x$  by  $\delta = -y \Xi_2^{10}(\mathbf{M}w^\star) / k^2$ , then the accuracy drops below  $e^{-\Omega (\log^2 d)}$ :

$$
\mathop{\bf P r}\limits_{x,y = y(x),\rho}\left[\operatorname {sign}\bigl(f_{t}(x - \delta)\bigr) = y\right]\leq e^{-\Omega (\log^{2}d)}  ,
$$

$$
\mathop{\bf P r}\limits_{x,y = y(x)}\left[\mathsf{sign}\bigl(\mathbb{E}_{\rho}[f_{t}(x - \delta)]\bigr) = y\right]\leq e^{-\Omega (\log^{2}d)}  .
$$

Note that

$$
\| \delta \| _ {2} \leq \frac {\Xi_ {2} ^ {1 0} \sqrt {d}}{k ^ {2}} \quad a n d \quad \| \delta \| _ {\infty} \leq \frac {\Xi_ {2} ^ {1 0} \| \mathbf {M} \| _ {1}}{k ^ {2}}.
$$

The proof of Theorem E.1 relies on the following main lemma (to be proved in Section E.1). It says that towards the end of clean training, neurons  $w_{i}^{(t)}$  have a (small) common direction in  $\mathbf{M}w^{\star}$ .

Lemma E.2 (non-robust). For any iteration  $t \geq \Omega\left(\frac{1}{\eta\lambda\Xi_2^2}\right)$ , let subset  $\mathcal{S} \stackrel{\mathrm{def}}{=} \cup_{j \in [d]} \mathcal{S}_{j, sure}^{(0)}$ , then

$$
\sum_ {i \in \mathcal {S}} \langle w _ {i} ^ {(t)}, \mathbf {M} w ^ {\star} \rangle = \Omega \left(\frac {k d}{\Xi_ {2} ^ {7}}\right) \quad a n d \quad \forall i \in [ m ] \colon \langle w _ {i} ^ {(t)}, \mathbf {M} w ^ {\star} \rangle \geq - O \left(\frac {1}{\lambda} \frac {k ^ {3} \Xi_ {2} ^ {4}}{d ^ {2}} \sigma_ {x} ^ {2}\right)
$$

With the help of Lemma E.2, one can calculate that by perturbing input in this direction  $-y\cdot \mathbf{M}w^{\star}$ , the output label of the network can change dramatically. This is the proof of Theorem E.1 and details can be found in Section E.2.

# E.1 Proof of Lemma E.2: Common Direction Among Neurons

Before proving Lemma E.2, let us first present Claim E.3.

Claim E.3. We have

$$
\begin{array}{l} \langle w _ {i} ^ {(t + 1)}, \mathbf {M} w ^ {\star} \rangle \geq \langle w _ {i} ^ {(t)}, \mathbf {M} w ^ {\star} \rangle \cdot (1 - \eta \lambda - \eta \lambda \| w _ {i} ^ {(t)} \|) - O \left(\eta \frac {k ^ {2}}{d ^ {1 . 5}} \sigma_ {x} ^ {2} + \frac {\eta}{\operatorname {p o l y} (d)}\right) \\ + \eta \underset {x, \rho} {\mathbb {E}} \left[ \ell_ {t} ^ {\prime} (w ^ {(t)}; x, y, \rho) \mathbb {1} _ {\langle w _ {i} ^ {(t)}, x \rangle + \rho_ {i} \geq b ^ {(t)}} | \langle w ^ {\star}, z \rangle | \right] \\ \end{array}
$$

Proof of Claim E.3. Let us recall from (C.19) that

$$
\begin{array}{l} \langle w _ {i} ^ {(t + 1)}, \mathbf {M} _ {j} \rangle = \langle w _ {i} ^ {(t)}, \mathbf {M} _ {j} \rangle \cdot (1 - \eta \lambda - \eta \lambda \| w _ {i} ^ {(t)} \|) \pm \frac {\eta}{\operatorname {p o l y} (d)} \\ + \eta \underset {x, y = y (x), \rho} {\mathbb {E}} \left[ y \ell_ {t} ^ {\prime} (w ^ {(t)}; x, y, \rho) \big (\mathbb {1} _ {\langle w _ {i} ^ {(t)}, x \rangle + \rho_ {i} \geq b ^ {(t)}} + \mathbb {1} _ {- \langle w _ {i} ^ {(t)}, x \rangle + \rho_ {i} \geq b ^ {(t)}} \big) \big (z _ {j} + \langle \xi , \mathbf {M} _ {j} \rangle \big) \right] \\ \end{array}
$$

and therefore

$$
\begin{array}{l} \langle w _ {i} ^ {(t + 1)}, \sum_ {j \in [ d ]} w _ {j} ^ {\star} \mathbf {M} _ {j} \rangle \geq \langle w _ {i} ^ {(t)}, \sum_ {j \in [ d ]} w _ {j} ^ {\star} \mathbf {M} _ {j} \rangle \cdot (1 - \eta \lambda - \eta \lambda \| w _ {i} ^ {(t)} \|) - \frac {\eta}{\mathrm {p o l y} (d)} \\ + \eta \mathbb {E} _ {x, \rho} \left[ y \ell_ {t} ^ {\prime} (w ^ {(t)}; x, y, \rho) \left(\mathbb {1} _ {\langle w _ {i} ^ {(t)}, x \rangle + \rho_ {i} \geq b ^ {(t)}} + \mathbb {1} _ {- \langle w _ {i} ^ {(t)}, x \rangle + \rho_ {i} \geq b ^ {(t)}}\right) \langle w ^ {\star}, z \rangle \right] \\ - O (\eta) \cdot \sum_ {j \in [ d ]} \left| \mathbb {E} _ {x, \rho} \left[ y \ell_ {t} ^ {\prime} (w ^ {(t)}; x, y, \rho) \big (\mathbb {1} _ {\langle w _ {i} ^ {(t)}, x \rangle + \rho_ {i} \geq b ^ {(t)}} + \mathbb {1} _ {- \langle w _ {i} ^ {(t)}, x \rangle + \rho_ {i} \geq b ^ {(t)}} \big) \langle \xi , \mathbf {M} _ {j} \rangle \right] \right| \\ \end{array}
$$

Applying Lemma C.17b and using  $y\langle w^{*},z\rangle = |\langle w^{*},z\rangle |$  and  $\| w_i^{(t)}\| \leq O(\Xi_2^2)$  (see Lemma C.21), we have

$$
\begin{array}{l} \langle w _ {i} ^ {(t + 1)}, \sum_ {j \in [ d ]} w _ {j} ^ {\star} \mathbf {M} _ {j} \rangle \geq \langle w _ {i} ^ {(t)}, \sum_ {j \in [ d ]} w _ {j} ^ {\star} \mathbf {M} _ {j} \rangle \cdot (1 - \eta \lambda - \eta \lambda \| w _ {i} ^ {(t)} \|) - O \left(\eta \frac {k ^ {3} \Xi_ {2} ^ {4}}{d ^ {2}} \sigma_ {x} ^ {2} + \frac {\eta}{\operatorname {p o l y} (d)}\right) \\ + \eta \underset {x, \rho} {\mathbb {E}} \left[ \ell_ {t} ^ {\prime} (w ^ {(t)}; x, y, \rho) \left(\mathbb {1} _ {\langle w _ {i} ^ {(t)}, x \rangle + \rho_ {i} \geq b ^ {(t)}} + \mathbb {1} _ {- \langle w _ {i} ^ {(t)}, x \rangle + \rho_ {i} \geq b ^ {(t)}}\right) | \langle w ^ {\star}, z \rangle | \right] \\ \geq \langle w _ {i} ^ {(t)}, \sum_ {j \in [ d ]} w _ {j} ^ {\star} \mathbf {M} _ {j} \rangle \cdot (1 - \eta \lambda - \eta \lambda \| w _ {i} ^ {(t)} \|) - O \left(\eta \frac {k ^ {3} \Xi_ {2} ^ {4}}{d ^ {2}} \sigma_ {x} ^ {2} + \frac {\eta}{\mathsf {p o l y} (d)}\right) \\ + \eta \underset {x, \rho} {\mathbb {E}} \left[ \ell_ {t} ^ {\prime} (w ^ {(t)}; x, y, \rho) \mathbb {1} _ {\langle w _ {i} ^ {(t)}, x \rangle + \rho_ {i} \geq b ^ {(t)}} | \langle w ^ {\star}, z \rangle | \right]. \\ \end{array}
$$

Proof of Lemma E.2. Recall Claim E.3 says that

$$
\begin{array}{l} \sum_ {i \in \mathcal {S}} \langle w _ {i} ^ {(t + 1)}, \mathbf {M} w ^ {\star} \rangle \geq \sum_ {i \in \mathcal {S}} \langle w _ {i} ^ {(t)}, \mathbf {M} w ^ {\star} \rangle \cdot (1 - \eta \lambda - \eta \lambda \| w _ {i} ^ {(t)} \|) - O \left(\eta | \mathcal {S} | \frac {k ^ {3} \Xi_ {2} ^ {4}}{d ^ {2}} \sigma_ {x} ^ {2}\right) \\ + \eta \underset {x, \rho} {\mathbb {E}} \left[ \ell_ {t} ^ {\prime} (w ^ {(t)}; x, y, \rho) | \langle w ^ {\star}, z \rangle | \sum_ {i \in \mathcal {S}} \mathbb {1} _ {\langle w _ {i} ^ {(t)}, x \rangle + \rho_ {i} \geq b ^ {(t)}} \right] \\ \end{array}
$$

Using  $S_{j,sure}^{(0)} \subseteq S_{j,sure+}^{(t)}$ , and a similar analysis to Lemma C.19, we know with probability at least  $1 - e^{-\Omega(\log^2 d)}$  it satisfies  $\sum_{i \in S} \mathbb{1}_{\langle w_i^{(s)}, x \rangle + \rho_i \geq b^{(s)}} \geq \Omega(k)$ . Therefore, the above inequality gives

$$
\begin{array}{l} \sum_ {i \in \mathcal {S}} \langle w _ {i} ^ {(t + 1)}, \mathbf {M} w ^ {\star} \rangle \geq \sum_ {i \in \mathcal {S}} \langle w _ {i} ^ {(t)}, \mathbf {M} w ^ {\star} \rangle \cdot (1 - \eta \lambda - \eta \lambda \| w _ {i} ^ {(t)} \|) - O \left(\eta | \mathcal {S} | \frac {k ^ {3} \Xi_ {2} ^ {4}}{d ^ {2}} \sigma_ {x} ^ {2}\right) \\ + \Omega (\eta k) \cdot \mathbb {E} _ {x, \rho} \left[ \ell_ {t} ^ {\prime} (w ^ {(t)}; x, y, \rho) | \langle w ^ {\star}, z \rangle | \right] \\ \end{array}
$$

Now, using small ball probability Lemma H.1a we have

$$
\Pr \left[ | \langle w ^ {*}, z \rangle | \leq 0. 0 1 \mathbb {E} [ \ell_ {s} ^ {\prime} (w ^ {(t)}; x, y, \rho) ] \right] \leq \frac {1}{2} \mathbb {E} [ \ell_ {s} ^ {\prime} (w ^ {(t)}; x, y, \rho) ] + O (\frac {1}{\sqrt {k}}).
$$

Therefore, let us abbreviate by writing  $\ell_s^\prime = \ell_s^\prime (w^{(t)};x,y,\rho)$ , then

$$
\begin{array}{l} \mathbb {E} \left[ \ell_ {s} ^ {\prime} (w ^ {(t)}; x, y, \rho) \cdot | \langle w ^ {*}, z \rangle | \right] \\ \geq \mathbb {E} \left[ \ell_ {s} ^ {\prime} \cdot | \langle w ^ {*}, z \rangle | \right| | \langle w ^ {*}, z \rangle | \geq 0. 0 1 \mathbb {E} \left[ \ell_ {s} ^ {\prime} \right] \cdot \mathbf {P r} \left[ | \langle w ^ {*}, z \rangle | \geq 0. 0 1 \mathbb {E} \left[ \ell_ {s} ^ {\prime} \right] \right. \\ \geq 0. 0 1 \mathbb {E} \left[ \ell_ {s} ^ {\prime} \right] \cdot \mathbb {E} \left[ \ell_ {s} ^ {\prime} \mid | \langle w ^ {*}, z \rangle | \geq 0. 0 1 \mathbb {E} \left[ \ell_ {s} ^ {\prime} \right] \right] \cdot \mathbf {P r} \left[ | \langle w ^ {*}, z \rangle | \geq 0. 0 1 \mathbb {E} \left[ \ell_ {s} ^ {\prime} \right] \right] \\ = 0. 0 1 \mathbb {E} \left[ \ell_ {s} ^ {\prime} \right] \cdot \left(\mathbb {E} \left[ \ell_ {s} ^ {\prime} \right] - \mathbb {E} \left[ \ell_ {s} ^ {\prime} \mid | \langle w ^ {*}, z \rangle | <   0. 0 1 \mathbb {E} \left[ \ell_ {s} ^ {\prime} \right] \right] \cdot \mathbf {P r} \left[ | \langle w ^ {*}, z \rangle | <   0. 0 1 \mathbb {E} \left[ \ell_ {s} ^ {\prime} \right] \right]\right) \\ \geq 0. 0 1 \mathbb {E} \left[ \ell_ {s} ^ {\prime} \right] \cdot \left(\mathbb {E} \left[ \ell_ {s} ^ {\prime} \right] - \mathbf {P r} \left[ | \langle w ^ {*}, z \rangle | <   0. 0 1 \mathbb {E} \left[ \ell_ {s} ^ {\prime} \right] \right]\right) \\ \geq 0. 0 1 \mathbb {E} [ \ell_ {s} ^ {\prime} ] \cdot \left(\frac {1}{2} \mathbb {E} \left[ \ell_ {s} ^ {\prime} \right] - O (\frac {1}{\sqrt {k}})\right) \geq \frac {1}{\Xi_ {2} ^ {5}} \\ \end{array}
$$

where the last inequality ① uses Lemma C.21 and Lemma C.22.

Therefore, using  $|S|\leq d\Xi_{2}$  , we have

$$
\begin{array}{l} \sum_ {i \in \mathcal {S}} \langle w _ {i} ^ {(t + 1)}, \mathbf {M} w ^ {\star} \rangle \geq \sum_ {i \in \mathcal {S}} \langle w _ {i} ^ {(t)}, \mathbf {M} w ^ {\star} \rangle \cdot (1 - \eta \lambda - \eta \lambda \| w _ {i} ^ {(t)} \|) - O \left(\eta | \mathcal {S} | \frac {k ^ {3} \Xi_ {2} ^ {4}}{d ^ {2}} \sigma_ {x} ^ {2}\right) + \Omega (\frac {\eta k}{\Xi_ {2} ^ {5}}) \\ \geq \sum_ {i \in \mathcal {S}} \langle w _ {i} ^ {(t)}, \mathbf {M} w ^ {\star} \rangle \cdot (1 - O (\eta \lambda \Xi_ {2} ^ {2})) + \Omega (\frac {\eta k}{\Xi_ {2} ^ {5}}) \\ \end{array}
$$

so we conclude for every  $t \geq \Omega\left(\frac{1}{\eta\lambda\Xi_2^2}\right)$  it satisfies

$$
\sum_ {i \in \mathcal {S}} \langle w _ {i} ^ {(t)}, \mathbf {M} w ^ {\star} \rangle \geq \Omega \left(\frac {k d}{\Xi_ {2} ^ {7}}\right) .
$$

As for any arbitrary  $i \in [m]$ , we have

$$
\begin{array}{l} \langle w _ {i} ^ {(t + 1)}, \mathbf {M} w ^ {\star} \rangle \geq \langle w _ {i} ^ {(t)}, \mathbf {M} w ^ {\star} \rangle \cdot (1 - \eta \lambda - \eta \lambda \| w _ {i} ^ {(t)} \|) - O \left(\eta \frac {k ^ {3} \Xi_ {2} ^ {4}}{d ^ {2}} \sigma_ {x} ^ {2}\right) \\ \geq \dots \geq - O \left(\frac {1}{\lambda} \frac {k ^ {3} \Xi_ {2} ^ {4}}{d ^ {2}} \sigma_ {x} ^ {2}\right) . \\ \end{array}
$$

# E.2 Proof of Theorem E.1

Proof of Theorem E.1. Recall  $\mathcal{S} \stackrel{\mathrm{def}}{=} \cup_{j \in [d]} S_{j,sure}^{(0)}$  from Lemma E.2 and  $S_{j,sure}^{(0)} \subseteq S_{j,sure+}^{(t)}$  from Theorem C.2. For every  $i \in S_{j,sure}^{(0)} \subseteq S_{j,sure+}^{(t)}$ , we know with probability at least  $1 - e^{-\Omega(\log^2 d)}$ , the

same proof of (D.1) gives

$$
\mathbb {1} _ {\langle w _ {i} ^ {(t)}, x \rangle + \rho_ {i} \geq 1 0 b ^ {(t)}} = \mathbb {1} _ {w _ {j} ^ {\star} z _ {j} > 0} \quad \text {a n d} \quad \mathbb {1} _ {- \langle w _ {i} ^ {(t)}, x \rangle + \rho_ {i} \geq 1 0 b ^ {(t)}} = \mathbb {1} _ {w _ {j} ^ {\star} z _ {j} <   0}
$$

Therefore, setting  $\delta = \delta_0\mathbf{M}w^\star$  for some  $\delta_0\in (0,\frac{\beta}{\sqrt{d}})$ , and using  $\| w_i^{(t)}\| _2\leq \Xi_2^2$  (since  $\mathcal{S}_{ept + + }^{(t)} = [m]$ ), we have  $|\langle w_i^{(t)},\delta \rangle |\leq \delta_0\Xi_2^2\sqrt{d}\leq b^{(t)}$ . Using this, we can sum up over all  $i\in S$ :

$$
\begin{array}{l} \sum_ {i \in \mathcal {S}} \mathsf {R e L U} (\langle w _ {i} ^ {(t)}, x - \delta \rangle + \rho_ {i} - b ^ {(t)}) - \mathsf {R e L U} (- \langle w _ {i} ^ {(t)}, x - \delta \rangle + \rho_ {i} - b ^ {(t)}) \\ = \sum_ {i \in \mathcal {S}} \mathsf {R e L U} (\langle w _ {i} ^ {(t)}, x \rangle + \rho_ {i} - b ^ {(t)}) - \mathsf {R e L U} (- \langle w _ {i} ^ {(t)}, x \rangle + \rho_ {i} - b ^ {(t)}) - \underbrace {\sum_ {i \in \mathcal {S}} \left(\mathbb {1} _ {w _ {j _ {i}} ^ {\star} z _ {j _ {i}} > 0} + \mathbb {1} _ {w _ {j _ {i}} ^ {\star} z _ {j _ {i}} <   0}\right) \langle w _ {i} ^ {(t)} , \delta \rangle} _ {\clubsuit} \\ \end{array}
$$

where  $j_{i}\in [d]$  is the unique index such that  $i\in S_{j_i,sure}^{(0)}$  . We can rewrite the decrement

$$
\widehat {\clubsuit} = \delta_ {0} \sum_ {j \in [ d ]} \left(\mathbb {1} _ {w _ {j} ^ {\star} z _ {j} > 0} + \mathbb {1} _ {w _ {j} ^ {\star} z _ {j} <   0}\right) \sum_ {i \in \mathcal {S} _ {j, s u r e} ^ {(0)}} \langle w _ {i} ^ {(t)}, \mathbf {M} w ^ {\star} \rangle
$$

Using  $\left| \sum_{i \in S_{j, sure}^{(0)}} \langle w_i^{(t)}, \mathbf{M}w^\star \rangle \right| \leq \Xi_2^2\sqrt{d} |\mathcal{S}_{j, sure}^{(0)}| \leq \Xi_2^3\sqrt{d}$  and  $\left( \mathbb{1}_{w_j^\star z_j > 0} + \mathbb{1}_{w_j^\star z_j < 0} \right) = 1$  with probability  $\Theta(\frac{k}{d})$ , we can apply Bernstein's inequality and derive

$$
\mathbf {P r} \left[ | \clubsuit - \underset {z} {\mathbb {E}} [ \clubsuit ] | > \delta_ {0} \cdot \Xi_ {2} ^ {3} \sqrt {k d} \log d \right] \leq e ^ {- \Omega (\log^ {2} d)}
$$

Also using  $\left(1_{w_j^* z_j > 0} + 1_{w_j^* z_j < 0}\right) = 1$  with probability  $\Theta\left(\frac{k}{d}\right)$ , we can derive using Lemma E.2 that

$$
\begin{array}{l} \underset {z} {\mathbb {E}} [ \clubsuit ] \geq \delta_ {0} \cdot \left(\Omega (\frac {k}{d}) \sum_ {i \in \mathcal {S}} \langle w _ {i} ^ {(t)}, \mathbf {M} w ^ {\star} \rangle - O (k \Xi_ {2}) \cdot \frac {k ^ {2}}{\lambda d ^ {1 . 5}} \sigma_ {x} ^ {2}\right) \\ \geq \delta_ {0} \cdot \left(\Omega (\frac {k ^ {2}}{\Xi_ {2} ^ {7}}) - O (k \Xi_ {2}) \cdot \frac {1}{\lambda} \frac {k ^ {3} \Xi_ {2} ^ {4}}{d ^ {2}} \sigma_ {x} ^ {2}\right) \geq \delta_ {0} \cdot \Omega (\frac {k ^ {2}}{\Xi_ {2} ^ {7}}) \\ \end{array}
$$

Combining the above equations and using  $k > d^{(1 - c_0) / 3}$ , we have with probability at least  $1 - e^{-\Omega (\log^2 d)}$ ,

$$
\clubsuit \geq \delta_ {0} \cdot \Omega \left(\frac {k ^ {2}}{\Xi_ {2} ^ {7}}\right)
$$

For the remainder terms, we using  $|\langle w_i^{(t)},\delta \rangle |\leq \delta_0\Xi_2^2\sqrt{d}\leq b^{(t)} / 2$  , we have

$$
\begin{array}{l} \sum_ {i \in [ m ] \backslash \mathcal {S}} \operatorname {R e L U} \left(\langle w _ {i} ^ {(t)}, x - \delta \rangle + \rho_ {i} - b ^ {(t)}\right) - \operatorname {R e L U} \left(- \langle w _ {i} ^ {(t)}, x - \delta \rangle + \rho_ {i} - b ^ {(t)}\right) \\ \leq \sum_ {i \in [ m ] \setminus \mathcal {S}} \operatorname {R e L U} (\langle w _ {i} ^ {(t)}, x \rangle + \rho_ {i} - b ^ {(t)}) - \operatorname {R e L U} (- \langle w _ {i} ^ {(t)}, x \rangle + \rho_ {i} - b ^ {(t)}) - \underbrace {\sum_ {i \in [ m ] \setminus \mathcal {S}} \mathbb {1} _ {| \langle w _ {i} ^ {(t)} , x \rangle | + | \rho_ {i} | > \frac {b (t)}{2}} \min  \left\{0 , \langle w _ {i} ^ {(t)}, \delta \rangle \right\}} _ {\spadesuit} \\ \end{array}
$$

Using Lemma E.2 and Lemma C.19 we have with probability at least  $1 - e^{\Omega (\log^2 d)}$ ,

$$
\spadesuit \geq - \delta_ {0} \cdot O \left(\frac {1}{\lambda} \frac {k ^ {3} \Xi_ {2} ^ {4}}{d ^ {2}} \sigma_ {x} ^ {2}\right) \cdot \sum_ {i \in [ m ] \backslash \mathcal {S}} \mathbb {1} _ {| \langle w _ {i} ^ {(t)}, x \rangle | + | \rho_ {i} | > \frac {b (t)}{2}} \geq - \delta_ {0} \cdot O \left(\frac {1}{\lambda} \frac {k ^ {3} \Xi_ {2} ^ {4}}{d ^ {2}} \sigma_ {x} ^ {2}\right) \cdot O (k \Xi_ {2}) \geq - \frac {\clubsuit}{2}.
$$

Putting together the bounds for  $\clubsuit$  and  $\spadesuit$  we have

$$
\begin{array}{l} f _ {t} (x - \delta) = \sum_ {i \in [ m ]} \mathsf {R e L U} (\langle w _ {i} ^ {(t)}, x - \delta \rangle + \rho_ {i} - b ^ {(t)}) - \mathsf {R e L U} (- \langle w _ {i} ^ {(t)}, x - \delta \rangle + \rho_ {i} - b ^ {(t)}) \\ \leq \sum_ {i \in [ m ]} \mathsf {R e L U} (\langle w _ {i} ^ {(t)}, x \rangle + \rho_ {i} - b ^ {(t)}) - \mathsf {R e L U} (- \langle w _ {i} ^ {(t)}, x \rangle + \rho_ {i} - b ^ {(t)}) - \frac {\clubsuit}{2} \leq f _ {t} (x) - \delta_ {0} \cdot \Omega \left(\frac {k ^ {2}}{\Xi_ {2} ^ {7}}\right) \\ \end{array}
$$

In other words, choosing  $\delta_0 = \frac{\Xi_2^{10}}{k^2}$ , then combining with  $|f_t(x)| \leq O(\Xi_2^2 \log d)$  from Lemma C.21, we immediately have  $f_t(x - \delta) < 0$ .

Using an analogous proof, one can also show that  $f_{t}(x + \delta) > 0$ . Therefore, if we choose a perturb direction  $-\delta_0 y \mathbf{M} w^* = -y \delta$ , we have

$$
\mathop{\Pr}\limits_{x,y = y(x),\rho}\left[\operatorname {sign}\bigl(f_{t}(x - \delta_{0}y\mathbf{M}w^{*})\bigr) = y\right]\leq e^{-\Omega (\log^{2}d)}  .
$$

This means the robust accuracy is below  $e^{-\Omega (\log^2 d)}$ . Finally, using  $\| \mathbf{M}w^{*}\|_{2}\leq O(\sqrt{d})$  and  $\| \mathbf{M}w^{*}\|_{\infty}\leq O(\sum_{j\in [d]}\| \mathbf{M}_j\|_{\infty}) = O(\| \mathbf{M}\| _1)$  finishes the proof.

Note that a similar proof as above also shows

$$
\operatorname * {P r} _ {x, y = y (x)} \left[ \operatorname {s i g n} \left(\underset {\rho} {\mathbb {E}} [ f _ {t} (x - \delta_ {0} y \mathbf {M} w ^ {*}) ]\right) = y \right] \leq e ^ {- \Omega (\log^ {2} d)}.
$$

# F Robust Training Through Local Feature Purification

Suppose we run clean training for  $T_{\mathsf{f}} \geq \Omega \left( \frac{d \Xi_2^6}{\eta} \right)$  iterations following Theorem D.1. From this iteration on, let us perform  $T$  more steps of robust training.

During the robust training phase, let us consider an arbitrary (norm-bounded) adversarial perturbation algorithm  $A$ . Recall from Definition 4.2 that, given the current network  $f$  (which includes hidden weights  $\{w_i\}$ , output weights  $\{a_i\}$ , bias  $\{b_i\}$  and smoothing parameter  $\sigma_{\rho}$ ), an input  $x$ , a label  $y$ , and some internal random string  $r$ , the perturbation algorithm  $A$  outputs a vector satisfying

$$
\left\| A (f, x, y, r) \right\| _ {p} \leq \tau .
$$

for some  $\ell_p$  norm. Our two main theorems below apply to all such perturbation algorithms  $A$  (including the Fast Gradient Method, FGM).

Theorem F.1 ( $\ell_2$ -adversarial training). In the same setting as Theorem D.1, suppose we first run  $T_{\mathsf{f}}$  iterations of clean training with  $\Omega \left(\frac{d\Xi_2^6}{\eta}\right) \leq T_{\mathsf{f}} \leq d^{\log d} / \eta$  and obtain

$$
\mathbf {O b j} _ {\text {c l e a n}} = \underset {x, y = y (x), \rho} {\mathbb {E}} \left[ \mathbf {O b j} _ {T _ {\mathrm {f}}} \left(w ^ {\left(T _ {\mathrm {f}}\right)}; x, y, \rho\right) \right] \leq o (1).
$$

Next, suppose  $\sigma_x \leq \min\{O(1), \frac{d^{2(1 - 2c_0)}}{k^{5.5}}\}$  and  $k^{2.5} < d^{1 - 2c_0} / \log d$ . Starting from iteration  $T_{\mathsf{f}}$ , suppose we perform robust training for additional  $T = T_{\mathbf{g}} = \Theta \left(\frac{k^2 \Xi_2^4 m \log d}{\eta d}\right) \leq O\left(\frac{k^2}{d^{1 - 2c_0}}\right)$  iterations, against some  $\ell_2$  perturbation algorithm  $A$  with radius  $\tau \stackrel{\mathrm{def}}{=} \frac{1}{\sqrt{k} \cdot d^{c_0}}$ . With probability  $\geq 1 - e^{-\Omega (\log^2 d)}$ ,

$$
\frac {1}{T} \sum_ {t = T _ {\mathrm {f}}} ^ {T _ {\mathrm {f}} + T - 1} \underset {x, y = y (x), \delta = A \left(f _ {t}, x, y, r\right), \rho} {\mathbb {E}} \left[ \mathbf {O b j} _ {t} \left(w ^ {(t)}; x + \delta , y, \rho\right) \right] \leq \mathbf {O b j} _ {\text {c l e a n}} + o (1)
$$

Corollary F.2. In Theorem F.1, if  $A$  is Fast Gradient Method (FGM) with  $\ell_2$  radius  $\tau$ , and

$$
\underset {x, y = y (x), \delta = A (f _ {t}, x, y), \rho} {\mathbb {E}} \left[ \mathbf {O b j} _ {t} (w ^ {(t)}; x + \delta , y, \rho) \right] \leq o (1)
$$

for some  $t \in [T_{\mathfrak{f}}, T_{\mathfrak{f}} + T_{\mathfrak{g}}]$ . Then,

$$
\mathop{\bf P r}\limits_{x,y = y(x)}\left[\exists \delta \in \mathbb{R}^{d},\| \delta \|_{2}\leq \tau \colon \operatorname {sign}(\underset {\rho}{\mathbb{E}}f_{t}(w^{(t)};x + \delta ,\rho))\neq y\right]\leq o(1)  .
$$

Corollary F.3. Consider for instance  $\sigma_{x} = 0$ ,  $c_{0} = 0.00001$ , and sufficiently large  $d > 1$ .

- For  $k \in [d^{0.0001}, d^{0.3999}]$ , robust training gives 99.9% accuracy against  $\ell_2$  perturbation  $\geq \frac{1}{k^{0.5} \cdot d^{0.0001}}$ .  
- For  $k \geq d^{0.3334}$ , clean training gives  $0.01\%$  accuracy against  $\ell_2$  perturbation radius  $\leq \frac{d^{0.5001}}{k^2}$ .  
- For  $k \in [d^{0.3334}, d^{0.3999}]$ , robust training provably beats clean training in  $\ell_2$  robust accuracy.

Theorem F.4 ( $\ell_{\infty}$ -adversarial training). In the same setting as Theorem D.1, suppose we first run  $T_{\mathsf{f}}$  iterations of clean training with  $\Omega \left(\frac{d\Xi_2^6}{\eta}\right) \leq T_{\mathsf{f}} \leq d^{\log d} / \eta$  and obtain

$$
\mathbf {O b j} _ {c l e a n} = \underset {x, y = y (x), \rho} {\mathbb {E}} \left[ \mathbf {O b j} _ {T _ {\mathrm {f}}} (w ^ {(T _ {\mathrm {f}})}; x, y, \rho) \right] \leq o (1).
$$

Next, suppose  $\sigma_x \leq O(1)$  and  $k^{2.5} < d^{1 - 2c_0} / \log d$ . Starting from iteration  $T_{\mathbf{f}}$ , suppose we perform robust training for additional  $T = T_{\mathbf{g}} = \Theta \left(\frac{k^2 \Xi_2^4 m \log d}{\eta d}\right) \leq O\left(\frac{k^2}{d^{1 - 2c_0}}\right)$  iterations, against some  $\ell_{\infty}$  perturbation algorithm  $A$  of radius  $\tau \stackrel{\mathrm{def}}{=} \frac{1}{k^{1.75} \cdot \| \mathbf{M} \|_{\infty} \cdot d^{c_0}}$ . Then, with probability  $\geq 1 - e^{-\Omega (\log^2 d)}$ ,

$$
