\frac {1}{T} \sum_ {t = T _ {\mathrm {f}}} ^ {T _ {\mathrm {f}} + T - 1} \mathbb {E} _ {x, y = y (x), \delta = A \left(f _ {t}, x, y, r\right), \rho} \left[ \mathbf {O b j} _ {t} \left(w ^ {(t)}; x + \delta , y, \rho\right) \right] \leq \mathbf {O b j} _ {\text {c l e a n}} + o (1)
$$

Corollary F.5. In Theorem F.4, if  $A$  is Fast Gradient Method (FGM) with  $\ell_{\infty}$  radius  $\tau$ , and

$$
\underset {x, y = y (x), \delta = A (f _ {t}, x, y), \rho} {\mathbb {E}} \left[ \mathbf {O b j} _ {t} (w ^ {(t)}; x + \delta , y, \rho) \right] \leq o (1)
$$

for some  $t \in [T_{\mathsf{f}}, T_{\mathsf{f}} + T_{\mathsf{g}}]$ . Then,

$$
\mathop{\bf Pr}\limits_{x,y = y(x)}\left[\exists \delta \in \mathbb{R}^{d},\| \delta \|_{\infty}\leq \tau \colon \operatorname {sign}\bigl(\underset {\rho}{\mathbb{E}}f_{t}(w^{(t)};x + \delta ,\rho)\bigr)\neq y\right]\leq o(1)  .
$$

# Corollary F.6.

- For  $k \in [d^{0.0001}, d^{0.3999}]$ , robust training gives 99.9% accuracy against  $\ell_{\infty}$  perturbation  $\geq \frac{1}{k^{1.75} \cdot d^{0.0001} \cdot \| \mathbf{M} \|_{\infty}}$ .  
- For  $k \geq d^{0.3334}$ , clean training gives  $0.01\%$  accuracy against  $\ell_{\infty}$  perturbation  $\leq \frac{d^{0.0001}\|\mathbf{M}\|_1}{k^2}$ .  
- For  $k \in [d^{0.3334}, d^{0.3999}]$  and when  $\| \mathbf{M} \|_{\infty}, \| \mathbf{M} \|_1 \leq d^{0.1248}$ , robust training provably beats clean training in  $\ell_{\infty}$  robust accuracy.

Remark F.7. With additional efforts, one can also prove that Theorem F.1 and Theorem F.4 holds with high probability for all  $T$  in the range  $T = \Theta (T_{\mathbf{g}})$ . We do not prove it here since it is not beyond the scope of this paper.

# F.1 Some Notations

We first note some simple structural properties that are corollaries of Theorem C.2.

Proposition F.8. At iteration  $t = T_{\mathsf{f}}$ , for every neuron  $i \in [m]$ , we can write

$$
w _ {i} ^ {(t)} \stackrel {\mathrm {d e f}} {=} g _ {i} + u _ {i} \stackrel {\mathrm {d e f}} {=} \sum_ {j \in \mathcal {S} _ {i}} \alpha_ {i, j} \mathbf {M} _ {r} + u _ {i}
$$

where  $\mathcal{S}_i\subseteq \{j\in [d]\mid i\in \mathcal{S}_{j,pot}^{(0)}\}$  with  $|\mathcal{S}_i| = O(1)$ ,  $|\alpha_{i,j}|\leq O(\Xi_2^2)$  and  $\max_{j\in [d]}\{| \langle u_i,\mathbf{M}_j\rangle |\} = \frac{k\Xi_2^2}{d}$ .

Proof. We can let  $\alpha_{i,j} = \langle w_i^{(t)},\mathbf{M}_j\rangle$  and let  $u_{i}$  be the remaining part. We have  $|\mathcal{S}_i|\leq O(1)$  because  $\mathcal{S}_{ept}^{(0)} = [m]$ . We have  $|\alpha_{i,j}| = |\langle w_i^{(t)},\mathbf{M}_j\rangle |\leq \| w_i^{(t)}\| \leq O(\Xi_2^2)$ . We also have

$$
\max _ {j \in [ d ]} \{\vert \langle u _ {i}, {\bf M} _ {j} \rangle \vert \} = \max _ {j \in [ d ]: i \not \in S _ {j, p o t +} ^ {(0)}} \{\vert \langle w _ {i} ^ {(t)}, {\bf M} _ {j} \rangle \vert \} \leq \frac {k}{d \beta} b ^ {(t)} \leq \frac {k \Xi_ {2} ^ {2}}{d}.
$$

![](images/d87619feffc3c9edba164a751978f51a1b0c4a34aa7121b5ed2ff30b74443246.jpg)

We next introduce an important notation that shall be used throughout the proofs of this section.

Definition F.9. For every  $t \geq T_{\mathsf{f}}$ , we write  $w_i^{(t)} = g_i + v_i^{(t)}$  by defining  $v_i^{(t)} \stackrel{\mathrm{def}}{=} w_i^{(t)} - g_i$ .

# F.2 Robust Coupling

Definition F.10 (robust coupling). At every iteration  $t$ , recalling  $w_{i}^{(t)} = g_{i} + v_{i}^{(t)}$ , we define a linear function in  $\mu$

$$
\begin{array}{l} g _ {t} (\mu ; x, x _ {0}, \rho) = \sum_ {i = 1} ^ {m} \left(\mathbb {1} _ {\langle g _ {i} + v _ {i} ^ {(t)}, x _ {0} \rangle + \rho_ {i} \geq b ^ {(t)}} \cdot \left(\langle g _ {i} + \mu_ {i}, x \rangle + \rho_ {i} - b ^ {(t)}\right) \right. \\ \left. - \mathbb {1} _ {- \langle g _ {i} + v _ {i} ^ {(t)}, x _ {0} \rangle + \rho_ {i} \geq b ^ {(t)}} \cdot \left(- \langle g _ {i} + \mu_ {i}, x \rangle + \rho_ {i} - b ^ {(t)}\right)\right) \\ \end{array}
$$

and it equals the output of the real network at point  $\mu = v^{(t)}$  both on its zero and first order:

$$
\begin{array}{l} \left. g _ {t} (\mu ; x + \delta , x + \delta , \rho) \right| _ {\mu = v ^ {(t)}} = f _ {t} (w; x + \delta , \rho) \big | _ {w = w ^ {(t)}} \\ \left. \nabla_ {\mu} g _ {t} (\mu ; x + \delta , x + \delta , \rho) \right| _ {\mu = v ^ {(t)}} = \left. \nabla_ {w} f _ {t} (w; x + \delta , \rho) \right| _ {w = w ^ {(t)}} \\ \end{array}
$$

We shall show in this section that, recalling  $w^{(t)} = g + v^{(t)}$ , then

$$
g _ {t} (v ^ {(t)}; x + \delta , x, \rho) \approx f _ {t} (w ^ {(t)}; x + \delta , \rho)
$$

$$
g _ {t} (0; x + \delta , x, \rho) \approx f _ {t} (w ^ {(T _ {\mathrm {f}})}, x, \rho)
$$

However, as regarding how close they are, it depends on whether we have an  $\ell_2$  bound or  $\ell_{\infty}$  bound on  $\delta$ , so we shall prove the two cases separately in Section F.2.1 and F.2.2.

It is perhaps worth noting that the "closeness" of the above terms depend on two things,

- One is regarding how small  $\sum_{i\in [m]}\| v_i^{(t)}\| _2^2$  is, and this shall later be automatically guaranteed via implicit regularization of first-order methods.  
- The other is regarding how small  $\| v_{i}^{(t)}\|_{2}$  or  $\| v_{i}^{(t)}\|_{1}$  is for every individual neuron  $i\in [m]$ . This is a bit non-trivial to prove, and we shall spend the entire Section F.3 to deal with this.

# F.2.1 Robust Coupling for  $\ell_2$  Perturbation

Lemma F.11. Suppose at iteration  $t$ ,  $\sum_{i\in [m]}\| v_i^{(t)}\| _2^2\leq r^2 m$  for some  $r\leq 1$ , and suppose  $\max_{i\in [m]}\| v_i^{(t)}\| _2\leq r'$ . Then for any vector  $\delta \in \mathbb{R}^d$  that can depend on  $x$  (but not on  $\rho$ ) with  $\| \delta \| _2\leq \tau$  for some  $\tau \leq o(\frac{b}{\Xi_2^2 + r'})$ , we have

$$
\underset {x, \rho} {\mathbb {E}} \left[ \big | g _ {t} (v ^ {(t)}; x + \delta , x, \rho) - f _ {t} (w ^ {(t)}; x + \delta , \rho) \big | \right] \leq O (\tau^ {2}) \cdot \left(\frac {\Xi_ {2} ^ {5}}{\sigma_ {\rho}} + \frac {(\Xi_ {2} ^ {2} + r ^ {\prime}) ^ {2} r ^ {2} m}{d b ^ {2} \sigma_ {\rho}}\right)
$$

As a corollary, in the event of  $r \leq O\left(\frac{k\Xi_2^2}{\sqrt{d}}\right)$  and  $r' \leq 1$  and using  $m = d^{1 + c_0}$ , we have

$$
\underset {x, \rho} {\mathbb {E}} \left[ \left| g _ {t} (v ^ {(t)}; x + \delta , x, \rho) - f _ {t} (w ^ {(t)}; x + \delta , \rho) \right| \right] \leq O (\tau^ {2}) \cdot \left(\frac {\Xi_ {2} ^ {5}}{\sigma_ {\rho}} + \frac {k ^ {3 . 5}}{d ^ {1 - 2 c _ {0}}}\right)
$$

Proof of Lemma F.11. Let us abbreviate the notations by setting  $v_{i} = v_{i}^{(t)}$  and  $b = b^{(t)}$ .

To upper bound  $|g_t(v^{(t)}; x + \delta, x, \rho) - f_t(w^{(t)}; x + \delta, \rho)|$  it suffices to upper bound  $|V_1 - V_2|$  for

$$
V _ {1} := \sum_ {i \in [ m ]} \left(\left\langle g _ {i} + v _ {i}, x + \delta \right\rangle + \rho_ {i} - b\right) \mathbb {1} _ {\left\langle g _ {i} + v _ {i}, x + \delta \right\rangle + \rho_ {i} \geq b}
$$

$$
V _ {2} := \sum_ {i \in [ m ]} \left(\left\langle g _ {i} + v _ {i}, x + \delta \right\rangle + \rho_ {i} - b\right) \mathbb {1} _ {\left\langle g _ {i} + v _ {i}, x \right\rangle + \rho_ {i} \geq b}
$$

(and one also needs to take into account the reverse part, whose proof is analogous).

We first make some calculations. Using the definition of  $g_{i}$ , we have  $\mathbf{Pr}_x[\langle g_i,x\rangle \geq |b| / 10]\leq O\left(\frac{k}{d}\right)$  for every  $i\in [m]$ . Thus, we can easily calculate that<sup>22</sup>

$$
\mathbb {E} _ {x} \left[ \sum_ {i \in [ m ]} \langle v _ {i}, \delta \rangle^ {2} \mathbb {1} _ {\langle g _ {i}, x \rangle \geq | b | / 1 0} \right] \leq \tau^ {2} \sum_ {i \in [ m ]} \| v _ {i} \| ^ {2} \mathbb {E} _ {x} \left[ \mathbb {1} _ {\langle g _ {i}, x \rangle \geq | b | / 1 0} \right] = O \left(\tau^ {2} \cdot r ^ {2} m \cdot \frac {k}{d}\right) (F. 1)
$$

$$
\begin{array}{l} \underset {x} {\mathbb {E}} \left[ \sum_ {i \in [ m ]} (\langle v _ {i}, \delta \rangle^ {2} + \langle g _ {i}, \delta \rangle^ {2}) \mathbb {1} _ {\langle v _ {i}, x \rangle \geq | b | / 1 0} \right] \leq \tau^ {2} \cdot O (\Xi_ {2} ^ {4} + (r ^ {\prime}) ^ {2}) \sum_ {i \in [ m ]} \underset {x} {\mathbb {E}} \left[ \mathbb {1} _ {\langle v _ {i}, x \rangle \geq | b | / 1 0} \right] \\ \leq \tau^ {2} \cdot O \left(\left(\Xi_ {2} ^ {2} + r ^ {\prime}\right) ^ {2}\right) \cdot \sum_ {i \in [ m ]} O \left(\frac {\mathbb {E}}{x} \frac {\langle v _ {i} , x \rangle^ {2}}{b ^ {2}}\right) \\ = O \left(\tau^ {2} \left(\Xi_ {2} ^ {2} + r ^ {\prime}\right) ^ {2} \cdot \frac {r ^ {2} m}{d b ^ {2}}\right) \tag {F.2} \\ \end{array}
$$

$$
\sum_ {i \in [ m ]} \langle g _ {i}, \delta \rangle^ {2} \mathbb {1} _ {\langle g _ {i}, x \rangle \geq | b | / 1 0} \leq \sum_ {i \in [ m ]} \langle g _ {i}, \delta \rangle^ {2} \leq \tau^ {2} \left\| \sum_ {i \in [ m ]} g _ {i} g _ {i} ^ {\top} \right\| _ {\text {s p e c t r a l - n o r m}} \leq O \left(\tau^ {2} \Xi_ {2} ^ {5}\right) \tag {F.3}
$$

Now, for every  $i\in [m]$

- Case 1,  $|\langle v_i,x\rangle |\leq \frac{b}{10}$  and  $|\langle g_i,x\rangle |\leq \frac{b}{10}$  both happen. In this case, it must satisfy  $|\langle g_i + v_i,\delta \rangle |\leq (\| g_i\| +\| v_i\|)\cdot \tau \leq O(\Xi_2^2 +r')\cdot \tau \leq \frac{b}{10}$ . Also, with probability at least  $1 - e^{-\Omega (\log^2 d)}$ , it satisfies

$\left|\rho_{i}\right|\leq \frac{b}{10}$  . To sum up, with high probability we have

$$
\mathbb {1} _ {\langle g _ {i} + v _ {i}, x + \delta \rangle + \rho_ {i} \geq b} = \mathbb {1} _ {\langle g _ {i} + v _ {i}, x \rangle + \rho_ {i} \geq b} = 0
$$

- Case 2, either  $|\langle v_i,x\rangle| > \frac{b}{10}$  or  $|\langle g_i,x\rangle| > \frac{b}{10}$ . In this case, to satisfy  $\mathbb{1}_{\langle g_i + v_i,x + \delta \rangle + \rho_i \geq b} \neq \mathbb{1}_{\langle g_i + v_i,x\rangle + \rho_i \geq b}$ , one must have  $|\langle g_i + v_i,x + \delta \rangle - b + \rho_i| \leq |\langle v_i,\delta \rangle| + |\langle g_i,\delta \rangle|$ . Also, using the randomness of  $\rho_i$ , we have

$$
\mathop{\bf P r}\limits_{\rho_{i}}\left[\mathbb{1}_{\langle g_{i} + v_{i},x + \delta \rangle +\rho_{i}\geq b}\neq \mathbb{1}_{\langle g_{i} + v_{i},x\rangle +\rho_{i}\geq b}\right]\leq O\left(\frac{|\langle v_{i},\delta\rangle| + |\langle g_{i},\delta\rangle|}{\sigma_{\rho}}\right)
$$

Together, we have

$$
\begin{array}{l} \underset {x, \rho , \delta} {\mathbb {E}} [ | V _ {1} - V _ {2} | ] \leq \underset {x, \delta , \rho} {\mathbb {E}} \left[ \sum_ {i \in [ m ]} \left(| \langle v _ {i}, \delta \rangle | + | \langle g _ {i}, \delta \rangle |\right) \left| \mathbb {1} _ {\langle g _ {i} + v _ {i}, x + \delta \rangle + \rho_ {i} \geq b} - \mathbb {1} _ {\langle g _ {i} + v _ {i}, x \rangle + \rho_ {i} \geq b} \right| \right] \\ \leq O (1) \cdot \underset {x, \delta , \rho} {\mathbb {E}} \left[ \sum_ {i \in [ m ]} \frac {\langle v _ {i} , \delta \rangle^ {2} + \langle g _ {i} , \delta \rangle^ {2}}{\sigma_ {\rho}} \left(\mathbb {1} _ {\langle v _ {i}, x \rangle \geq | b | / 1 0} + \mathbb {1} _ {\langle g _ {i}, x \rangle \geq | b | / 1 0}\right) \right] + \frac {1}{\operatorname {p o l y} (d)} \tag {F.4} \\ \leq O (\tau^ {2}) \cdot \left(\frac {\Xi_ {2} ^ {5}}{\sigma_ {\rho}} + \frac {k r ^ {2} m}{d \sigma_ {\rho}} + \frac {(\Xi_ {2} ^ {2} + r ^ {\prime}) ^ {2} r ^ {2} m}{d b ^ {2} \sigma_ {\rho}}\right) \\ \leq O (\tau^ {2}) \cdot \left(\frac {\Xi_ {2} ^ {5}}{\sigma_ {\rho}} + \frac {(\Xi_ {2} ^ {2} + r ^ {\prime}) ^ {2} r ^ {2} m}{d b ^ {2} \sigma_ {\rho}}\right) . \\ \end{array}
$$

Lemma F.12. Suppose at iteration  $t$ ,  $\max_{i\in [m],j\in [d]}\{|\langle u_i,\mathbf{M}_j\rangle |\} \leq \frac{r}{\sqrt{d}}$  and  $\sum_{i\in [m]}\| v_i^{(t)}\| _2^2\leq r^2 m$  with  $r\leq 1$ . Then for any vector  $\delta \in \mathbb{R}^d$  that can depend on  $x$  (but not on  $\rho$ ) with  $\| \delta \| _2\leq \tau$ , we have

$$
\underset {x, \rho} {\mathbb {E}} \left[ \left| g _ {t} (0; x + \delta , x, \rho) - f _ {t} \left(w ^ {(T _ {\mathrm {f}})}, x, \rho\right) \right| \right] \leq O \left(\frac {m r ^ {2}}{d \sigma_ {\rho}} + k \Xi_ {2} \frac {r \log d}{\sqrt {d}} + \tau \Xi_ {2} ^ {3} \sqrt {k \Xi_ {2} + \frac {r ^ {2} m}{d b ^ {2}}}\right)
$$

As a corollary, in the event of  $r \leq O\left(\frac{k\Xi_2^2}{\sqrt{d}}\right)$  and using  $m = d^{1 + c_0}$ , we have

$$
\underset {x, \rho} {\mathbb {E}} \left[ \left| g _ {t} (0; x + \delta , x, \rho) - f _ {t} \left(w ^ {(T _ {\mathrm {f}})}, x, \rho\right) \right| \right] \leq O \left(\frac {k ^ {2 . 5}}{d ^ {1 - 2 c _ {0}}} + \tau \cdot \sqrt {k \Xi_ {2} ^ {7}}\right)
$$

Proof of Lemma F.12. To upper bound  $|g_t(0; x + \delta, x, \rho) - f_t(v^{(T_f)}, x)|$  it suffices to upper bound  $|V_3 - V_4|$  for

$$
V _ {3} := \sum_ {i \in [ m ]} (\langle g _ {i}, x + \delta \rangle - b + \rho_ {i}) \mathbb {1} _ {\langle g _ {i} + v _ {i}, x \rangle + \rho_ {i} \geq b}
$$

$$
V _ {4} := \sum_ {i \in [ m ]} \left(\left\langle g _ {i} + u _ {i}, x \right\rangle - b + \rho_ {i}\right) \mathbb {1} _ {\left\langle g _ {i} + u _ {i}, x \right\rangle + \rho_ {i} \geq b}
$$

(and one also needs to take into account the reverse part, whose proof is analogous).

Let us first define

$$
V _ {5} := \sum_ {i \in [ m ]} \left(\langle g _ {i}, x \rangle - b + \rho_ {i}\right) \mathbb {1} _ {\langle g _ {i} + v _ {i}, x \rangle + \rho_ {i} \geq b}
$$

Let us define  $s = \sum_{i\in [m]}\mathbb{1}_{\langle g_i + v_i,x\rangle +\rho_i\geq b}$ . By the properties that (1)  $g_{i}$  is only supported on  $S_{i}$  with  $|\mathcal{S}_i|\leq O(1)$ , (2) for each  $j\in [d]$  at most  $\Xi_2$  of the  $g_{i}$  are supported on  $i$ , and (3)  $\| g_i\| _2\leq O(\Xi_2^2)$ ,

we can obtain

$$
\underset {x, \rho} {\mathbb {E}} \left\| \sum_ {i \in [ m ]} g _ {i} \mathbb {1} _ {\langle g _ {i} + v _ {i}, x \rangle + \rho_ {i} \geq b} \right\| _ {2} \leq O (\Xi_ {2} ^ {3}) \underset {x, \rho} {\mathbb {E}} [ \sqrt {s} ] \leq O (\Xi_ {2} ^ {3}) \sqrt {\underset {x , \rho} {\mathbb {E}} [ s ]}
$$

At the same time, we know

$$
\begin{array}{l} \underset {x, \rho} {\mathbb {E}} [ s ] \leq \underset {x, \rho} {\mathbb {E}} \left[ \sum_ {i \in [ m ]} \mathbb {1} _ {\langle g _ {i}, x \rangle \geq \frac {b}{4}} + \mathbb {1} _ {\langle v _ {i}, x \rangle \geq \frac {b}{4}} \right] + \frac {1}{\operatorname {p o l y} (d)} \leq O (k \Xi_ {2}) + O \left(\sum_ {i \in [ m ]} \frac {\mathbb {E} [ \langle v _ {i} , x \rangle^ {2} ]}{b ^ {2}}\right) \\ \leq O \left(k \Xi_ {2} + r ^ {2} m \cdot \frac {1}{d b ^ {2}}\right) \\ \end{array}
$$

Putting them together, we have

$$
\mathbb {E} _ {x, \rho} | V _ {3} - V _ {5} | \leq \tau \cdot \mathbb {E} _ {x, \rho} \left\| \sum_ {i \in [ m ]} g _ {i} \mathbb {1} _ {\langle g _ {i} + v _ {i}, x \rangle + \rho_ {i} \geq b} \right\| _ {2} \leq O \left(\tau \Xi_ {2} ^ {3} \sqrt {k \Xi_ {2} + \frac {r ^ {2} m}{d b ^ {2}}}\right) \tag {F.5}
$$

Next, let us define

$$
V _ {6} := \sum_ {i \in [ m ]} \left(\langle g _ {i}, x \rangle - b + \rho_ {i}\right) \mathbb {1} _ {\langle g _ {i}, x \rangle + \rho_ {i} \geq b}
$$

Using a similar analysis to (F.4), we have

$$
\begin{array}{l} \mathbb {E} _ {x, \rho} | V _ {5} - V _ {6} | \leq \mathbb {E} _ {x, \rho} \sum_ {i \in [ m ]} | \langle g _ {i}, x \rangle - b + \rho_ {i} | \cdot \left| \mathbb {1} _ {\langle g _ {i}, x \rangle + \rho_ {i} \geq b} - \mathbb {1} _ {\langle g _ {i} + v _ {i}, x \rangle + \rho_ {i} \geq b} \right| \\ \leq \underset {x, \rho} {\mathbb {E}} \sum_ {i \in [ m ]} | \langle v _ {i}, x \rangle | \mathbb {E} [ | \mathbb {1} _ {\langle g _ {i}, x \rangle + \rho_ {i} \geq b} - \mathbb {1} _ {\langle g _ {i} + v _ {i}, x \rangle + \rho_ {i} \geq b} | ] \\ \leq O \left(\mathbb {E} _ {x, \rho} \frac {\langle v _ {i} , x \rangle^ {2}}{\sigma_ {\rho}}\right) \leq O \left(\frac {r ^ {2} m}{d \sigma_ {\rho}}\right) \tag {F.6} \\ \end{array}
$$

Finally, we also have

$$
\begin{array}{l} \underset {x, \rho} {\mathbb {E}} | V _ {6} - V _ {4} | = \underset {x, \rho} {\mathbb {E}} \left| \sum_ {i \in [ m ]} (\langle g _ {i} + u _ {i}, x \rangle - b + \rho_ {i}) \mathbb {1} _ {\langle g _ {i} + u _ {i}, x \rangle + \rho_ {i} \geq b} - (\langle g _ {i}, x \rangle - b + \rho_ {i}) \mathbb {1} _ {\langle g _ {i}, x \rangle + \rho_ {i} \geq b} \right| \\ \leq \mathbb {E} _ {x, \rho} \left| \sum_ {i \in [ m ]} \langle u _ {i}, x \rangle \mathbb {1} _ {\langle g _ {i} + u _ {i}, x \rangle + \rho_ {i} \geq b} \right| + \mathbb {E} _ {x, \rho} \left| \sum_ {i \in [ m ]} \left(\langle g _ {i}, x \rangle - b + \rho_ {i}\right) \left(\mathbb {1} _ {\langle g _ {i} + u _ {i}, x \rangle + \rho_ {i} \geq b} - \mathbb {1} _ {\langle g _ {i}, x \rangle + \rho_ {i} \geq b}\right) \right| \\ \stackrel {1} {\leq} \underset {x, \rho} {\mathbb {E}} \sum_ {i \in [ m ]} | \langle u _ {i}, x \rangle | \mathbb {1} _ {\langle g _ {i} + u _ {i}, x \rangle + \rho_ {i} \geq b} + O \left(\frac {m r ^ {2}}{d \sigma_ {\rho}}\right) \\ \stackrel {{2}} {\leq} \underset {x, \rho} {\mathbb {E}} \sum_ {i \in [ m ]} | \langle u _ {i}, x \rangle | \left(\mathbb {1} _ {\langle g _ {i}, x \rangle \geq b / 4} + \mathbb {1} _ {\langle u _ {i}, x \rangle \geq b / 4}\right) + O \left(\frac {m r ^ {2}}{d \sigma_ {\rho}}\right) + \frac {1}{\operatorname {p o l y} (d)} \\ \end{array}
$$

Above, inequality ① is due to a similar analysis as (F.6), and inequality ② is because  $|\rho_i| \leq b/4$  with probability at least  $1 - e^{-\Omega (\log^2 d)}$ . Next, let us recall  $\langle u_i, \mathbf{M}_j \rangle \leq \frac{r}{\sqrt{d}}$  and thus, by Bernstein's inequality, with probability at least  $1 - e^{-\Omega (\log^2 d)}$ ,

$$
| \langle u _ {i}, x \rangle | \leq O \left(\frac {r \log^ {2} d}{\sqrt {d}}\right) \ll \frac {b}{4}.
$$

Putting this back we have

$$
\underset {x, \rho} {\mathbb {E}} | V _ {6} - V _ {4} | \leq O \left(\frac {m r ^ {2}}{d \sigma_ {\rho}} + k \Xi_ {2} \frac {r \log d}{\sqrt {d}}\right)
$$

Combining the bounds on  $|V_6 - V_4|, |V_3 - V_5|$ , and  $|V_5 - V_6|$  finishes the proof.

![](images/ebdf39d5256cc043992e3da611456154af37b97bd7fb370dd5a7ab1884aada48.jpg)

# F.2.2 Robust Coupling for  $\ell_{\infty}$  Perturbation

Lemma F.13. Suppose at iteration  $t$ ,  $\sum_{i\in [m]}\| v_i^{(t)}\| _2^2\leq r^2 m$  for some  $r\leq 1$ , and suppose  $\max_{i\in [m]}\| v_i^{(t)}\| _1\leq r'$ . Then for any vector  $\delta \in \mathbb{R}^d$  that can depend on  $x$  (but not on  $\rho$ ) with  $\| \delta \|_{\infty}\leq \tau$  for some  $\tau \leq o(\frac{b}{\Xi_2^2 + r'})$ , we have

$$
\underset {x, \rho} {\mathbb {E}} \left[ \left| g _ {t} (v ^ {(t)}; x + \delta , x, \rho) - f _ {t} (w ^ {(t)}; x + \delta , \rho) \right| \right] \leq O (\tau^ {2}) \cdot \left(\frac {k \Xi_ {2} ^ {5}}{\sigma_ {\rho}} + \frac {(\Xi_ {2} ^ {2} + r ^ {\prime}) ^ {2} r ^ {2} m}{d b ^ {2} \sigma_ {\rho}} + \frac {(r ^ {\prime}) ^ {2} k \Xi_ {2}}{\sigma_ {\rho}}\right)
$$

As a corollary, in the event of  $r \leq O\left(\frac{k\Xi_2^2}{\sqrt{d}}\right)$  and  $r' \leq O(k\Xi_2^2 \cdot \| \mathbf{M}\|_{\infty})$  and using  $m = d^{1 + c_0}$ , we have

$$
\underset {x, \rho} {\mathbb {E}} \left[ \big | g _ {t} (v ^ {(t)}; x + \delta , x, \rho) - f _ {t} (w ^ {(t)}; x + \delta , \rho) \big | \right] \leq O (\tau^ {2}) \cdot k ^ {3. 5} d ^ {c _ {0}} \cdot \| \mathbf {M} \| _ {\infty} ^ {2}
$$

Proof of Lemma F.13. The proof is analogous to Lemma F.11 so we only highly the differences. In fact, we only need to change (F.1), (F.2) and (F.3) with the following calculations.

Using the definition of  $g_{i}$ , we have  $\mathbf{Pr}_x[\langle g_i,x\rangle \geq |b| / 10]\leq O\left(\frac{k}{d}\right)$  for every  $i\in [m]$  as well as  $\sum_{i\in [m]}\mathbb{E}_x[\mathbb{1}_{\langle g_i,x\rangle \geq |b| / 10}]\leq O(k\Xi_2)$ . Thus, we can easily calculate that<sup>23</sup>

$$
\underset {x} {\mathbb {E}} \left[ \sum_ {i \in [ m ]} \langle v _ {i}, \delta \rangle^ {2} \mathbb {1} _ {\langle g _ {i}, x \rangle \geq | b | / 1 0} \right] \leq \tau^ {2} \sum_ {i \in [ m ]} (r ^ {\prime}) ^ {2} \underset {x} {\mathbb {E}} \left[ \mathbb {1} _ {\langle g _ {i}, x \rangle \geq | b | / 1 0} \right] = O \left(\tau^ {2} \cdot (r ^ {\prime}) ^ {2} \cdot k \Xi_ {2}\right)
$$

$$
\mathbb {E} _ {x} \left[ \sum_ {i \in [ m ]} (\langle v _ {i}, \delta \rangle^ {2} + \langle g _ {i}, \delta \rangle^ {2}) \mathbb {1} _ {\langle v _ {i}, x \rangle \geq | b | / 1 0} \right] \leq \tau^ {2} \cdot O (\Xi_ {2} ^ {4} + (r ^ {\prime}) ^ {2}) \sum_ {i \in [ m ]} \mathbb {E} _ {x} \left[ \mathbb {1} _ {\langle v _ {i}, x \rangle \geq | b | / 1 0} \right]
$$

$$
\begin{array}{l} \leq \tau^ {2} \cdot O \left(\left(\Xi_ {2} ^ {2} + r ^ {\prime}\right) ^ {2}\right) \cdot \sum_ {i \in [ m ]} O \left(\frac {\mathbb {E}}{x} \frac {\langle v _ {i} , x \rangle^ {2}}{b ^ {2}}\right) \\ = O \left(\tau^ {2} (\Xi_ {2} ^ {2} + r ^ {\prime}) ^ {2} \cdot \frac {r ^ {2} m}{d b ^ {2}}\right) \\ \end{array}
$$

$$
\underset {x} {\mathbb {E}} \left[ \sum_ {i \in [ m ]} \langle g _ {i}, \delta \rangle^ {2} \mathbb {1} _ {\langle g _ {i}, x \rangle \geq | b | / 1 0} \right] \leq O (\tau^ {2} \Xi_ {2} ^ {4}) \sum_ {i \in [ m ]} \underset {x} {\mathbb {E}} \left[ \mathbb {1} _ {\langle g _ {i}, x \rangle \geq | b | / 1 0} \right] \leq O (\tau^ {2} k \Xi_ {2} ^ {5})
$$

Putting those into the rest of the proof (to replace (F.1), (F.2) and (F.3)) finishes the proof.  $\square$

Lemma F.14. Suppose at iteration  $t$ ,  $\max_{i\in [m],j\in [d]}\{|\langle u_i,\mathbf{M}_j\rangle |\} \leq \frac{r}{\sqrt{d}}$  and  $\sum_{i\in [m]}\| v_i^{(t)}\| _2^2\leq r^2 m$  with  $r\leq 1$ . Then for any vector  $\delta \in \mathbb{R}^d$  that can depend on  $x$  (but not on  $\rho$ ) with  $\| \delta \|_{\infty}\leq \tau$ , we

have

$$
\underset {x, \rho} {\mathbb {E}} \left[ | g _ {t} (0; x + \delta , x, \rho) - f _ {t} (w ^ {(T _ {\mathfrak {f}})}; x, \rho) | \right] \leq O \left(\frac {m r ^ {2}}{d \sigma_ {\rho}} + k \Xi_ {2} \frac {r \log d}{\sqrt {d}} + \tau \Xi_ {2} ^ {3} \left(k \Xi_ {2} + \frac {r ^ {2} m}{d b ^ {2}}\right)\right)
$$

As a corollary, in the event of  $r \leq O\left(\frac{k\Xi_2^2}{\sqrt{d}}\right)$  and using  $m = d^{1 + c_0}$ , we have

$$
\underset {x, \rho} {\mathbb {E}} \left[ \left| g _ {t} (0; x + \delta , x, \rho) - f _ {t} \left(w ^ {(T _ {\mathrm {f}})}; x, \rho\right) \right| \right] \leq O \left(\frac {k ^ {2 . 5}}{d ^ {1 - 2 c _ {0}}} + \tau \cdot k \Xi_ {2} ^ {4}\right)
$$

Proof of Lemma F.14. The proof is analogous to Lemma F.12 so we only highly the differences. Recall we have defined

$$
V _ {3} := \sum_ {i \in [ m ]} \left(\langle g _ {i}, x + \delta \rangle - b + \rho_ {i}\right) \mathbb {1} _ {\langle g _ {i} + v _ {i}, x \rangle + \rho_ {i} \geq b}
$$

$$
V _ {4} := \sum_ {i \in [ m ]} (\langle g _ {i} + u _ {i}, x \rangle - b + \rho_ {i}) \mathbb {1} _ {\langle g _ {i} + u _ {i}, x \rangle + \rho_ {i} \geq b}
$$

$$
V _ {5} := \sum_ {i \in [ m ]} (\langle g _ {i}, x \rangle - b + \rho_ {i}) \mathbb {1} _ {\langle g _ {i} + v _ {i}, x \rangle + \rho_ {i} \geq b}
$$

$$
V _ {6} := \sum_ {i \in [ m ]} (\langle g _ {i}, x \rangle - b + \rho_ {i}) \mathbb {1} _ {\langle g _ {i}, x \rangle + \rho_ {i} \geq b}
$$

The bounds on  $\mathbb{E}|V_5 - V_6|$  and  $\mathbb{E}|V_6 - V_4|$  state exactly the same comparing to Lemma F.12 (because they do not have  $\delta$  involved). Let us now recalculate the difference  $|V_{3} - V_{5}|$ .

Let us define  $s = \sum_{i\in [m]}\mathbb{1}_{\langle g_i + v_i,x\rangle +\rho_i\geq b}$ . By the properties that (1)  $g_{i}$  is only supported on  $S_{i}$  with  $|\mathcal{S}_i|\leq O(1)$ , (2) for each  $j\in [d]$  at most  $\Xi_2$  of the  $g_{i}$  are supported on  $i$ , and (3)  $\| g_{i}\|_{2}\leq O(\Xi_{2}^{2})$ , we can obtain

$$
\underset {x, \rho} {\mathbb {E}} \left\| \sum_ {i \in [ m ]} g _ {i} \mathbb {1} _ {\langle g _ {i} + v _ {i}, x \rangle + \rho_ {i} \geq b} \right\| _ {1} \leq O (\Xi_ {2} ^ {3}) \underset {x, \rho} {\mathbb {E}} [ s ]
$$

At the same time, we know

$$
\begin{array}{l} \mathbb {E} _ {x, \rho} [ s ] \leq \mathbb {E} _ {x, \rho} \left[ \sum_ {i \in [ m ]} \mathbb {1} _ {\langle g _ {i}, x \rangle \geq \frac {b}{4}} + \mathbb {1} _ {\langle v _ {i}, x \rangle \geq \frac {b}{4}} \right] + \frac {1}{\mathsf {p o l y} (d)} \leq O (k \Xi_ {2}) + O \left(\sum_ {i \in [ m ]} \frac {\mathbb {E} [ \langle v _ {i} , x \rangle^ {2} ]}{b ^ {2}}\right) \\ \leq O \left(k \Xi_ {2} + r ^ {2} m \cdot \frac {1}{d b ^ {2}}\right) \\ \end{array}
$$

Putting them together, we have

$$
\underset {x, \rho , \delta} {\mathbb {E}} | V _ {3} - V _ {5} | \leq \tau \cdot \underset {x, \rho} {\mathbb {E}} \left\| \sum_ {i \in [ m ]} g _ {i} \mathbb {1} _ {\langle g _ {i} + v _ {i}, x \rangle + \rho_ {i} \geq b} \right\| _ {1} \leq O \left(\tau \Xi_ {2} ^ {3} \left(k \Xi_ {2} + \frac {r ^ {2} m}{d b ^ {2}}\right)\right)
$$

Using this new bound on  $\mathbb{E}[V_3 - V_5]$  to replace the old one (F.5), the rest of the proof follows.

# F.3 Individual Neuron Growth Lemma

As mentioned earlier, the purpose of this section is to upper bound  $\max_{i\in [m]}\| v_i^{(T_f + T)}\| _2$  (if it is  $\ell_2$  perturbation) or  $\max_{i\in [m]}\| v_i^{(T_f + T)}\| _1$  (if it is  $\ell_{\infty}$  perturbation) during the course of robust training. We have two subsections to deal with the two cases.

# F.3.1 Growth Lemma for  $\ell_2$  Perturbation

We first bound  $\| v_{i}^{(t)}\|_{2}$  during  $\ell_2$  robust training.

Lemma F.15 (movement bound). Suppose at iteration  $t$ ,  $\max_{i\in [m]}\| v_i\| _2\leq r'$ . Let  $\ell \in [-1,1]$  be any random variable that can depend on  $x,\rho$ , and  $\delta \in \mathbb{R}^d$  be any random vector that can depend on  $x$  with  $\| \delta \| _2\leq \tau$ . Then, for every  $i\in [m]$ ,

$$
\left\| \mathbb {E} _ {x, y = y (x), \rho} \left[ \ell \mathbb {1} _ {\langle g _ {i} + v _ {i}, x + \delta \rangle + \rho_ {i} \geq b} (x + \delta) \right] \right\| _ {2} \leq O \left(\left(\frac {k}{d} + \frac {(r ^ {\prime}) ^ {2} \log d}{d b ^ {2}}\right) \tau + \frac {\sqrt {k}}{d} + \frac {(r ^ {\prime}) ^ {2}}{d b ^ {2}} \left(\frac {\sqrt {k}}{\sqrt {d}} + \sigma_ {x} \log d\right) + \frac {r ^ {\prime}}{d b}\right)
$$

As a corollary, suppose we run robust training from iteration  $T_{\mathsf{f}}$  to  $T_{\mathsf{f}} + T$  with  $T\eta \leq o(db)$ ,  $\tau \leq \frac{1}{\sqrt{k}\log d}$  and  $\sigma_x \leq o(\frac{d^2b^2}{(T\eta)^2\sqrt{k}\log d})$ , then

$$
\max _ {i \in [ m ]} \| v _ {i} ^ {(T _ {\mathrm {f}} + T)} \| _ {2} \leq O \left(\frac {k \Xi_ {2} ^ {2}}{\sqrt {d}} + T \eta \cdot \frac {\sqrt {k}}{d}\right) \leq o (1)
$$

Proof of Lemma F.15. First of all we can reuse the analysis of (F.4) and derive that

$$
\begin{array}{l} \Pr [ \langle g _ {i} + v _ {i}, x + \delta \rangle + \rho_ {i} \geq b ] \leq \frac {1}{\operatorname {p o l y} (d)} + \Pr [ \langle g _ {i}, x \rangle \geq | b | / 1 0 ] + \Pr [ \langle v _ {i}, x \rangle \geq | b | / 1 0 ] \\ \leq O \left(\frac {k}{d} + \frac {\left(r ^ {\prime}\right) ^ {2}}{d b ^ {2}}\right) =: \kappa \\ \end{array}
$$

This immediately gives

$$
\left\| \mathbb {E} \left[ \ell \mathbb {1} _ {\langle g _ {i} + v _ {i}, x + \delta \rangle + \rho_ {i} \geq b} \delta \right] \right\| _ {2} \leq \tau \cdot \mathbb {E} \left[ \mathbb {1} _ {\langle g _ {i} + v _ {i}, x + \delta \rangle + \rho_ {i} \geq b} \right] \leq \kappa \tau .
$$

Next, in order to bound the norm of  $\phi \stackrel{\mathrm{def}}{=} \mathbb{E}\left[\ell \mathbb{1}_{\langle g_i + v_i, x + \delta \rangle + \rho_i \geq b}x\right]$ , we first inner product it with  $\mathbf{M}_j$  for each  $j \in [d]$ . This gives

$$
\begin{array}{l} | \langle \phi , \mathbf {M} _ {j} \rangle | = \left| \mathbb {E} \left[ \ell \mathbb {1} _ {\langle g _ {i} + v _ {i}, x + \delta \rangle + \rho_ {i} \geq b} \langle x, \mathbf {M} _ {j} \rangle \right] \right| \\ \leq \mathbb {E} \left[ \left(\mathbb {1} _ {\langle g _ {i}, x \rangle \geq b / 1 0} + \mathbb {1} _ {\langle v _ {i}, x \rangle \geq b / 1 0}\right) \cdot | \langle x, \mathbf {M} _ {j} \rangle | \right] + \frac {1}{\operatorname {p o l y} (d)} \\ \leq \mathbb {E} \left[ \left(\mathbb {1} _ {\langle g _ {i}, x \rangle \geq b / 1 0} + \mathbb {1} _ {\langle v _ {i}, x - \mathbf {M} _ {j} z _ {j} \rangle \geq b / 2 0} + \mathbb {1} _ {\langle v _ {i}, \mathbf {M} _ {j} \rangle z _ {j} \geq b / 2 0}\right) \cdot | \langle x, \mathbf {M} _ {j} \rangle | \right] + \frac {1}{\operatorname {p o l y} (d)} \tag {F.7} \\ \end{array}
$$

We bound the three terms separately.

- For the first term,

$$
\mathbb {E} [ \mathbb {1} _ {\langle g _ {i}, x \rangle \geq b / 1 0} \cdot | \langle x, \mathbf {M} _ {j} \rangle | ] \leq \mathbb {E} [ \mathbb {1} _ {\langle g _ {i}, x \rangle \geq b / 1 0} \cdot (| z _ {j} | + O (\frac {\log d}{\sqrt {d}})) ] \leq \mathbb {E} [ \mathbb {1} _ {\langle g _ {i}, x \rangle \geq b / 1 0} \cdot | z _ {j} | ] + O (\frac {k \log d}{d ^ {1 . 5}}))
$$

Using the property of  $g_{i}$  we have  $\mathbb{1}_{\langle g_i,x\rangle \geq b / 10}\leq \sum_{j'\in S_i}\mathbb{1}_{z_{j'}\neq 0}$  for  $|\mathcal{S}_i|\leq O(1)$  and therefore

$$
\mathbb {E} [ \mathbb {1} _ {\langle g _ {i}, x \rangle \geq b / 1 0} \cdot | z _ {j} | ] \leq \mathbb {E} \left[ \sum_ {j ^ {\prime} \in \mathcal {S} _ {i}} \mathbb {1} _ {z _ {j ^ {\prime}} \neq 0} \cdot | z _ {j} | \right] = \left\{ \begin{array}{l l} \sqrt {k} / d, & \mathrm {i f} j \in \mathcal {S} _ {i}; \\ k ^ {1. 5} / d ^ {2}, & \mathrm {i f} j \notin \mathcal {S} _ {i}. \end{array} \right.
$$

Therefore, we have

$$
\sum_ {j \in [ d ]} \left(\mathbb {E} \left[ \mathbb {1} _ {\langle g _ {i}, x \rangle \geq b / 1 0} \cdot | \langle x, \mathbf {M} _ {j} \rangle | \right]\right) ^ {2} \leq O \left(\frac {k}{d ^ {2}}\right) \tag {F.8}
$$

- For the second term,

$$
\begin{array}{l} \mathbb {E} \left[ \mathbb {1} _ {\langle v _ {i}, x - \mathbf {M} _ {j} z _ {j} \rangle \geq b / 2 0} \cdot | \langle x, \mathbf {M} _ {j} \rangle | \right] = \mathbb {E} \left[ \mathbb {1} _ {\langle v _ {i}, x - \mathbf {M} _ {j} z _ {j} \rangle \geq b / 2 0} \cdot | z _ {j} + \langle \mathbf {M} _ {j}, \xi \rangle | \right] \\ \leq \mathbb {E} \left[ \mathbb {1} _ {\langle v _ {i}, x - \mathbf {M} _ {j} z _ {j} \rangle \geq b / 2 0} \right] \cdot O \left(\mathbb {E} [ | z _ {j} | ] + \frac {\log d}{\sqrt {d}} \sigma_ {x}\right) \\ \leq O \left(\frac {\mathbb {E} \left[ \langle v _ {i} , x - \mathbf {M} _ {j} z _ {j} \rangle^ {2} \right]}{b ^ {2}}\right) \cdot O \left(\frac {\sqrt {k}}{d} + \frac {\log d}{\sqrt {d}} \sigma_ {x}\right) \\ \leq O \left(\frac {\left(r ^ {\prime}\right) ^ {2}}{d b ^ {2}}\right) \cdot O \left(\frac {\sqrt {k}}{d} + \frac {\log d}{\sqrt {d}} \sigma_ {x}\right) \\ \end{array}
$$

and therefore

$$
\sum_ {j \in [ d ]} \left(\mathbb {E} \left[ \mathbb {1} _ {\langle v _ {i}, x - \mathbf {M} _ {j} z _ {j} \rangle \geq b / 2 0} \cdot | \langle x, \mathbf {M} _ {j} \rangle | \right]\right) ^ {2} \leq O \left(\frac {(r ^ {\prime}) ^ {4}}{d ^ {2} b ^ {4}}\right) \cdot \left(\frac {k}{d} + \sigma_ {x} ^ {2} \log^ {2} d\right) \tag {F.9}
$$

- For the third term,

$$
\begin{array}{l} \mathbb {E} \left[ \mathbb {1} _ {\langle v _ {i}, \mathbf {M} _ {j} \rangle z _ {j} \geq b / 2 0} \cdot | \langle x, \mathbf {M} _ {j} \rangle | \right] \leq \mathbb {E} \left[ \mathbb {1} _ {\langle v _ {i}, \mathbf {M} _ {j} \rangle z _ {j} \geq b / 2 0} \cdot \left(| z _ {i} | + O (\frac {\log d}{\sqrt {d}})\right) \right] \\ \leq \mathbb {E} \left[ \left(\frac {| \langle v _ {i} , \mathbf {M} _ {j} \rangle z _ {j} |}{b} \cdot | z _ {i} | + \frac {| \langle v _ {i} , \mathbf {M} _ {j} \rangle z _ {j} |}{b} \cdot O (\frac {\log d}{\sqrt {d}})\right) \right] \\ \leq | \langle v _ {i}, \mathbf {M} _ {j} \rangle | \cdot O \left(\frac {1}{d b} + \frac {\sqrt {k} \log d}{b d ^ {1 . 5}}\right) \leq | \langle v _ {i}, \mathbf {M} _ {j} \rangle | \cdot O \left(\frac {1}{d b}\right) \\ \end{array}
$$

and therefore

$$
\sum_ {j \in [ d ]} \left(\mathbb {E} \left[ \mathbb {1} _ {\langle v _ {i}, \mathbf {M} _ {j} \rangle z _ {j} \geq b / 2 0} \cdot | \langle x, \mathbf {M} _ {j} \rangle | \right]\right) ^ {2} \leq O \left(\frac {\left(r ^ {\prime}\right) ^ {2}}{d ^ {2} b ^ {2}}\right) \tag {F.10}
$$

Putting (F.7), (F.8), (F.9), (F.10) these together, we have

$$
\| \phi \| ^ {2} \leq \sum_ {j \in [ d ]} | \langle \phi , \mathbf {M} _ {j} \rangle | ^ {2} \leq O \left(\frac {k}{d ^ {2}} + \frac {(r ^ {\prime}) ^ {4}}{d ^ {2} b ^ {4}} \left(\frac {k}{d} + \sigma_ {x} ^ {2} \log^ {2} d\right) + \frac {(r ^ {\prime}) ^ {2}}{d ^ {2} b ^ {2}}\right)
$$

Summing everything up, we have

$$
\left\| _ {x, y = y (x), \rho} \mathbb {E} \left[ \ell \mathbb {1} _ {\langle g _ {i} + v _ {i}, x + \delta \rangle + \rho_ {i} \geq b} (x + \delta) \right] \right\| _ {2} \leq O \left(\left(\frac {k}{d} + \frac {(r ^ {\prime}) ^ {2}}{d b ^ {2}}\right) \tau + \frac {\sqrt {k}}{d} + \frac {(r ^ {\prime}) ^ {2}}{d b ^ {2}} \left(\frac {\sqrt {k}}{\sqrt {d}} + \sigma_ {x} \log d\right) + \frac {r ^ {\prime}}{d b}\right)
$$

Now, suppose we run robust training for  $t = T_{\mathsf{f}}, T_{\mathsf{f}} + 1, \ldots, T_{\mathsf{f}} + T - 1$  and suppose for all of them we have  $\| v_i^{(T)} \|_2 \leq r'$  satisfied. Then, using the gradient update formula (see e.g. (C.19))

$$
\| v _ {i} ^ {(T _ {\mathrm {f}} + T)} \| _ {2} \leq \| v _ {i} ^ {(T _ {\mathrm {f}})} \| _ {2} + T \eta \cdot O \left(\left(\frac {k}{d} + \frac {(r ^ {\prime}) ^ {2}}{d b ^ {2}}\right) \tau + \frac {\sqrt {k}}{d} + \frac {(r ^ {\prime}) ^ {2}}{d b ^ {2}} \left(\frac {\sqrt {k}}{\sqrt {d}} + \sigma_ {x} \log d\right) + \frac {r ^ {\prime}}{d b}\right)
$$

This means, in order to show  $\| v_{i}^{(T_{\mathrm{f}} + T)} \|_{2} \leq r'$  we can choose any  $r' > 0$  satisfying

$$
O (\frac {k \Xi_ {2} ^ {2}}{\sqrt {d}}) + T \eta \cdot O \left(\left(\frac {k}{d} + \frac {(r ^ {\prime}) ^ {2}}{d b ^ {2}}\right) \tau + \frac {\sqrt {k}}{d} + \frac {(r ^ {\prime}) ^ {2}}{d b ^ {2}} \left(\frac {\sqrt {k}}{\sqrt {d}} + \sigma_ {x} \log d\right) + \frac {r ^ {\prime}}{d b}\right) \leq r ^ {\prime}.
$$

Using the assumption of  $T\eta \leq o(db)$  (which also implies  $(T\eta)^2 \leq o(\frac{d^{2.5}b^2}{k})$ ),  $\tau \leq \frac{1}{\sqrt{k}\log d}$  (which also

implies  $\tau \leq o\left(\frac{d^2b^2}{(T\eta)^2\sqrt{k}\log d}\right)$ , and  $\sigma_x \leq o\left(\frac{d^2b^2}{(T\eta)^2\sqrt{k}\log d}\right)$ , we can choose

$$
r ^ {\prime} \leq O \left(\frac {k \Xi_ {2} ^ {2}}{\sqrt {d}}\right) + T \eta \cdot O \left(\frac {\sqrt {k}}{d}\right).
$$

![](images/86c5968837554e366baaa696f6322178998ef49fb3b22d6ede18d70bc07d7680.jpg)

# F.3.2 Growth Lemma for  $\ell_{\infty}$  Perturbation

We now bound  $\| v_{i}^{(t)}\|_{1}$  during  $\ell_{\infty}$  robust training. Recall  $\| \mathbf{M}\|_{\infty}\stackrel {\mathrm{def}}{=}\max_{j\in [d]}\| \mathbf{M}_{j}\|_{1}$ .

Lemma F.16 (movement bound). Suppose at iteration  $t$ ,  $\max_{i\in [m]}\| v_i\| _1\leq r'$ . Let  $\ell \in [-1,1]$  be any random variable that can depend on  $x,\rho$ , and  $\delta \in \mathbb{R}^d$  be any random vector that can depend on  $x$  with  $\| \delta \|_{\infty}\leq \tau$ . Then, for every  $i\in [m]$ ,

$$
\| \mathbb {E} \left[ \ell \mathbb {1} _ {\langle g _ {i} + v _ {i}, x + \delta \rangle + \rho_ {i} \geq b} (x + \delta) \right] \| _ {1} \leq O \left(\frac {k}{d} + \frac {\left(r ^ {\prime}\right) ^ {2}}{d b ^ {2}}\right) \cdot (\tau d + \| \mathbf {M} \| _ {\infty} \log d)
$$

As a corollary, suppose we run robust training from iteration  $T_{\mathbf{f}}$  to  $T_{\mathbf{f}} + T$  with  $T\eta \leq \frac{db^2}{\|\mathbf{M}\|_{\infty}^2k\Xi_2^3}$  and  $\tau \leq o\left(\frac{b^2}{T\eta\cdot k\Xi_2^2\|\mathbf{M}\|_{\infty}}\right)$ , then

$$
\max _ {i \in [ m ]} \| v _ {i} ^ {(T _ {\mathrm {f}} + T)} \| _ {1} \leq O (k \Xi_ {2} ^ {2} \cdot \| \mathbf {M} \| _ {\infty})
$$

Proof of Lemma F.16. Similar to the proof of Lemma F.15, and using  $\| v_{i}\|_{2}\leq \| v_{i}\|_{1}\leq r^{\prime}$ , we have

$$
\begin{array}{l} \Pr [ \langle g _ {i} + v _ {i}, x + \delta \rangle + \rho_ {i} \geq b ] \leq \frac {1}{\operatorname {p o l y} (d)} + \Pr [ \langle g _ {i}, x \rangle \geq | b | / 1 0 ] + \Pr [ \langle v _ {i}, x \rangle \geq | b | / 1 0 ] \\ \leq O \left(\frac {k}{d} + \frac {\left(r ^ {\prime}\right) ^ {2}}{d b ^ {2}}\right) =: \kappa \\ \end{array}
$$

This implies

$$
\left\| \mathbb {E} \left[ \ell \mathbf {1} _ {\langle g _ {i} + v _ {i}, x + \delta \rangle + \rho_ {i} \geq b} \delta \right] \right\| _ {1} \leq \kappa \cdot \tau d
$$

On the other hand, let us look at  $h := \mathbb{E}\left[\ell \mathbb{1}_{\langle g_i + v_i,x + \delta \rangle +\rho_i\geq b}x\right]$  and set  $u = \mathrm{sign}(h)\in \{-1,1\} ^d$ . We have

$$
\left\| h \right\| _ {1} = \mathbb {E} \left[ \ell \mathbb {1} _ {\langle g _ {i} + v _ {i}, x + \delta \rangle + \rho_ {i} \geq b} \langle x, u \rangle \right]
$$

Since with probability at least  $1 - e^{-\Omega (\log d)}$  it satisfies  $|\langle x,u\rangle | = O(\max_{j\in [d]}\| \mathbf{M}_j\| _1\log d)$ , we can conclude that  $\| h\| _1 = O(\kappa \| \mathbf{M}\|_\infty \log d)$ . Together we have

$$
\left\| \mathbb {E} \left[ \ell \mathbb {1} _ {\langle g _ {i} + v _ {i}, x + \delta \rangle + \rho_ {i} \geq b} (x + \delta) \right] \right\| _ {1} \leq O \left(\frac {k}{d} + \frac {(r ^ {\prime}) ^ {2}}{d b ^ {2}}\right) \cdot (\tau d + \| \mathbf {M} \| _ {\infty} \log d)
$$

Now, suppose we run robust training for  $t = T_{\mathsf{f}}, T_{\mathsf{f}} + 1, \ldots, T_{\mathsf{f}} + T - 1$  and suppose for all of them we have  $\| v_i^{(T)} \|_1 \leq r'$  satisfied. Then, using the gradient update formula (see e.g. (C.19))

$$
\left\| v _ {i} ^ {\left(T _ {\mathrm {f}} + T\right)} \right\| _ {1} \leq \left\| v _ {i} ^ {\left(T _ {\mathrm {f}}\right)} \right\| _ {1} + T \eta \cdot O \left(\frac {k}{d} + \frac {\left(r ^ {\prime}\right) ^ {2}}{d b ^ {2}}\right) \cdot (\tau d + \left\| \mathbf {M} \right\| _ {\infty} \log d)
$$

Recalling  $|\langle v_i^{(T_{\mathfrak{f}})},\mathbf{M}_j\rangle |\leq \frac{k\Xi_2^2}{d}$  from (F.8), we have

$$
\left\| v _ {i} ^ {(T _ {\mathbf {f}})} \right\| _ {1} = \left\| \sum_ {j \in [ d ]} \langle v _ {i} ^ {(T _ {\mathbf {f}})}, \mathbf {M} _ {j} \rangle \cdot \mathbf {M} _ {j} \right\| _ {1} \leq k \Xi_ {2} ^ {2} \cdot \| \mathbf {M} \| _ {\infty}
$$

This means, to prove that  $\left\| v_{i}^{(T_{\mathfrak{f}})}\right\|_{1}\leq r^{\prime}$ , we can choose any  $r^\prime$  satisfying

$$
k \Xi_ {2} ^ {2} \cdot \| \mathbf {M} \| _ {\infty} + T \eta \cdot O \left(\frac {k}{d} + \frac {(r ^ {\prime}) ^ {2}}{d b ^ {2}}\right) \cdot (\tau d + \| \mathbf {M} \| _ {\infty} \log d) \leq r ^ {\prime}
$$

and using the assumption of  $T\eta \leq \frac{db^2}{\|\mathbf{M}\|_\infty^2k\Xi_2^3}$  (which implies  $T\eta \leq O(d)$ ), and  $\tau \leq o\left(\frac{b^2}{T\eta\cdot k\Xi_2^2\|\mathbf{M}\|_\infty}\right)$  (which implies  $\tau \leq \frac{1}{\eta T}$ ), we can choose

$$
r ^ {\prime} \leq 2 k \Xi_ {2} ^ {2} \cdot \| \mathbf {M} \| _ {\infty} + T \eta \cdot O (\tau k) \leq O (k \Xi_ {2} ^ {2} \cdot \| \mathbf {M} \| _ {\infty}).
$$

# F.4 Robust Convergence

We are now ready to prove the main convergence theorem (that is, Theorem F.1 and F.4) for robust learning. Let us first calculate a simple bound:

Claim F.17.  $\left|\sum_{i\in [m]}\mathbf{Reg}(g_i) - \mathbf{Reg}(w_i^{(T_f)})\right| \leq O(k\sqrt{d}\Xi_2^4)$

Proof. Recalling  $\| g_i\| _2\leq O(\Xi_2^2)$  and  $\| u_{i}\| \leq O\left(\frac{k\Xi_{2}^{2}}{\sqrt{d}}\right)$  from Proposition F.8, we have

$$
\left| \left\| g _ {i} \right\| ^ {3} - \left\| g _ {i} + u _ {i} \right\| ^ {3} \right| \leq O \left(\left\| u _ {i} \right\| \cdot \left(\left\| u _ {i} \right\| ^ {2} + \left\| g _ {i} \right\| ^ {2}\right)\right) \leq O \left(\frac {k \Xi_ {2} ^ {4}}{\sqrt {d}}\right).
$$

# F.4.1 Robust Convergence for  $\ell_2$  Perturbation

Proof of Theorem F.1. Since  $w_{i}^{(t + 1)} = w_{i}^{(t)} - \eta \nabla_{w_{i}}\widetilde{\mathbf{RobOb}}\mathbf{j}_{t}(w^{(t)})$ , we have the identity

$$
\eta \langle \widetilde {\nabla \mathbf {R o b O b}} \mathbf {j} _ {t} (w ^ {(t)}), w ^ {(t)} - g \rangle = \frac {\eta^ {2}}{2} \| \widetilde {\nabla \mathbf {R o b O b}} \mathbf {j} _ {t} (w ^ {(t)}) \| _ {F} ^ {2} + \frac {1}{2} \| w ^ {(t)} - g \| _ {F} ^ {2} - \frac {1}{2} \| w ^ {(t + 1)} - g \| _ {F} ^ {2}
$$

Applying (a variant of) Lemma A.2 (which requires us to use the Lipschitz continuity assumption on  $A$ , see Definition 4.2), we know that by letting

$$
\mathbf {R o b O b j} _ {t} (w) = \underset {x, y = y (x), \delta , \rho} {\mathbb {E}} \left[ \mathbf {O b j} _ {t} (w; x + \delta , y, \rho) \right],
$$

it satisfies

$$
\eta \langle \nabla \mathbf {R o b O b j} _ {t} (w ^ {(t)}), w ^ {(t)} - g \rangle \leq \eta^ {2} \cdot \mathsf {p o l y} (d) + \frac {1}{2} \| w ^ {(t)} - g \| _ {F} ^ {2} - \frac {1}{2} \| w ^ {(t + 1)} - g \| _ {F} ^ {2} + \frac {\eta}{\mathsf {p o l y} (d)} \quad (\mathrm {F}. 1 1)
$$

Let us also define the clean objective and the pseudo objective as follows:

$$
\mathbf {O b j} _ {t} (w) = \underset {x, y = y (x), \rho} {\mathbb {E}} \left[ \mathbf {O b j} _ {t} (w; x, y, \rho) \right]
$$

$$
\mathbf {R o b O b j} _ {t} ^ {\prime} (\mu) = \underset {x, y = y (x), \delta , \rho} {\mathbb {E}} \left[ \log \left(1 + e ^ {- y \cdot g _ {t} (\mu ; x + \delta , x, \rho)}\right) \right] + \lambda \sum_ {i \in [ m ]} \mathbf {R e g} \left(g _ {i} + \mu_ {i}\right),
$$

which is a convex function in  $\mu$  because  $g_{t}(\mu ;x + \delta ,x,\rho)$  is linear in  $\mu$

Now, we inductively prove that at every iteration  $t \in [T_{\mathfrak{f}}, T_{\mathfrak{f}} + T]$ , it satisfies

$$
\sum_ {i \in [ m ]} \| v _ {i} ^ {(t)} \| _ {2} ^ {2} \leq r ^ {2} m \quad \text {f o r} \quad r = \Theta \left(\frac {k \Xi_ {2} ^ {2}}{\sqrt {d}}\right) \tag {F.12}
$$

$$
\max  _ {i \in [ m ]} \| v _ {i} ^ {(t)} \| _ {2} \leq r ^ {\prime} \quad \text {f o r} \quad r ^ {\prime} = 1 \tag {F.13}
$$

In the base case  $t = T_{\mathbf{f}}$  this is obvious due to Proposition F.8. Next, suppose (F.12) and (F.13) hold at iteration  $t$ . Using the notation  $w_{i}^{(t)} = g_{i} + v^{(t)}$  and the Lipschitz continuity of  $\log (1 + e^t)$ , we have<sup>24</sup>

$$
\begin{array}{l} \mathbb {E} \left[ \left| \mathbf {R o b O b j} _ {t} ^ {\prime} (v ^ {(t)}) - \mathbf {R o b O b j} _ {t} (w ^ {(t)}) \right| \right] \leq \mathbb {E} \left[ \left| g _ {t} (v ^ {(t)}; x + \delta , x, \rho) - f _ {t} (w ^ {(t)}, x + \delta) \right| \right] \\ \leq O \left(\tau^ {2}\right) \cdot \left(\frac {\Xi_ {2} ^ {5}}{\sigma_ {\rho}} + \frac {k ^ {3 . 5}}{d ^ {1 - 2 c _ {0}}}\right) \quad \text {(u s i n g L e m m a F . 1 1)} \\ \leq O \left(\frac {1}{\log d}\right) \quad \text {(u s i n g} \tau \leq \frac {1}{\sqrt {k} \cdot d ^ {c _ {0}}}) \\ \end{array}
$$

$$
\begin{array}{l} \mathbb {E} [ | \mathbf {R o b O b j} _ {t} ^ {\prime} (0) - \mathbf {O b j} _ {t} (w ^ {(T _ {\mathrm {f}})}) | ] \leq \mathbb {E} [ | g _ {t} (0; x + \delta , x) - f _ {t} (w ^ {(T _ {\mathrm {f}})}, x) | ] + \lambda \left| \sum_ {i \in [ m ]} \mathbf {R e g} (w _ {i} ^ {(T _ {\mathrm {f}})}) - \mathbf {R e g} (g _ {i}) \right| \\ \leq O \left(\frac {k ^ {2 . 5}}{d ^ {1 - 2 c _ {0}}} + \tau \sqrt {k \Xi_ {2} ^ {7}}\right) + O \left(\frac {k \Xi_ {2} ^ {4} \log d}{\sqrt {d}}\right) \quad (\text {u s i n g L e m m a F . 1 2 a n d C l a i m F . 1 7}) \\ \leq O \left(\frac {1}{\log d} + \tau \sqrt {k \Xi_ {2} ^ {7}}\right) \quad \text {(u s i n g} k ^ {2. 5} <   d ^ {1 - 2 c _ {0}} / \log d) \\ \leq O \left(\frac {1}{\log d}\right) \quad (\text {u s i n g} \tau \leq \frac {1}{\sqrt {k} \cdot d ^ {c _ {0}}}) \\ \end{array}
$$

Therefore, we can bound the left hand side of (F.11) as follows:

$$
\begin{array}{l} \langle \nabla \mathbf {R o b O b j} _ {t} (w ^ {(t)}), w ^ {(t)} - g \rangle = \langle \nabla \mathbf {R o b O b j} _ {t} ^ {\prime} (v ^ {(t)}), v ^ {(t)} \rangle \\ \geq \mathbf {R o b O b j} _ {t} ^ {\prime} (v ^ {(t)}) - \mathbf {R o b O b j} _ {t} ^ {\prime} (0) \geq \mathbf {R o b O b j} _ {t} (w ^ {(t)}) - \mathbf {O b j} _ {t} (w ^ {(T _ {\mathrm {f}})}) - O \left(\frac {1}{\log d}\right). \\ \end{array}
$$

Putting this back to (F.11) and telescoping for  $t = T_{\mathfrak{f}}, T_{\mathfrak{f}} + 1, \ldots, T_{\mathfrak{f}} + T_0 - 1$  for any  $T_0 \leq T$ , we have

$$
\begin{array}{l} \frac {1}{T _ {0}} \sum_ {t = T _ {\mathrm {f}}} ^ {T _ {\mathrm {f}} + T _ {0} - 1} \left(\mathbf {R o b O b j} _ {t} (w ^ {(t)}) - \mathbf {O b j} _ {t} (w ^ {(T _ {\mathrm {f}})}) - O \left(\frac {1}{\log d}\right)\right) \\ \leq \frac {1}{2 \eta T _ {0}} \| w ^ {(T _ {\mathrm {f}})} - g \| _ {F} ^ {2} - \frac {1}{2 \eta T _ {0}} \| w ^ {(T _ {\mathrm {f}} + T _ {0})} - g \| _ {F} ^ {2} \leq \frac {1}{\eta T _ {0}} \cdot O \left(\frac {k ^ {2} \Xi_ {2} ^ {4}}{d} m\right) - \frac {1}{2 \eta T _ {0}} \| w ^ {(T _ {\mathrm {f}} + T _ {0})} - g \| _ {F} ^ {2} \tag {F.14} \\ \end{array}
$$

Inequality (F.14) now implies that

$$
\sum_ {i \in [ m ]} \| v _ {i} ^ {(T _ {\mathrm {f}} + T _ {0})} \| ^ {2} = \| w ^ {(T _ {\mathrm {f}} + T _ {0})} - g \| _ {F} ^ {2} \leq O \left(\frac {k ^ {2} \Xi_ {2} ^ {4}}{d} m\right)
$$

so (F.12) holds at iteration  $t = T_{\mathsf{f}} + T_0$ . We can then also apply Lemma F.15 which ensures (F.13) holds at iteration  $t = T_{\mathsf{f}} + T_0$ .

Finally, let us go back to (F.14) and choose  $T_0 = T = \Theta \left( \frac{k^2 \Xi_2^4 m \log d}{\eta d} \right)$ . It implies

$$
\frac {1}{T} \sum_ {t = T _ {\mathrm {f}}} ^ {T _ {\mathrm {f}} + T - 1} \left(\mathbf {R o b O b j} _ {t} (w ^ {(t)}) - \mathbf {O b j} _ {t} (w ^ {(T _ {\mathrm {f}})}) - O \left(\frac {1}{\log d}\right)\right) \leq O (\frac {1}{\log d})
$$

Note that our final choice of  $T$  also ensures that the pre-requisite  $T\eta \leq o(db)$  and  $\tau, \sigma_x \leq o\left(\frac{d^2b^2}{(T\eta)^2\sqrt{k}\log d}\right)$  of Lemma F.15 hold.

# F.4.2 Robust Convergence for  $\ell_{\infty}$  Perturbation

Proof of Theorem F.4. The proof is nearly identical to that of Theorem F.1. In particular, we want to inductively prove that at every iteration  $t \in [T_{\mathsf{f}}, T_{\mathsf{f}} + T]$ , it satisfies

$$
\sum_ {i \in [ m ]} \| v _ {i} ^ {(t)} \| _ {2} ^ {2} \leq r ^ {2} m \quad \text {f o r} \quad r = \Theta \left(\frac {k \Xi_ {2} ^ {2}}{\sqrt {d}}\right) \tag {F.15}
$$

$$
\max  _ {i \in [ m ]} \| v _ {i} ^ {(t)} \| _ {1} \leq r ^ {\prime} \quad \text {f o r} \quad r ^ {\prime} = \Theta (k \Xi_ {2} ^ {2} \cdot \| \mathbf {M} \| _ {\infty}) \tag {F.16}
$$

We also need to redo the following calculations:[25]

$$
\begin{array}{l} \mathbb {E} [ | \mathbf {R o b O b j} _ {t} ^ {\prime} (v ^ {(t)}) - \mathbf {R o b O b j} _ {t} (w ^ {(t)}) | ] \leq \mathbb {E} \left[ \left| g _ {t} (v ^ {(t)}; x + \delta , x, \rho) - f _ {t} (w ^ {(t)}, x + \delta) \right| \right] \\ \leq O (\tau^ {2}) \cdot k ^ {3. 5} d ^ {c _ {0}} \cdot \| \mathbf {M} \| _ {\infty} ^ {2} \quad \text {(u s i n g L e m m a F . 1 3)} \\ \leq O \left(\frac {1}{\log d}\right) \quad \text {(u s i n g} \tau \leq \frac {1}{k ^ {1 . 7 5} \cdot \| \mathbf {M} \| _ {\infty} \cdot d ^ {c _ {0}}}) \\ \end{array}
$$

$$
\begin{array}{l} \mathbb {E} [ | \mathbf {R o b O b j} _ {t} ^ {\prime} (0) - \mathbf {O b j} _ {t} (w ^ {(T _ {\mathrm {f}})}) | ] \leq \mathbb {E} [ | g _ {t} (0; x + \delta , x) - f _ {t} (w ^ {(T _ {\mathrm {f}})}, x) | ] + \lambda \left| \sum_ {i \in [ m ]} \mathbf {R e g} (w _ {i} ^ {(T _ {\mathrm {f}})}) - \mathbf {R e g} (g _ {i}) \right| \\ \leq O \left(\frac {k ^ {2 . 5}}{d ^ {1 - 2 c _ {0}}} + \tau \cdot k \Xi_ {2} ^ {4}\right) + O \left(\frac {k \Xi_ {2} ^ {4} \log d}{\sqrt {d}}\right) \quad \text {(u s i n g L e m m a F . 1 4 a n d C l a i m F . 1 7)} \\ \leq O \left(\frac {1}{\log d} + \tau \sqrt {k \Xi_ {2} ^ {7}}\right) \quad \text {(u s i n g} k ^ {2. 5} <   d ^ {1 - 2 c _ {0}} / \log d) \\ \leq O \left(\frac {1}{\log d}\right) \quad \mathrm {(u s i n g} \tau \leq \frac {1}{k ^ {1 . 7 5 .} \| \mathbf {M} \| _ {\infty} \cdot d ^ {c _ {0}}}) \\ \end{array}
$$

![](images/cc2f97eb76dfaedec97e5609f6e88d9e3aaaffcd971a5cc132ff7649af6e0394.jpg)

# F.5 Fast Gradient Method (FGM) Robust Training

Let us prove Corollary F.2 only for the  $\ell_2$  case, and the other  $\ell_{\infty}$  case Corollary F.5 is completely analogous.

Proof of Corollary F.2. At any iteration  $t \in [T_{\mathbf{f}}, T_{\mathbf{f}} + T_{\mathbf{g}}]$ , consider any perturbation vector  $\delta \in \mathbb{R}^d$  which may depend on  $x$  but not on  $\rho$ , with  $\| \delta \|_2 \leq \tau$ .

Recall from Lemma F.11 that

$$
\mathbb {E} _ {x} \left[ \big | \mathbb {E} _ {\rho} g _ {t} (v ^ {(t)}; x + \delta , x, \rho) - \mathbb {E} _ {\rho} f _ {t} (w ^ {(t)}; x + \delta , \rho) \big | \right] \leq O (\tau^ {2}) \cdot \left(\frac {\Xi_ {2} ^ {5}}{\sigma_ {\rho}} + \frac {k ^ {3 . 5}}{d ^ {1 - 2 c _ {0}}}\right) \leq O \left(\frac {1}{\log^ {2} d}\right)
$$

This means for at least  $1 - O\left(\frac{1}{\log d}\right)$  probability mass of inputs  $x$ , we have

$$
\left| \underset {\rho} {\mathbb {E}} g _ {t} (v ^ {(t)}; x + \delta , x, \rho) - \underset {\rho} {\mathbb {E}} f _ {t} (w ^ {(t)}; x + \delta , \rho) \right| \leq O \left(\frac {1}{\log d}\right)
$$

For those choices of  $x$ , using the fact that  $g_{t}$  is linear in  $\delta$ , we also have

$$
\begin{array}{l} \underset {\rho} {\mathbb {E}} g _ {t} (v ^ {(t)}; x + \delta , x, \rho) - \underset {\rho} {\mathbb {E}} f _ {t} (w ^ {(t)}; x, \rho) \\ = \underset {\rho} {\mathbb {E}} g _ {t} (v ^ {(t)}; x + \delta , x, \rho) - \underset {\rho} {\mathbb {E}} g _ {t} (v ^ {(t)}; x, x, \rho) = \langle \nabla_ {x} \underset {\rho} {\mathbb {E}} g _ {t} (v ^ {(t)}; x, x, \rho), \delta \rangle \\ = \langle \nabla_ {x} \underset {\rho} {\mathbb {E}} f _ {t} (w ^ {(t)}; x, \rho), \delta \rangle \\ \end{array}
$$

Putting them together we have

$$
\begin{array}{l} \left[ - y (x) \underset {\rho} {\mathbb {E}} f _ {t} \left(w ^ {(t)}; x + \delta , \rho\right) \right] = \left[ - y (x) \underset {\rho} {\mathbb {E}} f _ {t} \left(w ^ {(t)}; x, \rho\right) - \langle y (x) \nabla_ {x} \underset {\rho} {\mathbb {E}} f _ {t} \left(w ^ {(t)}; x, \rho\right), \delta \rangle \right] \pm O \left(\frac {1}{\log d}\right) (F.17) \\ \leq \left[ - y (x) \underset {\rho} {\mathbb {E}} f _ {t} \left(w ^ {(t)}; x, \rho\right) - \langle y (x) \nabla_ {x} \underset {\rho} {\mathbb {E}} f _ {t} \left(w ^ {(t)}; x, \rho\right), \delta^ {\star} \rangle \right] + O \left(\frac {1}{\log d}\right) (F.18) \\ \end{array}
$$

where  $\delta^{\star} = A(f_t,x,y)$  is the perturbation obtained by the fast gradient method with  $\ell_2$  radius  $\tau$ . This means two things.

On the other hand, by applying Markov's inequality and Jensen's inequality to  $\mathbb{E}_{x,y = y(x),\rho}\left[\mathbf{Obj}_t(w^{(t)};x + \delta^\star ,y,\rho)\right]\leq o(1)$ , we know for at least  $1 - o(1)$  probability mass of the choices of  $x$ , it satisfies

$$
\begin{array}{l} \log (1 + e ^ {- y (x) \mathbb {E} _ {\rho} f _ {t} (w ^ {(t)}; x + \delta^ {\star}, \rho)}) \leq o (1) \\ \Rightarrow - y (x) \underset {\rho} {\mathbb {E}} f _ {t} \left(w ^ {(t)}; x + \delta^ {\star}, \rho\right) \leq - 1 0 \\ \end{array}
$$

Therefore, for all of those  $x$  (with total mass  $\geq 1 - o(1)$ ) satisfying both, we can first apply (F.17) (with  $\delta = \delta^{\star}$ ) to derive

$$
- y (x) \underset {\rho} {\mathbb {E}} f _ {t} (w ^ {(t)}; x, \rho) - \langle y (x) \nabla_ {x} \underset {\rho} {\mathbb {E}} f _ {t} (w ^ {(t)}; x, \rho), \delta^ {\star} \rangle \leq - 9
$$

Applying (F.18) then we obtain (for any  $\delta$ )

$$
- y (x) \underset {\rho} {\mathbb {E}} f _ {t} (w ^ {(t)}; x + \delta , \rho) \leq - 8
$$

This means, the output of the network  $f_{t}$  is robust at point  $x$  against any perturbation  $\delta$  with radius  $\tau$ . We finish the proof of Corollary F.2.

# G NTK Lower Bound For  $\ell_{\infty}$  Perturbation

Recall from Definition 5.6 that the feature mapping of the neural tangent kernel for our two-layer network  $f$  is

$$
\Phi (x) = \left(x \underset {\rho_ {i}} {\mathbb {E}} \left(\mathbb {1} _ {\langle w _ {i}, x \rangle + \rho_ {i} \geq b _ {i}} - \mathbb {1} _ {- \langle w _ {i}, x \rangle + \rho_ {i} \geq b _ {i}}\right)\right) _ {i = 1} ^ {m}
$$

Therefore, given weights  $\{v_{i}\}_{i\in [m]}$ , the NTK function  $p(x)$  is given as

$$
p (x) = \sum_ {i \in [ m ]} \langle x, v _ {i} \rangle \underset {\rho_ {i} \sim \mathcal {N} (0, \sigma_ {\rho_ {i}} ^ {2})} {\mathbb {E}} \left(\mathbb {1} _ {\langle w _ {i}, x \rangle + \rho_ {i} \geq b _ {i}} - \mathbb {1} _ {- \langle w _ {i}, x \rangle + \rho_ {i} \geq b _ {i}}\right)
$$

To make our lower bound stronger, in this section, we consider the simplest input distribution with  $\mathbf{M} = \mathbf{I}$  and  $\sigma_{x} = 0$  (so  $\xi \equiv 0$ ). Our main theorem is the following.

Theorem G.1. Suppose  $w_{1},\ldots ,w_{m}\in \mathbb{R}^{d}$  are i.i.d. sampled from  $\mathcal{N}(0,\mathbf{I})$  with  $m\leq d^C$  for some constant  $C > 1$ ; and suppose  $\rho_{i}\sim \mathcal{N}(0,\sigma_{\rho_{i}}^{2})$  with  $|\sigma_{\rho_i}|\leq d^{o(1)}$  and  $|b_{i}|\leq d^{o(1)}$ . Then, there exists constant  $c_{6} > 0$  so that, with probability at least  $1 - e^{-\Omega (\log^2 d)}$ , choosing  $\tau = \frac{1}{d^{c_6}}$ , then for any  $k\in \left[d^{\frac{c_6}{100}},d^{0.5 - \frac{c_6}{100}}\right]$  and sufficiently large  $d$ .

$$
\Pr_ {x, y = y (x)} \left[ \exists \delta \in \mathbb {R} ^ {d}, \| \delta \| _ {\infty} \leq \tau : \operatorname {s i g n} (p (x + \delta)) \neq y \right] \geq \frac {1 - o (1)}{2}.
$$

# G.1 Proof of Theorem G.1

We first note the following:

Claim G.2. Suppose at point  $z \in \mathbb{R}^d$ , some function  $p(z)$  gives the correct label  $y(z) = \mathrm{sign}(\langle w^\star, z \rangle)$  against any  $\ell_{\infty}$  perturbation of radius  $\tau$ . Then, letting  $\zeta \sim \mathcal{N}(0, \frac{\tau^2}{\log^8 d} \mathbf{I}_{d \times d})$  be a random vector, and  $\delta \in \mathbb{R}^d$  be any vector with  $\| \delta \|_{\infty} \leq \tau / 2$ , it satisfies

$$
\underset {\zeta} {\mathbb {E}} \left[ p (z + \delta + \zeta) \right] \cdot \operatorname {s i g n} (\langle w ^ {\star}, z \rangle) \geq - e ^ {- \Omega (\log^ {2} d)} \max  _ {i \in [ m ]} \{\| v _ {i} \| _ {2} \} _ {i \in [ m ]}
$$

Proof of Claim G.2. With probability at least  $1 - e^{-\Omega (\log^2 d)}$  it satisfies  $\| \zeta +\delta \|_{\infty}\leq \tau$ . When this happens, we must have  $p(z + \delta +\zeta)\cdot \mathsf{sign}(\langle w^{\star},z\rangle)\geq 0$ .

Therefore, for the analysis purpose (by sacrificing  $\ell_{\infty}$  norm radius from  $\tau$  to  $\tau /2$ ), we can imagine as if the input is randomly perturbed by  $\zeta$ . This serves for the purpose of smoothing the NTK function  $p(\cdot)$ , which originally has indicator functions in it so may be trickier to analyze.

Next, we define

$$
M _ {W} (x) \stackrel {\text {d e f}} {=} \max  _ {i \in [ m ]} | \langle w _ {i}, x \rangle |.
$$

One can carefully apply the Taylor expansion of the smoothed indicator function (using the randomness of  $\zeta$ ), to derive the following claim. (Detailed proof in Section G.4.)

Claim G.3. Consider any NTK function  $p(x)$  with parameters  $\| w_i\|_2 \geq \frac{\sqrt{d}}{2}$ ,  $\| w_i\|_{\infty} \leq \log^2 d$ ,  $\rho_i \sim \mathcal{N}(0, \sigma_{\rho_i}^2)$  with  $|\sigma_{\rho_i}| \leq d^{o(1)}$  and  $|b_i| \leq d^{o(1)}$ . Suppose  $\tau \in [\frac{1}{d^{1/5}}, 1]$ , then there exists coefficients  $\{c_{i,r}, c_{i,r}', c_i''\}_{i \in [m], r \geq 0}$  with

- each  $|c_{i,r}|, |c_{i,r}'| \leq O(1)$ ,  
- each  $|c_{i,r}'| \leq |c_{i,r}| \cdot O(d^{-0.1}r)$ ,  
- each  $|c_{i,r}| \geq \Omega \left( \frac{1}{d^2} \right)$  for every odd constant  $r \geq 1$ .  
- each  $|c_i''| \leq O(d^{-1/4})$ .

so that, for every  $z$  with  $\| z\| _1\leq d^{1 / 4}$  and every  $\delta$  with  $\| \delta \|_{\infty}\leq \tau /2$  and  $M_W(\delta)\leq \tau d^{1 / 4}$ , we have:

$$
\begin{array}{l} \underset {\zeta \sim \mathcal {N} (0, \frac {\tau_ {2}}{\log^ {8} d} \mathbf {I} _ {d \times d})} {\mathbb {E}} [ p (z + \delta + \zeta) ] \\ = \sum_ {r \geq 0} \| v _ {i} \| _ {2} \left(c _ {i} ^ {\prime \prime} + \sum_ {i \in [ m ]} \left(c _ {i, r} \langle \frac {v _ {i}}{\| v _ {i} \| _ {2}}, z + \delta \rangle + c _ {i, r} ^ {\prime} \langle \frac {w _ {i}}{\| w _ {i} \| _ {2}}, z + \delta \rangle\right) \left(\frac {\langle w _ {i} , z + \delta \rangle}{\tau \| w _ {i} \| _ {2}}\right) ^ {r}\right) \\ \end{array}
$$

Using the above formula, we can write

$$
\begin{array}{l} \underset {\zeta} {\mathbb {E}} [ p (x + \zeta) ] = C O N S T + \sum_ {r \geq 0} T _ {r + 1} (x ^ {\otimes r + 1}) \\ \mathrm {f o r} T _ {r + 1} (x ^ {\otimes r + 1}) \stackrel {\mathrm {d e f}} {=} \sum_ {i \in [ m ]} \| v _ {i} \| _ {2} \left\langle c _ {i, r} \frac {v _ {i}}{\| v _ {i} \| _ {2}} + c _ {i, r} ^ {\prime} \frac {w _ {i}}{\| w _ {i} \| _ {2}}, x \right\rangle \left(\frac {\langle w _ {i} , x \rangle}{\tau \| w _ {i} \| _ {2}}\right) ^ {r} \\ \end{array}
$$

Using  $|c_{i,r}| \geq \Omega \left(\frac{1}{d^2}\right)$  for odd constant  $r \geq 1$ , and  $|c_{i,r}'| \leq O(d^{-0.1}) \cdot |c_{i,r}|$ , by applying Lemma G.4, $^{26}$  we know that when  $r = 3C + 3$  (say wlog.  $3C + 3$  is odd),

$$
\left\| T _ {3 C + 4} \right\| _ {F} = \Omega \left(\frac {1}{d ^ {3}} \max  _ {i \in [ m ]} \left\{\left\| v _ {i} \right\| _ {2} \right\}\right) \tag {G.1}
$$

Also, for a parameter  $q = \sqrt{d}$ , let us apply Lemma G.5 to derive

$$
\lambda_ {r} \stackrel {\text {d e f}} {=} \max  _ {\delta \in \mathbb {R} ^ {d}: \| \delta \| _ {\infty} \leq \tau , M _ {W} (\delta) \leq \tau \sqrt {q}} \left| T _ {r} \left(\delta^ {\otimes r}\right) \right| \geq \Omega \left(\frac {1}{\left(\tau\right) ^ {r}} \| T _ {r} \| _ {F}\right) \tag {G.2}
$$

Let  $R \geq 3C + 3$  be a constant to be chosen later,  $\lambda_{\max} = \max_{r < R}\{\lambda_r\}$ , and let  $\delta_{\max}$  be the choice of  $\delta$  which maximizes the value of  $\lambda_{\max}$ .

Consider the high probability event that  $M_W(z) = \tilde{O}(1)$ , then using  $M_W(\delta_{\max}) \leq \tau \sqrt{q}$ , we have

$$
\begin{array}{l} \left| \sum_ {r \geq R} T _ {r + 1} \left(\left(z + \delta_ {\max }\right) ^ {\otimes r + 1}\right) \right| = \left| \sum_ {r \geq R} \sum_ {i \in [ m ]} c _ {i, r} \left\langle v _ {i}, z + \delta_ {\max } \right\rangle \left(\frac {\left\langle w _ {i} , z + \delta_ {\max } \right\rangle}{\tau \| w _ {i} \| _ {2}}\right) ^ {r} \right| \\ \leq d ^ {4} m \max  _ {i \in [ m ]} \left\{\| v _ {i} \| _ {2} \right\} _ {i \in [ m ]} \sum_ {r \geq R} \left(\frac {\tilde {O} (1) + \tau \sqrt {q}}{\tau \sqrt {d}}\right) ^ {r} \\ \leq d ^ {5} m \max  _ {i \in [ m ]} \left\{\| v _ {i} \| _ {2} \right\} _ {i \in [ m ]} \sum_ {r \geq R} \left(\frac {O (1)}{d ^ {1 / 4}}\right) ^ {r} \\ \end{array}
$$

When  $R\geq 10000(C + 1)$  , we have

$$
\left| \sum_ {r \geq R} T _ {r + 1} \left(\left(z + \delta_ {\max }\right) ^ {\otimes r + 1}\right) \right| \leq O \left(\frac {\operatorname* {m a x} _ {i \in [ m ]} \left\{\left\| v _ {i} \right\| _ {2} \right\} _ {i \in [ m ]}}{d ^ {1 0 0 C}}\right) \tag {G.3}
$$

Next, for every  $s \in [1/2, 1]$ , let us define

$$
q _ {<   R} (z, s) := C O N S T + \sum_ {r <   R} T _ {r + 1} ((z + s \delta_ {\max }) ^ {\otimes r + 1})
$$

- On one hand, by applying Lemma G.5 twice for each  $r$ , we know for every set of vectors  $z_1, \dots, z_q$  with  $\| z_i \|_{\infty} \leq 1$ ,  $M_W(z_i) = \widetilde{O}(1)$  and  $\operatorname{supp}(z_i) \cap \operatorname{supp}(z_j) = \emptyset$  for  $i \neq j$ , it satisfies

$$
\sum_ {j \in [ q ]} (| q _ {<   R} (z _ {j}, s) - q _ {<   R} (0, s) | + | q _ {<   R} (- z _ {j}, s) - q _ {<   R} (0, s) |) \leq \sum_ {r <   R} \widetilde {O} \left(\frac {\lambda_ {\max}}{\tau^ {r}}\right) \leq \widetilde {O} \left(\frac {\lambda_ {\max}}{\tau^ {R}}\right)
$$

This means by Markov's inequality, for at least  $\left(1 - \frac{1}{\log d}\right)$  fraction of the indices  $j \in [q]$ , denoting them by  $\Lambda \subseteq [q]$ , it satisfies

$$
| q _ {<   R} (z _ {j}, s) - q _ {<   R} (0, s) | + | q _ {<   R} (- z _ {j}, s) - q _ {<   R} (0, s) | \leq \frac {1}{q} \widetilde {O} \left(\frac {\lambda_ {\max}}{\tau^ {R}}\right)
$$

- On the other hand, by Claim G.7, we know that there is an  $s \in [1/2, 1]$  such that

$$
| q _ {<   R} (0, s) | \geq \Omega (\lambda_ {\max })
$$

Without loss of generality, suppose  $q_{<R}(0,s)$  is positive and  $q_{<R}(0,s) \geq \Omega (\lambda_{\max})$ .

Combining the two, when  $\tau^{100000(C + 1)}\geq \frac{1}{d}$ , we derive that for those  $j\in \Lambda$

$$
q _ {<   R} (z _ {j}, s) \geq \Omega (\lambda_ {\max }) \quad \text {a n d} \quad q _ {<   R} (- z _ {j}, s) \geq \Omega (\lambda_ {\max })
$$

Thus, combining with (G.1), (G.2) and (G.3), we have for those  $j \in \Lambda$ ,

$$
\mathbb {E} _ {\zeta} [ p (z _ {j} + s \delta_ {\max } + \zeta) ] = C O N S T + \sum_ {r \geq 0} T _ {r + 1} ((z _ {j} + s \delta_ {\max }) ^ {\otimes r + 1}) \quad \geq \Omega \left(\frac {1}{d ^ {4}} \max  _ {i \in [ m ]} \{\| v _ {i} \| _ {2} \}\right)
$$

$$
\mathbb {E} _ {\zeta} [ p (- z _ {j} + s \delta_ {\max } + \zeta) ] = C O N S T + \sum_ {r \geq 0} T _ {r + 1} ((- z _ {j} + s \delta_ {\max }) ^ {\otimes r + 1}) \quad \geq \Omega \left(\frac {1}{d ^ {4}} \max  _ {i \in [ m ]} \{\| v _ {i} \| _ {2} \}\right)
$$

but according to Claim G.2 (see end of this section), this means the NTK function  $p(\cdot)$  outputs the wrong label either  $z_{j}$  or for  $-z_{j}$ . Therefore, among those  $2q$  data points  $z_{1},\ldots ,z_{q}, - z_{1},\ldots , - z_{q}$ , at least  $50\% \cdot (1 - o(1))$  of them must be wrong under  $\ell_2$  perturbation with radius  $\tau$ .

Finally, recall when  $z$  is generated from the data distribution, with high probability  $z \in \mathbb{R}^d$  is  $O(k)$ -sparse. Therefore, we can divide (nearly) all possible choices of  $z$  into  $q = \sqrt{d} \ll O(d / k)$  groups, in a way that when we generate  $z_1, \ldots, z_q$  from those groups, they have disjoint support and they together match the overall distribution. Using this argument one can prove that, for at least  $50\% \cdot (1 - o(1))$  of the probability mass of  $z$  from the data distribution, the prediction must be wrong under  $\ell_2$  perturbation with radius  $\tau$ .

This finishes the proof of Theorem G.1.

# G.2 Tensor Lower Bound

Next, for each degree- $r$  homogenous part of the polynomial expansion of Claim G.3, we can write it as a tensor and lower bound its Frobenius norm as follows.

Lemma G.4. Suppose  $w_{1},\ldots ,w_{m}\in \mathbb{R}^{d}$  are i.i.d. sampled from  $\mathcal{N}(0,\mathbf{I})$  with  $m\leq d^C$  for some constant  $C > 0$ . Let  $v_{1},\dots,v_{m}\in \mathbb{R}^{d}$  be arbitrary vectors that can depend on the randomness of  $w_{1},\ldots ,w_{m}$ . Let us denote by  $T_{r + 1}$  the symmetric tensor  $\mathbb{R}^{d\times (r + 1)}\to \mathbb{R}$  such that

$$
T _ {r + 1} (x ^ {\otimes r + 1}) = \sum_ {i \in [ m ]} \langle v _ {i}, x \rangle \left(\frac {\langle w _ {i} , x \rangle}{\| w _ {i} \| _ {2}}\right) ^ {r}
$$

We have as long as  $r \geq 3C$ , then w.p.  $\geq 1 - e^{-\Omega (\log^2 d)}$  over the randomness of  $\{w_i\}_{i \in [m]}$ , for every  $\{v_i\}_{i \in [m]}$  we have

$$
\left\| T _ {r + 1} \right\| _ {F} \geq \Omega \left(\frac {1}{\sqrt {d}} \max  _ {i \in [ m ]} \left\{\left\| v _ {i} \right\| _ {2} \right\}\right)
$$

Proof of Lemma G.4. Consider any fixed  $j \in [m]$ , and some  $\gamma \in [-1, 1]$  to be chosen later.

Let us define  $x = \frac{w_j}{2\|w_j\|_2} + \gamma \frac{v_j}{2\|v_j\|_2}$  which satisfies  $\|x\|_2 \leq 1$ . We have

$$
\begin{array}{l} T _ {r + 1} \left(x ^ {\otimes r + 1}\right) = \sum_ {i \in [ m ]} \left\langle v _ {i}, x \right\rangle \left(\frac {\left\langle w _ {i} , x \right\rangle}{\left\| w _ {i} \right\| _ {2}}\right) ^ {r} \\ = \sum_ {i \in [ m ] \backslash \{j \}} \left\langle v _ {i}, x \right\rangle \left(\frac {\left\langle w _ {i} , x \right\rangle}{\| w _ {i} \| _ {2}}\right) ^ {r} + \left(\frac {\gamma}{2} \| v _ {j} \| _ {2} + \frac {\left\langle v _ {j} , w _ {j} \right\rangle}{2 \| w _ {j} \| _ {2}}\right) \left(\frac {1}{2} + \gamma \frac {\left\langle w _ {j} , v _ {j} \right\rangle}{2 \| v _ {j} \| _ {2} \| w _ {j} \| _ {2}}\right) ^ {r} \\ \end{array}
$$

Note that for every  $j \neq i$ , with probability at least  $1 - e^{-\Omega (\log^2 d)}$

$$
\left| \frac {\langle w _ {i} , x \rangle}{\| w _ {i} \| _ {2}} \right| = \left| \frac {\langle w _ {i} , w _ {j} \rangle}{2 \| w _ {i} \| _ {2} \| w _ {j} \| _ {2}} + \gamma \frac {\langle w _ {i} , v _ {j} \rangle}{2 \| v _ {j} \| _ {2} \| w _ {i} \| _ {2}} \right| \leq O \left(\frac {\log d}{\sqrt {d}} + \gamma\right) .
$$

This implies that as long as  $|\gamma |\leq \frac{1}{\sqrt{d}}$

$$
| T _ {r + 1} (x ^ {\otimes r + 1}) | \geq \frac {1}{3 ^ {r}} \left| \frac {\gamma}{2} \| v _ {j} \| _ {2} + \frac {\langle v _ {j} , w _ {j} \rangle}{2 \| w _ {j} \| _ {2}} \right| - \left(\frac {O (\log d)}{\sqrt {d}}\right) ^ {r} m \cdot \max _ {i \in [ m ]} \{\| v _ {i} \| _ {2} \}
$$

Since the above lower bound holds for every  $|\gamma| \leq \frac{1}{\sqrt{d}}$  and every  $j \in [d]$ , we immediately know

$$
\max  _ {\| x \| _ {2} \leq 1} \left\{T _ {r + 1} \left(x ^ {\otimes r + 1}\right) \right\} \geq \Omega \left(\frac {1}{\sqrt {d}} \max  _ {i \in [ m ]} \left\{\| v _ {i} \| _ {2} \right\}\right)
$$

This implies our bound on the Frobenius norm as well.

![](images/71aeb2055ed859bb0b8d135ab51adfed0480eefb9d25ba4c569632bae444178a.jpg)

# G.3 Tensor Perturbation

We present the following critical lemma, which serves as the major step to prove the non-robustness of Neural Tangent Kernel:

Lemma G.5 (Tensor difference). For every  $m = \mathrm{poly}(d)$ , every set of vectors  $W = \{w_{i}\}_{i\in [m]}$  with each  $\| w_{i}\|_{2} = O(\sqrt{d})$ , for every constant  $r > 0$ , every  $q\in [0,d]$ , every symmetric tensor  $T$  of degree  $r\colon \mathbb{R}^{d\times r}\to \mathbb{R}$ , for every  $\tau >0$

1. The following is true

$$
\lambda := \max  _ {\delta \in \mathbb {R} ^ {d}: \| \delta \| _ {\infty} \leq \tau , M _ {W} (\delta) \leq \tau \sqrt {q}} \left| T (\delta^ {\otimes r}) \right| \geq \Omega \left(\frac {1}{(\tau) ^ {r}} \| T \| _ {F}\right)
$$

2. For every vectors  $z_{1},\dots ,z_{q}\in \mathbb{R}^{d}$  with  $\| z_i\|_{\infty}\leq 1,M_W(z_i) = \widetilde{O} (1)$  and  $\operatorname {supp}(z_i)\cap \operatorname {supp}(z_j) = \varnothing$  for  $i\neq j$  , for every  $y$  such that  $\| y\|_{\infty}\leq \tau$  and  $M_W(y)\leq \tau \sqrt{q}$  , the following holds:

$$
\sum_ {i \in [ q ]} \left| T (y ^ {\otimes r}) - T ((y + z _ {i}) ^ {\otimes r}) \right| \leq \widetilde {O} \left(\frac {\lambda}{\tau^ {r}}\right)
$$

Proof of Lemma G.5. For the first item, we can simply let  $\delta \sim \mathcal{N}\left(0,\frac{\tau^2}{\log^2d}\right)$ . This choice of  $\delta$  satisfies  $\| \delta \|_{\infty} \leq \tau$  and  $M_W(\delta) \leq \tau \sqrt{q}$  with high probability. Furthermore, by applying anticoncentration of Gaussian polynomials (see for instance [5, Lemma I.1]), we know with at least constant probability  $|T(\delta^{\otimes r})| \geq \frac{\Omega(\|T\|_F)}{\tau^r}$ . This proves the first item.

To see the second item, we first note by tensor  $r$ -linearity and symmetry,

$$
\sum_ {i \in [ q ]} \left| T (y ^ {\otimes r}) - T ((y + z _ {i}) ^ {\otimes r}) \right| \leq \sum_ {r ^ {\prime} = 1} ^ {r} \binom {r} {r ^ {\prime}} \sum_ {j \in [ q ]} | T (z _ {j} ^ {\otimes r ^ {\prime}}, y ^ {\otimes (r - r ^ {\prime})}) |
$$

and therefore we only need to bound the terms on the right hand side for any fixed  $r' \in [r]$ .

Define random variable  $\{\xi_{i,j}\}_{i\in [r' - 1],j\in [q]}$  where each  $\xi_{i,j}$  is i.i.d. uniformly at random chosen from  $\{-\tau ,\tau \}$ . Consider arbitrary fixed values  $\gamma_j\in \{-1,1\}$  for  $j\in [q]$ . Let us define random variables  $Z_{1},Z_{2},\dots ,Z_{r^{\prime}}\in \mathbb{R}^{d}$  as:

$$
\forall i \in [ r ^ {\prime} - 1 ]: Z _ {i} := \sum_ {j \in [ q ]} \xi_ {i, j} z _ {j}, \qquad Z _ {r ^ {\prime}} := \sum_ {j \in [ q ]} \gamma_ {j} \Big (\prod_ {i \in [ r ^ {\prime} - 1 ]} \xi_ {i, j} \Big) z _ {j}
$$

From these notions one can directly calculate that

$$
\underset {\xi} {\mathbb {E}} [ T (Z _ {1}, Z _ {2}, \dots , Z _ {r ^ {\prime}}, y ^ {\otimes (r - r ^ {\prime})}) ] = \tau^ {r ^ {\prime}} \sum_ {j \in [ q ]} \gamma_ {j} T \left(z _ {j} ^ {\otimes r ^ {\prime}}, y ^ {\otimes (r - r ^ {\prime})}\right)
$$

On the other hand, we have  $\| Z_i\|_{\infty} \leq \tau$  and moreover, using the randomness of  $\xi_{i,j}$ , we know w.h.p.  $|M_W(Z_i)| = \widetilde{O} (\tau \sqrt{q})$  for every  $i \in [q]$ . Hence, by Claim G.8, we know that

$$
| \mathbb {E} [ T (Z _ {1}, Z _ {2}, \dots , Z _ {r ^ {\prime}}, y ^ {\otimes (r - r ^ {\prime})}) ] | = \widetilde {O} (\lambda)
$$

Putting them together, we have  $\left|\sum_{i\in [q]}\gamma_iT(z_i^{\otimes r'},y^{\otimes (r - r')})\right| = \widetilde{O}\left(\frac{\lambda}{\tau^{r'}}\right)$ , and since this holds for every  $\gamma_i\in \{-1,1\}$ , we conclude that:

$$
\sum_ {i \in [ q ]} | T (z _ {i} ^ {\otimes r ^ {\prime}}, y ^ {\otimes (r - r ^ {\prime})}) | = \widetilde {O} \left(\frac {\lambda}{\tau^ {r ^ {\prime}}}\right)
$$

Putting this back to the binomial expansion finishes the proof.

![](images/4273e8c2ef09a6aba587232b38847cdbb75461075505880cc3edeec74848b353.jpg)

# G.4 Smoothed ReLU Taylor Series: Proof of Claim G.3

We first note the following Taylor expansion formula for smoothed ReLU.

Claim G.6 (smoothed ReLU). Let  $a \geq 0$  be any real and  $\rho \sim \mathcal{N}(0, \sigma^2)$  for  $\sigma \geq a$ . Then, for every  $x \in [-a, a]$ ,

$$
\mathbb {E} _ {\rho} \left[ \rho \mathbb {1} _ {\rho + x \geq 0} \right] = \sigma \sum_ {i = 0} ^ {\infty} c _ {2 i} \left(\frac {x}{\sigma}\right) ^ {2 i} \quad a n d \quad \mathbb {E} _ {\rho} \left[ \mathbb {1} _ {\rho + x \geq 0} \right] = \frac {1}{2} + \sum_ {i = 0} ^ {\infty} c _ {2 i + 1} ^ {\prime} \left(\frac {x}{\sigma}\right) ^ {2 i + 1}
$$

where  $|c_{2i}| = \Theta \left(\frac{1}{i!}\right), |c_{2i+1}'| = \Theta \left(\frac{1}{(i+1)!}\right)$ .

Proof of Claim G.6. We can directly calculate that

$$
\mathbb {E} \left[ \rho \mathbb {1} _ {\rho + x \geq 0} \right] = \frac {1}{\sqrt {2 \pi} \sigma} \int_ {\rho \geq - x} \rho e ^ {- \frac {\rho^ {2}}{2 \sigma^ {2}}} d \rho = \frac {1}{\sqrt {2 \pi}} e ^ {- \frac {x ^ {2}}{2 \sigma^ {2}}} \sigma
$$

so using Taylor expansion of  $e^{-\frac{x^2}{2\sigma^2}}$  we prove the first equation. As for the second equation, we have

$$
\mathbb {E} \left[ \mathbb {1} _ {\rho + x \geq 0} \right] = \frac {1}{\sqrt {2 \pi} \sigma} \int_ {\rho \geq - x} e ^ {- \frac {\rho^ {2}}{2 \sigma^ {2}}} d \rho
$$

This implies that

$$
\frac {d}{d x} \mathbb {E} \left[ \mathbb {1} _ {\rho + x \geq 0} \right] = - \frac {1}{\sqrt {2 \pi} \sigma} e ^ {- \frac {x ^ {2}}{2 \sigma^ {2}}}
$$

Using Taylor expansion and integrating once, we prove the second equation.

![](images/1ae9d018c25c3805fcd57f6507643a2d8ae3a9d121b2555167ff57c1b5f3cca0.jpg)

We are now ready to prove Claim G.3.

Proof of Claim G.3. Specifically, for each  $i \in [m]$ , denoting by  $x = z + \delta$ , we wish to apply Claim G.6 to

$$
\begin{array}{l} \mathop{\mathbb{E}}_{\rho_{i},\zeta}\langle x + \zeta ,v_{i}\rangle \left(\mathbb{1}_{\langle w_{i},x + \zeta \rangle +\rho_{i}\geq b_{i}} - \mathbb{1}_{-\langle w_{i},x + \zeta \rangle +\rho_{i}\geq b_{i}}\right) \\ = \underbrace {\mathbb {E} _ {\rho_ {i} , \zeta} \langle x , v _ {i} \rangle \left(\mathbb {1} _ {\langle w _ {i} , x + \zeta \rangle + \rho_ {i} \geq b _ {i}} - \mathbb {1} _ {- \langle w _ {i} , x + \zeta \rangle + \rho_ {i} \geq b _ {i}}\right)} _ {\diamondsuit} + \underbrace {\mathbb {E} _ {\rho_ {i} , \zeta} \langle \zeta , v _ {i} \rangle \left(\mathbb {1} _ {\langle w _ {i} , x + \zeta \rangle + \rho_ {i} \geq b _ {i}} - \mathbb {1} _ {- \langle w _ {i} , x + \zeta \rangle + \rho_ {i} \geq b _ {i}}\right)} _ {\diamondsuit} \\ \end{array}
$$

Note that  $g \stackrel{\mathrm{def}}{=} \langle w_i, \zeta \rangle + \rho_i \sim \mathcal{N}(0, \sigma^2)$  for  $\sigma^2 = \frac{\tau^2}{\log^{16}d} \|w_i\|_2^2 + \sigma_{\rho_i}^2 \in \left[\frac{\tau^2}{\log^{16}d} \|w_i\|_2^2, \frac{2\tau^2}{\log^{16}d} \|w_i\|_2^2\right]$ .

- We first deal with the  $\heartsuit$  part. Using Claim G.6, we have

$$
\underset {\rho_ {i, \zeta}} {\mathbb {E}} \langle x, v _ {i} \rangle \mathbb {1} _ {\langle w _ {i}, x + \zeta \rangle + \rho_ {i} \geq b _ {i}} = \langle x, v _ {i} \rangle \underset {g} {\mathbb {E}} \mathbb {1} _ {\langle w _ {i}, x \rangle - b _ {i} + g \geq 0} = \langle x, v _ {i} \rangle \left(\frac {1}{2} + \sum_ {r = 0} ^ {\infty} c _ {2 r + 1} ^ {\prime} \left(\frac {\langle w _ {i} , x \rangle - b _ {i}}{\sigma}\right) ^ {2 r + 1}\right)
$$

for  $|c_{2r + 1}'| = \Theta \left(\frac{1}{(r + 1)!}\right)$ . Similarly, we also have

$$
- \underset {\rho_ {i}, \zeta} {\mathbb {E}} \langle x, v _ {i} \rangle \mathbb {1} _ {- \langle w _ {i}, x + \zeta \rangle + \rho_ {i} \geq b _ {i}} = \langle x, v _ {i} \rangle \left(- \frac {1}{2} - \sum_ {r = 0} ^ {\infty} c _ {2 r + 1} ^ {\prime} \left(\frac {- \langle w _ {i} , x \rangle - b _ {i}}{\sigma}\right) ^ {2 r + 1}\right)
$$

Putting them together, and using the fact that  $b_{i} \ll d^{-0.2} \ll \sigma$ , we can write

$$
\begin{array}{l} \begin{array}{l} \heartsuit = \underset {\rho_ {i}, \zeta} {\mathbb {E}} \left\langle x, v _ {i} \right\rangle \left[ \mathbb {1} _ {\left\langle w _ {i}, x + \zeta \right\rangle + \rho_ {i} \geq b _ {i}} - \mathbb {1} _ {- \left\langle w _ {i}, x + \zeta \right\rangle + \rho_ {i} \geq b _ {i}} \right] \end{array} \\ = \left\langle x, v _ {i} \right\rangle \left(\sum_ {r = 0} ^ {\infty} c _ {2 r + 1} ^ {\prime} \left(\frac {\left\langle w _ {i} , x \right\rangle - b _ {i}}{\sigma}\right) ^ {2 r + 1} - c _ {2 r + 1} ^ {\prime} \left(\frac {- \left\langle w _ {i} , x \right\rangle - b _ {i}}{\sigma}\right) ^ {2 r + 1}\right) \\ = \langle x, v _ {i} \rangle \sum_ {r \geq 0} c _ {r} ^ {\prime \prime} \left(\frac {\langle w _ {i} , x \rangle}{\sigma}\right) ^ {r} \\ \end{array}
$$

for  $|c_{2r}^{\prime \prime}| \leq O\left(\frac{1}{(r)!}\right)$  for every  $r \geq 0$  and  $|c_{2r + 1}^{\prime \prime}| \geq \Omega \left(\frac{1}{(r + 1)!}\right)$ .

- Let us now focus on the  $\diamond$  part. Let  $v_{i}^{\parallel}$  be the part of  $v_{i}$  that is parallel to  $w_{i}$ . Then obviously we have

$$
\begin{array}{l} \underset {\zeta , \rho_ {i}} {\mathbb {E}} \langle \zeta , v _ {i} \rangle \mathbb {1} _ {\langle w _ {i}, x + \zeta \rangle + \rho_ {i} \geq b _ {i}} = \underset {\zeta , \rho_ {i}} {\mathbb {E}} \langle \zeta , v _ {i} ^ {\parallel} \rangle \mathbb {1} _ {\langle w _ {i}, x + \zeta \rangle + \rho_ {i} \geq b _ {i}} = \frac {\| v _ {i} ^ {\parallel} \| _ {2}}{\| w _ {i} \| _ {2}} \underset {\zeta , \rho_ {i}} {\mathbb {E}} \langle \zeta , w _ {i} \rangle \mathbb {1} _ {\langle w _ {i}, x + \zeta \rangle + \rho_ {i} \geq b _ {i}} \\ \stackrel {1} {=} \frac {\| v _ {i} ^ {\parallel} \| _ {2}}{\| w _ {i} \| _ {2}} \underset {\zeta , \rho_ {i}} {\mathbb {E}} (\langle \zeta , w _ {i} \rangle + \rho_ {i}) \mathbb {1} _ {\langle w _ {i}, x + \zeta \rangle + \rho_ {i} \geq b _ {i}} \pm \| v _ {i} ^ {\parallel} \| _ {2} \cdot O (d ^ {- 1 / 4}) \\ \end{array}
$$

Above, the last ① is due to  $\sigma_{\rho_i} \leq d^{o(1)}$  and  $\|w_i\|_2 \geq \Omega(\sqrt{d})$ .

Next, we again treat  $g \stackrel{\mathrm{def}}{=} \langle \zeta, w_i \rangle + \rho_i \sim \mathcal{N}(0, \sigma^2)$  and apply Claim G.6. We have

$$
\underset {\rho_ {i}, \zeta} {\mathbb {E}} (\langle \zeta , w _ {i} \rangle + \rho_ {i}) \mathbb {1} _ {\langle w _ {i}, x + \zeta \rangle + \rho_ {i} \geq b _ {i}} = \underset {g} {\mathbb {E}} g \mathbb {1} _ {\langle w _ {i}, x \rangle - b _ {i} + g \geq 0} = \sigma \sum_ {r = 0} ^ {\infty} c _ {2 r} \left(\frac {\langle w _ {i} , x \rangle - b _ {i}}{\sigma}\right) ^ {2 r}
$$

for  $|c_{2r}| = \Theta \left(\frac{1}{r!}\right)$ . Putting them together, and doing the same thing for the symmetric part, we have

$$
\begin{array}{l} \diamondsuit = \underset {\rho_ {i}, \zeta} {\mathbb {E}} \langle \zeta , v _ {i} \rangle \left(\mathbb {1} _ {\langle w _ {i}, x + \zeta \rangle + \rho_ {i} \geq b _ {i}} - \mathbb {1} _ {- \langle w _ {i}, x + \zeta \rangle + \rho_ {i} \geq b _ {i}}\right) \\ = \frac {\left\| v _ {i} ^ {\parallel} \right\| _ {2} \sigma}{\left\| w _ {i} \right\| _ {2}} \sum_ {r = 0} ^ {\infty} \left(c _ {2 r} \left(\frac {\langle w _ {i} , x \rangle - b _ {i}}{\sigma}\right) ^ {2 r} - c _ {2 r} \left(\frac {- \langle w _ {i} , x \rangle - b _ {i}}{\sigma}\right) ^ {2 r}\right) \pm \left\| v _ {i} ^ {\parallel} \right\| _ {2} \cdot O \left(d ^ {- 1 / 4}\right) \\ \stackrel {①} {=} \frac {\| v _ {i} ^ {\parallel} \| _ {2} \sigma}{\| w _ {i} \| _ {2}} \sum_ {r = 1} ^ {\infty} c _ {r} ^ {\prime \prime \prime} \left(\frac {\langle w _ {i} , x \rangle}{\sigma}\right) ^ {r} \pm \| v _ {i} ^ {\parallel} \| _ {2} \cdot O (d ^ {- 1 / 4}) \\ = \| v _ {i} ^ {\parallel} \| _ {2} \left\langle \frac {w _ {i}}{\| w _ {i} \| _ {2}}, x \right\rangle \sum_ {r = 0} ^ {\infty} c _ {r + 1} ^ {\prime \prime \prime} \left(\frac {\langle w _ {i} , x \rangle}{\sigma}\right) ^ {r} \pm \| v _ {i} ^ {\parallel} \| _ {2} \cdot O (d ^ {- 1 / 4}) \\ \end{array}
$$

Above, using the property of  $b_{i} \ll d^{-0.2} \ll \sigma$ , equation ① holds for some  $|c_{2r}^{\prime \prime \prime}| \leq O\left(\frac{d^{-0.1}}{r!}\right)$  and  $|c_{2r + 1}^{\prime \prime \prime}| \leq O\left(\frac{d^{-0.1}}{(r + 1)!}\right)$ .

Finally, putting the bounds for  $\heartsuit$  and  $\diamondsuit$  together, and using  $\frac{\tau\|w_i\|_2}{\log^8d} \leq \sigma \leq \tau \| w_i\| _2$ , we derive that

$$
\left. \right.\left. \right.\left. \right.\left. \right.\left. \right.\left. \right.\left. \right.\left. \right.\left. \right.\left. \right.\left. \right.\left. \right.\left. \right.\left. \right.\left. \right.\left. \right.\left. \right.\left. \right.\left. \right.\left. \right.\left. \right.\left. \right.\left. \right.\left. \right.\left. \right.\left. \right.\left. \right.\left. \right.\left. \right.\left. \right.\left. \right.\left. \right.\left. \right.\left.\right.\left. \right.\left. \right.\left. \right.\left. \right.\left. \right.\left. \right.\left. \right.\left. \right.\left. \right.\left. \right.\left. \right.\left. \right.\left.\left.\left.\left.\left.\left.\left.\left.\left.\left.\left.\left.\left.\left.\left.\left.\left.\left.\left.\left.\left\langle v _ {i} \right\rangle_ {i} = 1 / 4}\right) ^ {\prime} = 1 / 4\right) ^ {\prime} = 1 / 4\right) ^ {\prime} = 1 / 4\right) ^ {\prime} = 1 / 4\right) ^ {\prime} = 1 / 4\right) ^ {\prime} = 1 / 4\right) ^ {\prime} = 1 / 4\right) ^ {\prime} = 1 / 4\right) ^ {\prime} = 0\right) ^ {\prime} = 0\right) ^ {\prime} = 0\right) ^ {\prime} = 0\right) ^ {\prime} = 0\right) ^ {\prime} = 0\right) ^ {\prime} = 0\right) ^ {\prime} = 0\right) ^ {\prime} = 0\right) ^ {\prime} = 0\right) ^ {\prime} = 1 / 4\right) ^ {\prime} = 1 / 4
$$
