for  $|c_{i,r}^{\prime \prime \prime \prime}| \leq O(1)$  for every  $r \geq 0$ ,  $c_r^{\prime \prime \prime \prime} \leq O(d^{-0.1}r) \cdot c_r^{\prime \prime \prime}$  for every  $r \geq 0$ , and  $|c_{i,r}^{\prime \prime \prime \prime}| \geq \Omega\left(\frac{1}{d^2}\right)$  for every odd constant  $r \geq 1$ . This finishes the proof of Claim G.3.

# G.5 Simple Lemmas

We have the following claim relating polynomial value with its coefficients:

Claim G.7 (low degree polynomial). Let  $p: \mathbb{R} \to \mathbb{R}$  be a constant-degree polynomial  $p(x) = \sum_{r=0}^{R} c_r x^r$ , then there exists  $x \in [1/2, 1]$  such that

$$
| p (x) | \geq \Omega \left(\max  _ {r = 0, 1, 2, \dots , R} | c _ {r} |\right).
$$

Proof of Claim G.7. Let us define  $q(x) \stackrel{\mathrm{def}}{=} p\left(x + \frac{1}{2}\right)$  and write accordingly  $q(x) = \sum_{r=0}^{R} c_r' x^r$ . Using the identity formula  $\sum_{r=0}^{R} c_r' x^r = \sum_{r=0}^{R} c_r (x + 0.5)^r$  we can derive (recalling  $R$  is constant)

$$
\max  _ {r = 0, 1, 2, \dots , R} | c _ {r} ^ {\prime} | \leq O \left(\max  _ {r = 0, 1, 2, \dots , R} | c _ {r} |\right)
$$

Conversely, by writing  $\sum_{r=0}^{R} c_r'(x - 0.5)^r = \sum_{r=0}^{R} c_r x^r$ , we also have the other direction and therefore

$$
\max  _ {r = 0, 1, 2, \dots , R} | c _ {r} ^ {\prime} | = \Theta \left(\max  _ {r = 0, 1, 2, \dots , R} | c _ {r} |\right)
$$

Now, notice that  $\left|\frac{d^r}{dx^r} q(x)\right|_{x=0} = \Theta(|c_r'|)$ , so we can apply Markov brother's inequality to derive that

$$
\max  _ {x \in [ 0, 1 / 2 ]} | q (x) | \geq \Omega \left(\max  _ {r = 0, 1, 2, \dots , R} | c _ {r} ^ {\prime} |\right).
$$

This finishes the proof.

Using this Claim, we also have the following claim about symmetric tensor:

Claim G.8 (symmetric tensor norms). For every constant  $r > 0$ , every fixed  $a_1, a_2 > 0$ , every symmetric tensor  $T$  of degree  $r$  of the form  $T \colon \mathbb{R}^{d \times r} \to \mathbb{R}$ , let

$$
\lambda_ {1} := \max  _ {x: \| x \| _ {\infty} \leq a _ {1}, M _ {W} (x) \leq a _ {2}} \left\{| T (x ^ {\otimes r}) | \right\}, \quad \lambda_ {2} := \max  _ {\{x _ {i} \} _ {i \in [ r ]}: \| x _ {i} \| _ {\infty} \leq a _ {1}, M _ {W} (x _ {i}) \leq a _ {2}} \left\{| T (x _ {1}, x _ {2}, \dots , x _ {r}) | \right\}
$$

then we have:

$$
\lambda_ {1} \leq \lambda_ {2} \leq O (\lambda_ {1})
$$

Proof of Claim G.8.  $\lambda_1\leq \lambda_2$  is obvious so let us prove the other direction. Define polynomial

$$
p (s) = T \left(\left(x _ {1} + s ^ {r + 1} x _ {2} + s ^ {(r + 1) ^ {2}} x _ {3} + \dots + s ^ {(r + 1) ^ {r - 1}} x _ {r}\right) ^ {\otimes r}\right)
$$

The coefficient of  $p(s)$  at degree  $\sum_{r' \in [r]} (r + 1)^{r' - 1}$  is  $\Theta(T(x_1, x_2, \dots, x_r))$ . Thus, applying Claim G.7 and appropriately scaling the operator, we complete the proof.

# H Appendix for Probability Theory

# H.1 Small ball probability: The basic property

We also have the following property:

Lemma H.1 (small ball probability, 1-d case).

(a) For every subset  $\Lambda \subseteq [d]$ , every  $r$ , and every  $t > 0$

$$
\mathbf {P r} \left[ | \sum_ {j \in \Lambda} w _ {j} ^ {\star} \cdot z _ {j} - r | \leq t \right] \leq O (\frac {t}{\sqrt {| \Lambda | / d}} + \frac {1}{\sqrt {| \Lambda | k / d}})
$$

(b) For every subset  $\Lambda \subseteq [d]$  with  $|\Lambda| \geq \Omega(d)$ , and every  $t > 0$ ,

$$
\Pr \left[ \left| \sum_ {j \in \Lambda} w _ {j} ^ {\star} \cdot z _ {j} \right| \leq t \right] \geq \Omega (t) - O \left(\frac {\log k}{\sqrt {k}}\right)
$$

Proof of Lemma H.1. Recall we have  $\mathbf{Pr}_{z_j}[z_j\neq 0]\geq \Omega (\frac{k}{d})$  for each  $j\in \Lambda$ . Let  $\Lambda^{\prime}\subseteq \Lambda$  be the subset of such indices  $j$  with non-zero  $z_{j}$ , so by our assumption we have  $|z_{j}|\geq \frac{1}{\sqrt{k}}$  for each  $j\in \Lambda^{\prime}$ . By Chernoff bound, with probability at least  $1 - e^{-\Omega (|\Lambda |k / d)}$ , we know  $|\Lambda^{\prime}|\geq \Omega (\frac{k}{d})\cdot |\Lambda|$

Conditioning on such  $\Lambda^{\prime}$ , by the Littlewood-Offord problem (a.k.a. small ball probability theorem, or anti-concentration for sum of Bernoulli variables, see [30]), we know

$$
\mathbf {P r} \left[ \left| \sum_ {j \in \Lambda} w _ {j} ^ {\star} \cdot z _ {j} - r \right| \leq t \mid \mathcal {E} \right] \leq O (\frac {\sqrt {k} t + 1}{\sqrt {| \Lambda^ {\prime} |}}) = O (\frac {\sqrt {k} t + 1}{\sqrt {| \Lambda | k / d}})
$$

As for the lower bound, let us denote by  $\Lambda'' \subseteq \Lambda$  be the subset of such indices  $j$  with non-zero  $z_j$  and  $|z_j| \leq O\left(\frac{1}{\sqrt{k}}\right)$ . We know with high probability  $|\Lambda''| \geq \Omega(k)$ . Using  $\mathbb{E}\left[\left|\sum_{j \in \Lambda \setminus \Lambda''} w_j^{\star} \cdot z_j\right|\right] \leq O(1)$ , we can apply Markov's inequality and get

$$
\Pr \left[ \left| \sum_ {j \in \Lambda \backslash \Lambda^ {\prime \prime}} w _ {j} ^ {\star} \cdot z _ {j} \right| \leq B \right] \geq 0. 6 \quad \text {f o r s o m e c o n s t a n t} B = O (1)
$$

Now, for the sum over  $\Lambda''$ , we can apply a Wasserstein distance version of the central limit theorem (that can be derived from [111], full statement see [6, Appendix A.2]) to derive that, for a Gaussian variable  $g \sim (0, V^2)$  where  $V = \sum_{j \in \Lambda''} (w_j^\star)^2 \mathbb{E}[(z_j)^2] \geq \Omega(1)$ , the Wasserstein distance:

$$
\mathcal {W} _ {2} \left(\sum_ {j \in \Lambda^ {\prime \prime}} w _ {j} ^ {\star} \cdot z _ {j}, g\right) \leq O \left(\frac {\log k}{\sqrt {k}}\right)
$$

Using the property of Gaussian variables and  $B = O(1)$ , we have

$$
\mathbf {P r} \left[ g \in \left[ \sum_ {j \in \Lambda \backslash \Lambda^ {\prime \prime}} w _ {j} ^ {\star} \cdot z _ {j} - \frac {t}{2}, \sum_ {j \in \Lambda \backslash \Lambda^ {\prime \prime}} w _ {j} ^ {\star} \cdot z _ {j} + \frac {t}{2} \right] \right] \geq \Omega (t)
$$

and using the above Wasserstein distance bound, we have

$$
\mathbf {P r} \left[ \sum_ {j \in \Lambda^ {\prime \prime}} w _ {j} ^ {\star} \cdot z _ {j} \in \left[ \sum_ {j \in \Lambda \backslash \Lambda^ {\prime \prime}} w _ {j} ^ {\star} \cdot z _ {j} - t, \sum_ {j \in \Lambda \backslash \Lambda^ {\prime \prime}} w _ {j} ^ {\star} \cdot z _ {j} + t \right] \right] \geq \Omega (t) - O \left(\frac {\log k}{\sqrt {k}}\right)
$$

![](images/04f8887e2c9cd240e2a8a4a79c6c186b09cf1714f7b7c54262736a885762fd9f.jpg)

# H.2 McDiarmid's Inequality and An Extension

We state the standard McDiarmid's inequality,

Lemma H.2 (McDiarmid's inequality). Consider independent random variables  $x_{1},\dots ,x_{n}\in \mathcal{X}$  and a mapping  $f:\mathcal{X}^n\to \mathsf{R}$ . If for all  $i\in [n]$  and for all  $y_{1},\dots ,y_{n},y_{i}^{\prime}\in \mathcal{X}$ , the function  $f$  satisfies

$$
\left| f \left(y _ {1}, \dots , y _ {i - 1}, y _ {i}, y _ {i + 1}, \dots , y _ {n}\right) - f \left(y _ {1}, \dots , y _ {i - 1}, y _ {i} ^ {\prime}, y _ {i + 1}, \dots , y _ {n}\right) \right| \leq c _ {i}.
$$

Then

$$
\mathbf {P r} [ f (x _ {1}, \dots , x _ {n}) - \mathbb {E} f \geq t ] \geq \exp (\frac {- 2 t ^ {2}}{\sum_ {i = 1} ^ {n} c _ {i} ^ {2}}),
$$

$$
\mathbf {P r} [ f (x _ {1}, \dots , x _ {n}) - \mathbb {E} f \leq - t ] \geq \exp (\frac {2 t ^ {2}}{\sum_ {i = 1} ^ {n} c _ {i} ^ {2}}).
$$

We prove a more general version of McDiarmid's inequality,

Lemma H.3 (McDiarmid extension). Let  $w_{1}, \ldots, w_{N}$  be independent random variables and  $f\colon (w_{1}, \ldots, w_{N}) \mapsto [0, B]$ . Suppose it satisfies for every  $k \in \{2, 3, \ldots, N\}$ ,

with probability at least  $1 - p$  over  $w_{1},\ldots ,w_{N}$  , it satisfies

$$
\forall w _ {k} ^ {\prime \prime}: | f (w _ {- k}, w _ {k}) - f (w _ {- k}, w _ {k} ^ {\prime \prime}) | \leq c
$$

with probability at least  $1 - p$  over  $w_{1},\ldots ,w_{k - 1},w_{k + 1},\ldots ,w_{N}$  , it satisfies

$$
\underset {w _ {k} ^ {\prime}, w _ {k} ^ {\prime \prime}} {\mathbb {E}} \left[ (f (w _ {- k}, w _ {k} ^ {\prime}) - f (w _ {- k}, w _ {k} ^ {\prime \prime})) ^ {2} \right] \leq V _ {k} ^ {2}
$$

Then,

$$
\begin{array}{l} \mathbf {P r} _ {w _ {1}, \dots , w _ {N}} \left[ \left| f (w _ {1}, \dots , w _ {N}) - \underset {w _ {2}, \dots , w _ {N}} {\mathbb {E}} [ f (w _ {1}, \dots , w _ {N}) \mid w _ {1} ] \right| \geq t \right] \\ \leq O (N \sqrt {p}) + \exp \left(\frac {- \Omega (t ^ {2})}{t (c + \sqrt {p} B) + \sum_ {t = 2} ^ {N} (V _ {t} ^ {2} + \sqrt {p} B ^ {2}) ^ {2}}\right). \\ \end{array}
$$

Proof of Lemma H.3. For each  $t = 1, \ldots, N - 1$ , we have with probability at least  $1 - \sqrt{p}$  over  $w_1, \ldots, w_t$ , it satisfies

$$
\mathbf {P r} _ {w _ {t + 1}, \ldots , w _ {N}} \left[ \forall w _ {t + 1} ^ {\prime \prime}: \left| f (w _ {\leq t}, w _ {t + 1}, w _ {> t + 1}) - f (w _ {\leq t}, w _ {t + 1} ^ {\prime \prime}, w _ {> t + 1}) \right| \leq c \right] \geq 1 - \sqrt {p}.
$$

We also have with probability at least  $1 - \sqrt{p}$  over  $w_{1},\ldots ,w_{t}$ , it satisfies

$$
\mathbf {P r} _ {w _ {t + 2}, \dots , w _ {N}} \left[ \mathbb {E} _ {w _ {t + 1} ^ {\prime}, w _ {t + 1} ^ {\prime \prime}} \left(f (w _ {\leq t}, w _ {t + 1} ^ {\prime}, w _ {> t + 1}) - f (w _ {\leq t}, w _ {t + 1} ^ {\prime \prime}, w _ {> t + 1})\right) ^ {2} \leq V _ {t + 1} ^ {2} \right] \geq 1 - \sqrt {p} .
$$

We denote by  $K_{\leq t}$  the event (over  $w_{\leq t} = (w_1, \ldots, w_t)$ ) that the above two statements hold. We know that  $\mathbf{Pr}[w_{\leq t} \in K_{\leq t}] \geq 1 - 2\sqrt{p}$ . For notational simplicity, we denote by  $K_{\leq N}$  the full set over all possible  $(w_1, \ldots, w_N)$ .

Define random variable  $X_{t}$  (which depends only on  $w_{1},\ldots ,w_{t}$ ) as

$$
X _ {t} := \underset {w > t} {\mathbb {E}} \left[ f (\vec {w}) \mid w _ {\leq t} \right] \mathbb {1} _ {(w _ {\leq 1}, \dots , w _ {\leq t}) \in K _ {\leq 1} \times \dots \times K _ {\leq t}} \in [ 0, B ]
$$

For every  $t$  and fixed  $w_{1},\ldots ,w_{t - 1}$

- If  $(w_{\leq 1},\ldots ,w_{< t})\notin K_{\leq 1}\times \dots \times K_{< t}$ , then  $X_{t} = X_{t - 1} = 0$

$$
\begin{array}{l} \bullet \text {I f} (w _ {\leq 1}, \dots , w _ {<   t}) \in K _ {\leq 1} \times \dots \times K _ {<   t}, \\ - \text {I f} w _ {\leq t} \notin K _ {\leq t}, \text {t h e n} X _ {t} - X _ {t - 1} = 0 - X _ {t - 1} \leq 0. \\ - \text {I f} w _ {\leq t} \in K _ {\leq t}, \text {t h e n} \\ \end{array}
$$

$$
X _ {t} - X _ {t - 1} = \underset {w > t} {\mathbb {E}} \left[ f \left(w _ {<   t}, w _ {t}, w _ {> t}\right) \mid w _ {\leq t} \right] - \underset {w \geq t} {\mathbb {E}} \left[ f \left(w _ {<   t}, w _ {t}, w _ {> t}\right) \mid w _ {<   t} \right]
$$

Recall the property  $w_{<t} \in K_{<t}$ , we know with probability at least  $1 - \sqrt{p}$  over  $w_t$  and  $w_{>t}$ , it satisfies

$$
\forall w _ {t} ^ {\prime \prime}: | f (w _ {<   t}, w _ {t} ^ {\prime \prime}, w _ {> t}) - f (w _ {<   t}, w _ {t}, w _ {> t}) | \leq c
$$

Taking expectation over  $w_{t}$  and  $w_{>t}$ , we have

$$
\forall w _ {t} ^ {\prime \prime}: \underset {w > t} {\mathbb {E}} \left[ f \left(w _ {<   t}, w _ {t} ^ {\prime \prime}, w _ {> t}\right) \right] - \underset {w _ {\geq t}} {\mathbb {E}} \left[ f \left(w _ {<   t}, w _ {t}, w _ {> t}\right) \right] \leq (c + \sqrt {p} B)
$$

This precisely means  $X_{t} - X_{t - 1}\leq c + \sqrt{p} B$

- Using the property  $w_{<t} \in K_{<t}$ , we know with probability at least  $1 - \sqrt{p}$  over  $w_{>t}$ , it satisfies

$$
\underset {w _ {t}, w _ {t} ^ {\prime \prime}} {\mathbb {E}} \left(f (w _ {<   t}, w _ {t}, w _ {> t}) - f (w _ {<   t}, w _ {t} ^ {\prime \prime}, w _ {> t})\right) ^ {2} \leq V _ {t} ^ {2}
$$

Taking expectation also over  $w_{>t}$ , we have

$$
\begin{array}{l} \operatorname * {\mathbb {E}} _ {w _ {t}, w _ {t} ^ {\prime \prime}, w _ {> t}} \left(f (w _ {<   t}, w _ {t}, w _ {> t}) - f (w _ {<   t}, w _ {t} ^ {\prime \prime}, w _ {> t})\right) ^ {2} \leq V _ {t} ^ {2} + \sqrt {p} B ^ {2} \\ \Longrightarrow \underset {w _ {t}} {\mathbb {E}} \left(\underset {w > t} {\mathbb {E}} [ f (w _ {<   t}, w _ {t}, w _ {> t}) ] - \underset {w _ {t} ^ {\prime \prime}, w _ {> t}} {\mathbb {E}} [ f (w _ {<   t}, w _ {t} ^ {\prime \prime}, w _ {> t}) ]\right) ^ {2} \leq V _ {t} ^ {2} + \sqrt {p} B ^ {2} \\ \end{array}
$$

Now observe that, since  $(w_{\leq 1},\ldots ,w_{< t})\in K_{\leq 1}\times \dots \times K_{< t}$ , we have  $X_{t - 1} = \mathbb{E}_{w_t^{\prime \prime},w > t}[f(w_{< t},w_t^{\prime \prime},w_{>t})]$ . We also have that as long as  $w_{\leq t}\in K_{\leq t}$ , then  $X_{t} = \mathbb{E}_{w_{>t}}[f(w_{< t},w_{t},w_{>t})]$ . Putting these together, and using the fact  $\mathbf{Pr}[w_{\leq t}\in K_{\leq t}]\geq 1 - 2\sqrt{p}$ , we have

$$
\underset {w _ {k}} {\mathbb {E}} \left[ (X _ {t + 1} - X _ {t}) ^ {2} \mid w _ {<   t} \right] \leq V _ {t} ^ {2} + 3 \sqrt {p} B ^ {2}
$$

In sum, we have just shown that for all choices of  $w_{1},\ldots ,w_{t - 1}$

$$
X _ {t} - X _ {t - 1} \leq (c + \sqrt {p} B) \quad \text {a n d} \quad \mathbb {E} _ {w _ {k}} \left[ \left(X _ {t + 1} - X _ {t}\right) ^ {2} \mid w _ {<   t} \right] \leq V _ {t} ^ {2} + 3 \sqrt {p} B ^ {2}
$$

always holds. Note in addition we also have  $\mathbb{E}_{w_t}[X_t|w_{< t}]\leq X_{t - 1}$ . Therefore, by applying martingale concentration (with its one-sided and Bernstein form, see Lemma H.4),

$$
\mathbf {P r} [ X _ {N} - X _ {1} > t ] \leq \exp \left(\frac {- \Omega (t ^ {2})}{t (c + \sqrt {p} B) + \sum_ {t = 2} ^ {N} (V _ {t} ^ {2} + \sqrt {p} B ^ {2}) ^ {2}}\right)
$$

Recalling

$$
X _ {N} := f (\vec {w}) \mathbb {1} _ {(w _ {\leq 1}, \dots , w _ {\leq t}) \in K _ {\leq 1} \times \dots \times K _ {\leq t}}
$$

and we have  $X_{N} = f(w_{1},\ldots ,w_{N})$  with probability at least  $1 - 2N\sqrt{p}$  (and  $X_{N} = 0$  with the remaining probability). Also recalling

$$
X _ {1} := \underset {w _ {2}, \ldots , w _ {N}} {\mathbb {E}} \left[ f (\vec {w}) \mid w _ {1} \right] \mathbb {1} _ {w _ {\leq 1}}
$$

and we have  $X_{1} = \mathbb{E}_{w_{2},\ldots ,w_{N}}\left[f(\vec{w})\mid w_{1}\right]$  with probability at least  $1 - 2\sqrt{p}$  (and  $X_{1} = 0$  with the remaining probability).

Together, we have the desired theorem.

Let us state, for completeness' sake, a simple one-sided Bernstein form of martingale concentration (that we do not know a good reference to it).

Lemma H.4. Suppose we have a submartingale sequence  $X_0, X_1, \ldots, X_N$ , satisfying:

-  $X_0 = 0$  and  $\mathbb{E}[X_t\mid X_{t - 1}]\leq X_t$  
-  $X_{t} - X_{t - 1}\leq c$  always holds, and  
-  $\mathbb{E}_{X_t}[(X_t - X_{t - 1})^2\mid X_{t - 1}]\leq V_t^2$  always holds.

Then,

$$
\mathbf {P r} [ X _ {N} > t ] \leq e ^ {- \Omega (\frac {t ^ {2}}{t c + \sum_ {t} V _ {t} ^ {2}})}
$$

Proof. Define potential function  $\Psi_t = e^{\frac{\eta}{2c} X_t}$  for some  $\eta \in (0,1)$  to be chosen later. We have

$$
\Psi_ {t} = \Psi_ {t - 1} \cdot e ^ {\frac {\eta}{2 c} (X _ {t} - X _ {t - 1})} \leq \Psi_ {t - 1} \cdot \left(1 + \left(\frac {\eta (X _ {t} - X _ {t - 1})}{2 c}\right) + \left(\frac {\eta (X _ {t} - X _ {t - 1})}{2 c}\right) ^ {2}\right)
$$

where the inequality is due to  $e^y \leq 1 + y + y^2$  which holds for all  $-\infty < y \leq 0.5$ . Taking conditional expectation, we have

$$
\begin{array}{l} \mathbb {E} \left[ \Psi_ {t} \mid X _ {t - 1} \right] \leq \Psi_ {t - 1} \cdot \left(1 + \eta \mathbb {E} \left[ \frac {X _ {t} - X _ {t - 1}}{2 c} \mid X _ {t - 1} \right] + \eta^ {2} \mathbb {E} \left[ \left(\frac {X _ {t} - X _ {t - 1}}{2 c}\right) ^ {2} \mid X _ {t - 1} \right]\right) \\ \le \Psi_ {t - 1} \cdot \left(1 + \eta^ {2} \frac {V _ {t} ^ {2}}{4 c ^ {2}}\right) \le \Psi_ {t - 1} \cdot e ^ {\eta^ {2} \frac {V _ {t} ^ {2}}{4 c ^ {2}}} . \\ \end{array}
$$

After telescoping, we have  $\mathbb{E}[\Psi_N] \leq e^{\eta^2 \frac{\sum_t V_t^2}{4c^2}}$ , and therefore

$$
\mathbf {P r} [ X _ {N} > t ] \leq \frac {\mathbb {E} [ e ^ {\eta X _ {N} / (2 c)} ]}{e ^ {\eta t / (2 c)}} \leq e ^ {\eta^ {2} \frac {\sum_ {t} V _ {t} ^ {2}}{4 c ^ {2}} - \eta \frac {t}{2 c}}
$$

Choosing the optimal  $\eta \in (0,1)$  gives us bound

$$
\mathbf {P r} [ X _ {N} > t ] \leq e ^ {- \Omega (\frac {t ^ {2}}{t c + \sum_ {t} V _ {t} ^ {2}})}
$$
